## What's Done (iter 260+)

- **React error #310 fix (Rules of Hooks)** -- `MessageBubbleInner` had a `useMemo` (rendered markdown cache) declared AFTER the early returns for `dispatch`/`thinking`/`tool` roles. A component instance whose message role changed mid-life would render a different number of hooks and React would throw `Minified React error #310: Rendered more hooks than during the previous render`. The bug was latent (each message has a stable role for its lifetime) until the very first production build was run. Moved the `useMemo` to the top of the component, before any early return, with a comment documenting WHY it must stay there. Same fix-pattern recommendation applies to any future hooks added below early-return paths.
- **Cross-session search (Ctrl+Shift+F)** -- new substantial feature: a modal that searches every chat session in the active project. Backend: `Studio.SearchProjectHistory(projectID, query)` returns `[]SearchHit{SessionID, SessionName, MessageIdx, Role, Snippet, MatchOffset}`. Case-insensitive substring search; per-session cap of 5 hits and global cap of 200 so a noisy match can't drown the UI; thinking parts excluded so internal model deliberation isn't searchable; ~120-char snippet centered on the first match with leading/trailing ellipses. `MatchOffset` is byte-indexed and accounts for the prepended ellipsis (`len("…") == 3` in UTF-8) so the frontend can correctly highlight the matched substring. `MessageIdx` is the index in the filtered history (matching `GetHistory`'s output) so the frontend can scroll to the right message after switching sessions. Frontend: full modal in `ChatPanel.tsx` with debounced (200 ms) search, autofocus input, loading/empty/error states, `<mark>` highlighted matches in snippets, click-to-jump that switches sessions and flashes the matched message via a `.msg-flash` keyframe animation. Wired into Cmd palette ("Search across all sessions", group: Actions, hint: Ctrl+Shift+F) and Settings keyboard-shortcuts grid. Added unique `.global-search-backdrop` class so window-level Escape handler can close the modal explicitly (deferring to `dispatch-backdrop`'s bail-out wouldn't close it from non-input focus). Tests (8): `TestSearchProjectHistory_*` covering unknown-project error, empty/whitespace queries returning no hits, finding matches across sessions with map-iteration-randomness-tolerant assertions, case-insensitive matching, per-session cap, thinking-part exclusion, snippet trimming with correct UTF-8 byte offsets, and `MessageIdx` alignment with `GetHistory`. Studio test suite: **254 tests**, coverage: **92.7%**.

## What's Done (iter 270+)

- **Persistent drafts** -- new substantial feature: an in-progress message that the user typed but hasn't sent now survives an app crash, OS reboot, or accidental Cmd+Q. Previously drafts lived only in `chatStore` (in-memory), so any restart wiped them. Backend (new file `internal/studio/drafts.go`): `SaveDraft(projectID, sessionID, text)`, `GetDraft(projectID, sessionID) string`, `ClearDraft(projectID, sessionID)` Wails methods. Storage: one plain UTF-8 file per `(project, session)` under `<configDir>/drafts/<projectID>_<sessionID>.txt`. Why a flat file (not embedded in session JSON): saves are very frequent (every keystroke after debounce) and rewriting the full session history on each save would thrash a much larger file. Sanitisation: `sanitiseDraftKey` replaces `/`, `\`, `.`, `:`, and control chars with `_` so future ID schemes can't escape `draftsDir()` via path traversal. Defensive 200 KB cap (`DraftMaxBytes`) backstops the frontend's 100 K-char textarea limit. Empty / whitespace-only text removes the file rather than writing it. Lifecycle wired: `RemoveProject` → `removeProjectDrafts(id)` (sweeps all sessions for that project); `DeleteChatSession` → `ClearDraft`; `ClearHistory` → `ClearDraft`. Send-clears-draft cascades through the existing frontend `setInput('')` → SaveDraft useEffect → empty text → file removal. Frontend (`ChatPanel.tsx`): debounced (400 ms) `SaveDraft` on every input change so typing isn't blocked by disk I/O; on session switch when the in-memory draft is empty, fires `GetDraft` to hydrate from disk with a "user has started typing in the interim" guard against the slow-disk-clobbers-fresh-keystroke race. Tests (15): round-trip; empty/whitespace clears file; truncation at MaxBytes; missing-file returns empty without error; missing-file ClearDraft is not error; empty projectID is no-op for Save/Get/Clear; empty sessionID defaults to "default" symmetrically across all three; sanitisation rejects `../`, `\\`, `:`, control chars, dot-prefixes, empty input; `removeProjectDrafts` clears only the target project's drafts (project B's draft survives); end-to-end `RemoveProject` / `DeleteChatSession` / `ClearHistory` clear drafts. Studio test suite: **267 tests**, coverage: **92.7%**.

## What's Done (iter 280+)

- **Conversation forking ("Branch from here")** -- new substantial feature: right-click any user message → "Branch from here" creates a brand-new session pre-populated with the conversation up to and including that message. Solves a real pain point: previously the user's options were "keep typing in the same session" (loses the original continuation) or "/clear" (loses everything). Now they can branch a thread to try a different approach without touching the original. Backend: `Studio.ForkChatSession(projectID, sessionID, userIndexFromEnd, newName) -> *ChatSessionInfo`. Index semantics match `EditUserMessage` (0 = most recent user turn). The fork includes the chosen user turn so the new session is ready for a fresh model response. **Deep-copies** `*genai.Content` and each `*genai.Part` — sharing pointers between sessions would couple them on subsequent saves. Auto-name pattern: `<source name> (branch)`, capped at 60 chars; custom name overrides. Persisted to disk immediately via `SaveHistoryWithName` so a branch survives restart even before the user sends anything in it. Skips tool-only "user" turns (FunctionResponse-only) when counting `userIndexFromEnd`, matching `EditUserMessage` semantics — the index always points to a real human message. Frontend: new "Branch from here" item in the message context menu (uses `GitFork` lucide icon) shown on user messages whenever `userIdx >= 0`. After fork: `setActiveSession` + dispatches `gokin:switch-tab` with the new session ID + new `gokin:sessions-changed` event so App.tsx reloads the tab list and the branch appears immediately. Forking is allowed even while the agent is running in the source session (the fork is a snapshot, not a mutation). Tests (13): unknown project / session / negative index / out-of-range index; `IncludesChosenTurn` (idx=0 keeps the most recent user turn as the last turn); `TrimsAfterChosenTurn` (idx=1 drops the third user turn + reply that came after); `DeepCopiesHistory` (mutating source's `Part.Text` doesn't bleed into fork and vice versa); auto-name with branch suffix; custom name override; long name truncation; persists immediately and survives reload via `GetHistory`; empty session ID defaults to "default"; tool-only "user" turns skipped when matching index. `ForkChatSession` coverage: **95.7%**. Studio test suite: **283 tests**, coverage: **92.8%**.

## What's Done (iter 290+)

- **Token cost estimator** -- new substantial feature: every completed assistant turn now shows an approximate USD cost (e.g. `≈$0.0023`) computed from the per-token rates of the active model. A running session-total chip in the chat header aggregates every turn so users can glance at their spend at any time. Backend (new file `internal/studio/pricing.go`): `ModelPricing{InputPerMTok, OutputPerMTok, CacheReadPerMTok, CacheWritePerMTok}` per model + `LookupPricing(provider, model)` (longest-prefix-wins case-insensitive) + `EstimateCost(provider, model, inTok, outTok, cacheRead, cacheWrite) float64` package fn + `(*Studio).EstimateCost(...)` Wails method for ad-hoc UI queries. Pricing data covers GLM (5.1 / 5 / 5-turbo / 4.7 / 4.6 / 4.5 / 4.5-air / 4 — flagship $0.55-in/$2.19-out, air tier $0.20/$1.10), MiniMax (abab series — $0.30-1.00-in/$0.60-2.00-out), Kimi (kimi-for-coding $0.60-in/$2.50-out with cache pricing); Ollama returns zero (local). Server-side computation: `project.go` adds `EstimatedCostUSD` to the `chat:complete` event payload so the frontend doesn't duplicate the pricing table. Frontend: new green `≈$X.YYZZ` chip rendered in the message footer (`.msg-cost` CSS) next to the existing token-count chip, with a help-cursor tooltip explaining the figure is approximate (not authoritative billing — provider rounding, credits, and tier discounts not applied). Chat header gets a permanent `chat-header-cost` chip showing the running session total when > $0. `formatCostUSD()` helper renders `<$0.01` for sub-cent costs, 4-decimal trimmed for sub-dollar (e.g. `$0.0023`), 2-decimal for $1+. Tests (14): Ollama returns zero; unknown model returns zero; exact match retrieval; longest-prefix wins (`glm-5.1-experimental` → glm-5.1, not glm-5 or glm); case-insensitive lookup; empty model returns zero; per-million arithmetic verified for GLM-5.1, GLM-4.5-air, Kimi; cache-read and cache-write summed correctly; cache fields ignored for models without cache pricing (MiniMax); zero-token call returns zero; Wails binding passthrough matches package fn. Both `LookupPricing` and `EstimateCost` (package + Studio binding) at **100%**. Studio test suite: **299 tests**, coverage: **92.9%**.

## What's Done (iter 300+)

- **Message pinning ("Pin message")** -- new substantial feature: right-click any user or assistant message → "Pin message" bookmarks it for the session. A bookmark icon in the chat header (visible only when ≥1 pin exists) opens a modal listing every pin with role/timestamp/content preview, an "Open" button that scrolls to and flashes the matching live message, and a Trash button to remove the pin. Solves the "where did the agent figure out X?" / "I want to remember this snippet from earlier" pain on long sessions. Backend (new file `internal/studio/pins.go`): `PinMessage(projectID, sessionID, role, content, messageID) -> string` (returns pin ID), `UnpinMessage(projectID, sessionID, pinID)`, `ListPinnedMessages(projectID, sessionID) -> []PinnedMessage`. **Snapshot-based design** rather than ID-based: pins store the full content because frontend message IDs are regenerated every `GetHistory` call and history indices shift on edit/trim — a snapshot stays useful even if the source message is deleted. Storage: one JSON file per `(project, session)` at `<configDir>/pins/<projectID>_<sessionID>.json` (same flat-file pattern as drafts). Defensive 200 KB cap on content. Auto-dedup of identical (role, content) pairs returns the existing pin ID instead of creating a duplicate. Sorted newest-first on list. Lifecycle wired: `RemoveProject` → `removeProjectPins(id)` (sweeps every session); `DeleteChatSession` → `removeSessionPins`. Empty list removes the file rather than persisting `[]`. Frontend (`ChatPanel.tsx`): new `pinnedKeys: Set<string>` populated on session switch via `ListPinnedMessages` so the context menu can flip "Pin"↔"Unpin" without per-render RPC. Pin modal uses unique `.pins-backdrop` class for explicit Escape handling. "Open" jump uses exact (role, content) match against the live message list; if the source has been deleted/edited, shows a warning banner and keeps the snapshot in the modal. New BookmarkPlus/BookmarkMinus/Bookmark icons. Pinning works on user AND assistant messages, but not on tool/dispatch/thinking (those aren't really "messages"). Pinning is allowed even while the agent is running — it's a snapshot, not a mutation. Tests (24): backend round-trip; reject empty/whitespace content; reject bad role (`""`, `tool`, `model`, `system`, `USER`); reject empty projectID; unknown project errors; truncates at MaxBytes; dedup of identical (role,content); same content with different role NOT deduped; unpin removes; unpin unknown ID is idempotent; last unpin removes the file (not left as `[]`); list sorted newest-first; list returns empty for unknown project; list returns empty for missing file; empty session ID defaults to "default"; `removeProjectPins` clears all sessions of target project but NOT other projects; end-to-end `RemoveProject`/`DeleteChatSession` clear pins; corrupt JSON file returns error from PinMessage / UnpinMessage / ListPinnedMessages; empty-file early-return covered; `removeSessionPins` empty-pid no-op + empty-sid defaults to "default". `pins.go` coverage: `pinsDir`/`pinsPath`/`ListPinnedMessages`/`removeSessionPins` at **100%**, `PinMessage` 96.4%, `loadPinsFile` 90.9%, `UnpinMessage` 85.7%, `removeProjectPins` 83.3%, `savePinsFile` 70.0%. Studio test suite: **324 tests**, coverage: **92.9%**.

## What's Done (iter 310+)

- **Fork lineage tracking** -- extends iter 280's "Branch from here" feature with persistent parent-child relationships between sessions. Forked sessions now show a `GitFork` icon (instead of the default `MessageSquare`) in the tab bar, and hovering reveals "Forked from: <source name>". Solves the "wait, where did this session come from?" problem when users have a few branches open weeks later. Backend: extended `historyFile` schema with optional `parentSessionID` field — old v2 files without it unmarshal cleanly with empty string (no schema bump). New `SaveHistoryWithMetadata(projectID, name, parentID, history)` is the explicit-metadata variant; `SaveHistoryWithName` now reads the existing file before writing so the parent ID is **preserved across every turn-finished save** without callers needing to thread it through every layer. New `LoadHistoryParent` parallel to `LoadHistoryName`. `loadHistoryRaw` signature extended from 3 to 4 returns. `ChatSession` gains a `ParentID string` field; `NewProject`'s session-load loop populates it via `LoadHistoryParent` so lineage survives restart. `ChatSessionInfo` gains `ParentID` + `ParentName`; `ListChatSessions` builds an `id→name` index of all sessions and fills in `ParentName` (or `"(deleted)"` when the source is gone — better than silently dropping the indicator). `ForkChatSession` now sets `ParentID` on the new ChatSession AND uses `SaveHistoryWithMetadata` to stamp the parent on the first persisted write. Frontend: `SessionTab` interface gains `parentName?: string`; the tab map renders `GitFork` icon + `is-fork` class for any session with a non-empty `parentName`, with the parent name in the tooltip. CSS: `.view-tab.is-fork .tab-fork-icon { color: var(--accent); opacity: 0.85; }`. Tests (9 new in `lineage_test.go`): `ForkChatSession` stores ParentID in memory and persists it to disk; `NewProject` restores ParentID from disk on cold start (forked + non-forked sessions); `ListChatSessions` fills `ParentName` from sibling lookup AND falls back to `"(deleted)"` after the parent is removed; non-forked sessions have empty `ParentID`/`ParentName`; `SaveHistoryWithName` preserves a previously-stamped parent ID across subsequent writes (the read-then-write contract); `SaveHistoryWithMetadata` skips the write when all metadata fields are empty; legacy bare-array history files report no parent (forking didn't exist yet). Studio test suite: **333 tests**, coverage: **92.9%**.

## What's Done (iter 320+)

- **System prompt template library** -- new substantial feature: a curated picker of ready-made system prompts inside the per-project system-prompt editor. New users no longer face an empty textarea; experienced users can switch personas (Code Reviewer ↔ Refactorer ↔ Architect) without rewriting every time. Backend (new file `internal/studio/prompt_templates.go`): `PromptTemplate{ID, Name, Category, Description, Prompt}` struct + curated set of 11 templates across five categories — **Coding** (Pair Programmer = balanced default; Code Reviewer = read-only with severity grouping; Test Writer = branch-targeted, edge-case-first; Refactorer = behaviour-preserving with test gates; Bug Hunter = root-cause-first with regression tests), **Design** (Software Architect = ADRs + tradeoffs without implementation; Performance Optimizer = measure-before-optimise discipline), **Docs** (Documentation Writer = read-the-code-first; Code Explainer = read-only teaching mode), **Ops** (Incident Responder = stabilise-then-RCA, postmortem-aware), **Reset** (Minimal = empty for clean-slate paste). New `(*Studio).ListPromptTemplates()` Wails method returns a deep copy so a frontend mutation can't poison the singleton. Frontend (`ChatPanel.tsx`): new "Templates" button in the system-prompt editor action row (next to Save/Cancel). Click → loads templates lazily (one RPC, then cached in component state) and toggles a categorised picker below the editor. Each template shows name + description; clicking replaces the draft (Save still requires explicit click — picking doesn't auto-overwrite the persisted prompt). Templates list is grouped by Category with uppercase labels. CSS: `.sys-prompt-templates` dropdown + `.sys-prompt-template-item` button styling matching the rest of the app. Reset on session switch so a different project's draft doesn't surface a stale picker. Tests (8 in `prompt_templates_test.go`): list non-empty; every template has all required fields populated (only "minimal" is allowed empty Prompt); IDs are unique; Names are unique; ListPromptTemplates returns a copy (frontend mutation doesn't poison singleton); all five expected categories present (defends against accidentally dropping a block in a refactor); minimal template's Prompt is empty as designed; no template has trailing whitespace (would render as visible cursor offset). `ListPromptTemplates` at **100%**. Studio test suite: **341 tests**, coverage: **92.9%**.

- **Agent activity timeline (Ctrl+Shift+A)** -- new substantial feature: a modal that aggregates every tool call in the current session into a chronological list with summary stats. Solves the "I scrolled forever and still can't find what the agent ran" problem on long sessions. New header button (Activity icon, always visible) + `Ctrl+Shift+A` keyboard shortcut. ## What's Done (iter 340+)

- **User-defined system prompt templates** -- extension of iter 320's curated library. Users can now save their own custom prompts ("React + shadcn", "Embedded Rust driver", "Postgres performance review") and re-use them across projects. The system-prompt editor's templates picker merges curated + user-saved entries into one list with the user's templates pinned to the top under category "Yours". Backend (new file `internal/studio/user_prompt_templates.go`): `(*Studio).SaveUserPromptTemplate(name, description, prompt) -> id` (validates: name required + 80-char cap, prompt required + 20 KB cap, description optional + 200-char cap; preserves leading whitespace in prompt body for intentional indentation); `DeleteUserPromptTemplate(id)` (idempotent on unknown ID); `ListUserPromptTemplates() -> []PromptTemplate` (sorted newest-first; projects onto the same `PromptTemplate` shape so the picker can render mixed lists; falls back to `Saved YYYY-MM-DD` description when none provided). Storage: single JSON file at `<configDir>/user_prompt_templates.json` (same flat-file pattern as drafts/pins). **Dedup by lowercased name**: saving "React Dev" then "REACT DEV" updates the existing template in place rather than creating a duplicate, preserving the original ID — matches user expectation that "Save as <descriptive name>" overwrites the previous version. Empty list removes the file rather than persisting `[]`. New "Save as…" button next to "Templates" in the editor action row uses `window.prompt` for the name and calls `SaveUserPromptTemplate` with the current draft. Per-row delete (Trash2 icon) on items in the "Yours" category only — curated templates are not user-deletable. Picker categories sorted by stable order: `[Yours, Coding, Design, Docs, Ops, Reset]`. Tests (17 in `user_prompt_templates_test.go`): round-trip save/list; reject empty/whitespace name AND prompt; truncate over-long fields at the per-field caps; dedup by lowercased name updates in place (ID stable, content updated, last save's case preserved); distinct names create distinct entries; delete removes; delete unknown ID idempotent; delete empty ID errors; last delete removes the file; list empty for missing file; sorted newest-first; fallback "Saved <date>" description; corrupt file returns error from List/Save/Delete (handles three call sites); empty file returns nil; preserves leading whitespace in prompt body. `userPromptTemplatesPath` and `ListUserPromptTemplates` at **100%**; `Save`/`Delete`/`load` at 90.9%-93.8%.
