## What's Done (iter 350+)

- **Smart welcome screen with git context** -- the empty-session welcome screen now shows live git state (current branch, ahead/behind upstream, uncommitted changes, recent commits) when the project is a git repo. Users opening a project see at a glance "you're on `main`, 3 modified files, last commit 2 hours ago" and get one-click suggestions like "Review uncommitted changes" / "Draft a commit message" that pre-fill the input. Clickable file rows attach the path as `@ref` so the user can build a question around it. Backend (new file `internal/studio/git_status.go`): `(*Studio).GetProjectGitContext(projectID) -> *ProjectGitContext` returns `{Branch, IsRepo, ChangedFiles []GitFileChange, UntrackedFiles []GitFileChange, RecentCommits []GitCommit, AheadBehind string}`. Each git invocation runs through a 3-second timeout via `runGit`/`runGitBool` helpers so a hung git can't block the UI. All errors are swallowed (best-effort): missing git → empty context → frontend falls through to language-only welcome. `git status --porcelain=v1` parsed into per-file `Status` enum (`modified`/`added`/`deleted`/`renamed`/`untracked`/`changed`). `git log --pretty=format:"%h<sep>%ct<sep>%s" -5` decodes to `{Hash, Subject, Age, AgeMs}`; **the separator is `|||~~~|||` rather than NUL bytes** because NULs get mangled through `exec` — discovered when the first test variant returned zero commits. Caps lists at 20 changed / 20 untracked / 5 commits. Ahead-behind via `git rev-list --left-right --count HEAD...@{u}` (silently empty when there's no upstream). Includes `humanAge(time.Duration) string` formatter ("just now", "3 minutes ago", "2 days ago", "1 week ago", "5 months ago"). Frontend (`ChatPanel.tsx`): new `gitCtx` state loaded once per project switch via `GetProjectGitContext`; `useEffect` clears stale state immediately on switch with cancelled-flag guard. Welcome screen extended with a `.welcome-git` block containing branch + ahead/behind chip, "working tree clean" indicator when no changes, two suggestion-chip buttons that pre-fill the input (review changes / draft commit message), file list with status-coloured single-letter prefix (M=amber, A=green, D=red, ?=indigo, R=purple), and a recent-commits list. Click a file → appends `@<path>` to input + focuses textarea. Tests (8 in `git_status_test.go`): unknown project errors; non-repo dir returns `IsRepo:false` cleanly; real repo with modified+untracked detected; commits cap at 5; clean repo reports no changes; status detection for added/deleted (separate test); ahead/behind populated when upstream is set (clones bare repo to test); `humanAge` covers all unit boundaries (just-now/minutes/hours/days/weeks/months/years, with singular/plural); `plural` helper covers 0/1/N; `runGit` swallows non-zero exit. `humanAge`/`plural`/`runGitBool` at **100%**; `GetProjectGitContext` 88.3%, `runGit` 85.7% (timeout branch is hard to test deterministically). Studio test suite: **371 tests**, coverage: **92.8%**.

## What's Done (iter 360+)

- **Per-project usage statistics** -- aggregated cost / tokens / turn count across every session in a project, persisted across restarts so users can answer "how much has this project cost me?" without manual accounting. Backend: extended `historyFile` with optional `Usage *SessionUsage` field — same forward-compatible pattern used for `parentSessionID` in iter 310+ (no schema bump). New `SessionUsage{TotalCostUSD, TotalInputTokens, TotalOutputTokens, TotalCacheTokens, TurnCount, LastTurnAt}` struct. Three save variants now: `SaveHistoryWithName` (preserves both parent + usage from disk via read-then-write), `SaveHistoryWithMetadata` (explicit parent, preserves usage), new `SaveHistoryWithUsage` (explicit-everything, used by the agent loop after each `chat:complete`). Common writer extracted to internal `saveHistoryFull(projectID, name, parent, usage, history)`. New helpers: `LoadHistoryUsage`, `loadPrevMetadata` (DRY for read-then-preserve). `loadHistoryRaw` signature extended from 4 → 5 returns (entries, name, parent, usage, err). `ChatSession` gains `usage *SessionUsage` field; `NewProject`'s session-load loop populates it from disk so totals survive restart. Agent loop's `chat:complete` path now atomically bumps `session.usage` (under `session.mu.Lock()`) and persists via `SaveHistoryWithUsage`. New `(*Studio).ProjectUsageStats(projectID) -> *ProjectUsageStatsInfo` Wails method walks every session, accumulates totals, and returns `{TotalCostUSD, TotalInputTokens, TotalOutputTokens, TotalCacheTokens, TotalTurns, TotalSessions, Sessions: []SessionUsageInfo}`. Sessions sorted by cost desc → turn count desc → name asc (stable so equal-cost rows don't reshuffle on every call). Frontend (`ChatPanel.tsx`): new `FileText`-icon button in chat header next to Activity → opens a usage modal. Modal shows a 4-cell totals grid (Cost / Input / Output / Turns) with the cost cell highlighted green, then a per-session table with columns `Session / Cost / In / Out / Turns`. Lazy-loaded on open; "—" placeholder for never-run sessions; "approximate, not authoritative billing" footer. Reset on session switch. Esc closes. Tests (8 in `usage_stats_test.go`): unknown project errors; empty project all zeros (default session listed); aggregates across two seeded sessions correctly + sorts by cost desc; nil usage renders as zeros (never-run sessions still appear); `SaveHistoryWithUsage` round-trip; `SaveHistoryWithName` preserves usage from disk (read-then-write contract); `SaveHistoryWithMetadata` preserves usage from disk; `NewProject` restores usage on cold start. `SaveHistoryWithUsage` and `LoadHistoryUsage` at **100%**; `ProjectUsageStats` at 91.9%. Studio test suite: **379 tests**, coverage: **92.9%**.

## What's Done (iter 370+)

- **Bulk session export** -- new substantial feature: export EVERY session in a project as a single Markdown document, sorted most-recently-used first. Useful for archiving, handoff, or feeding the whole project's history into a different tool. Backend: `(*Studio).ExportProjectAllSessions(projectID) -> string` builds `# <Project> — all sessions` header with timestamp + session count, then per-session `## <Session Name>` blocks with their conversation history. Skips sessions that have no visible text (only tool-call / function-response turns) so the output isn't noisy. Filters thinking/reasoning parts (matching `ExportChat` semantics — internal model deliberation never lands in human-readable exports). Materialises the per-session block locally before appending to the outer builder so the session lock is released before doing the cross-session string concat. Sort by `lastUsedAt` desc with `CreatedAt asc` as the tiebreaker (matches `ListChatSessions`). Empty result shows "_No sessions with visible history yet._" footnote rather than failing. New `plural2(n int, _ string) string` helper for header pluralisation ("1 session" vs "5 sessions"); kept separate from `git_status.go::plural` because that one prepends the count while this only appends "s". Frontend (`ChatPanel.tsx`): new "Export all sessions" item in the `…` chat header menu (next to "Export as Markdown"); same Blob+download pattern as the per-session export, with date-stamped filename `<project>-all-sessions-<YYYY-MM-DD>.md`. New `/exportall` slash command + autocomplete entry. Tests (7 in `bulk_export_test.go`): unknown project errors; empty-history project shows "No sessions" footnote; two-session round-trip with most-recently-used ordering verified; sessions with only tool-call turns are skipped (not rendered as empty sections); thinking parts excluded; "1 session" singular pluralisation; `plural2` covers 0/1/N. `ExportProjectAllSessions` at 96.6%, `plural2` at **100%**. Studio test suite: **386 tests**, coverage: **92.9%**.

## What's Done (iter 380+)

- **Provider health check** -- new substantial feature: a "Test connection" button next to each API key in Settings probes the provider's API and reports back whether the key + URL combination actually works. Closes the "did I copy the API key right?" question without forcing the user to send a real chat message. Backend (new file `internal/studio/provider_health.go`): `(*Studio).CheckProviderHealth(provider) -> *ProviderHealthInfo` dispatches per provider — `glm`/`minimax`/`kimi` go through `probeAnthropicCompat` (GET `<base>/v1/models` with `Authorization: Bearer <key>` + `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01` headers), `ollama` goes through `probeOllama` (GET `<base>/api/tags`, no auth). 5-second `http.Client` timeout via `context.WithTimeout` so a hung provider can't block the Settings UI. Status-code interpretation: 2xx → OK; 401 → "invalid API key"; 403 → "forbidden"; 404 → **treated as OK** (some Anthropic-compatible providers don't expose `/v1/models` but do have working chat endpoints — the description notes "may still work" so the user isn't blocked); 4xx → "client error"; 5xx → "server error"; connection refused → "unreachable"; deadline exceeded → "timeout". Returns `{Provider, OK, LatencyMs, StatusCode, Error, Endpoint, Description}`. Drains the response body before close so connections stay reusable. Frontend (`SettingsPage.tsx`): each API-key card gets a "Test connection" button (Wifi icon + Loader2 spinner while in flight) plus a result chip — green ✓ for OK, red ✗ for error, with the description text and endpoint URL in the tooltip. The chip clears whenever the user edits the corresponding API key (dirty state) so a stale "Connected" badge doesn't lie about the new untested key. Mapping `glmKey/miniMaxKey/kimiKey/ollamaUrl → glm/minimax/kimi/ollama` so the dirty-state listener wipes the right cache. Ollama section gets the same Test button. Tests (13 in `provider_health_test.go`): unknown provider errors; GLM with no key short-circuits; the helper-tested `doProbe` covers all status branches (200 / 401 / 403 / 404 / 4xx / 5xx / unreachable / timeout-via-closed-port); `CheckProviderHealth("ollama")` end-to-end against httptest.Server with custom URL; default-URL fallback to localhost:11434 when ollamaUrl is empty; `probeAnthropicCompat` driven against a fake server verifies Authorization Bearer + anthropic-version headers AND the /v1/models endpoint suffix; empty-key short-circuit makes no HTTP call. `probeAnthropicCompat` 86.7%, `doProbe` 90.3%, `CheckProviderHealth` 85.7%. Studio test suite: **399 tests**, coverage: **92.7%**.

## What's Done (iter 390+)

- **Conversation summary via LLM** -- new substantial feature: `/summarize` slash command (also in chat-header menu) generates a 3-5 bullet TL;DR of the current session via a one-shot LLM call. Useful for long sessions / context handoff / "wait, what was this about?" moments. Backend (new file `internal/studio/summarize.go`): `(*Studio).SummarizeSession(projectID, sessionID) -> string` snapshots session history under read lock, strips thinking parts (no leaking model deliberation) AND function-response-only turns (noise for summarisation), then sends "Summarize the conversation above in 3-5 concise bullet points..." through the project's existing client via `SendMessageWithHistory`. 60-second timeout via `context.WithTimeout` since reasoning models can take time. Doesn't modify session history — pure read + return. Returns errors on: empty projectID, unknown project/session, no visible history, init-client failure, LLM call failure (with humanized error message via existing `humanizeAPIError`), partial-collect (returns text + error so the user sees what got through), or empty model response (rejected — better than silent empty modal). Frontend (`ChatPanel.tsx`): new `/summarize` slash command + autocomplete entry; "Summarize session" item in the `…` chat header menu; modal with `Loader2` spinner during fetch + warning that this calls the LLM; rendered summary uses the same `ReactMarkdown + MD_REHYPE_PLUGINS` pipeline as chat messages so bullets/links/code render correctly. Modal actions: `Copy` (clipboard), `Re-generate` (clears + re-fetches — costs tokens again), `Esc/backdrop close`. Reset on session switch so a different session's draft summary doesn't bleed across. CSS: `.summary-modal` shares the dim+blur backdrop pattern of other modals; `.summary-content` scrollable for long summaries; `.summary-actions` button row + footer disclaimer "Approximate · LLM-generated · Not part of session history". Bonus: **Go 1.25 wg.Go cleanup** — replaced two `s.wg.Add(1) + go func() { defer s.wg.Done(); ... }()` patterns with `s.wg.Go(func() { ... })` in `SendMessage` and `Dispatch`. Goes lint-green on the long-deferred `(waitgroup)` warnings. Tests (9 in `summarize_test.go`): empty projectID errors; unknown project errors; unknown session errors; empty-history rejected; happy path with mockClient verifies callCount==1 + content; thinking parts stripped from snapshot; function-response-only history rejected (no LLM call); empty/whitespace-only model response rejected; empty sessionID defaults to "default". `SummarizeSession` at 87.5%. Studio test suite: **408 tests**, coverage: **92.6%**.

## What's Done (iter 400+)

- **Per-project budget with spend warnings** -- new substantial feature that closes the "how much have I spent on this project?" loop opened by iter 290+ (cost estimator) and iter 360+ (usage stats). Users set a USD spend cap once, then the chat header chip + usage modal show progress at a glance and visually escalate at 80% / 100%. Backend: extended `ProjectConfig` with `BudgetUSD float64` (yaml `budget_usd`, json `budgetUSD`) — additive field, old config files load with budget=0 (no cap). Wired through `Project.BudgetUSD`, `ProjectInfo.BudgetUSD`, `NewProject`/`Info()`/`ToConfig()` so it round-trips on save+restart. New `(*Studio).SetProjectBudget(id, budgetUSD float64) error` Wails method validates non-negative and clamps at $100,000 (defends against typos like "100000000"); does NOT invalidate the cached client (no model state depends on budget). Persists via `saveConfig` so it survives restart. Frontend: imports `SetProjectBudget` + `EventsOn` + `DollarSign` icon. New states `projectTotalCostUSD`, `showBudget`, `budgetDraft`, `budgetError`, `budgetSaving` in `ChatPanel.tsx`. New effect loads `projectTotalCostUSD` on `activeProjectId` switch via `ProjectUsageStats` AND subscribes to `chat:complete` events to refresh after every turn — so the chip stays accurate across all sessions in the project, not just the active one. Cancelled-flag guard prevents stale responses from bleeding across switches. Modal toggles + `budgetError`/`budgetSaving` reset on session switch (added to the existing reset useEffect). Added to the global Escape handler with explicit `budget-backdrop` class so it doesn't collide with the dispatch-backdrop bail-out. Chat header chip: `chat-header-budget` button shown only when `BudgetUSD > 0`, displays `$X.YY/$Z.ZZ NN%` with `DollarSign` icon; click opens the editor pre-populated with current value; `warn` class (amber) at 80%, `over` class (red) at 100%. Uses `Math.max(projectTotalCostUSD, sessionCostUSD)` to handle the race where chat:complete refresh hasn't yet arrived for the very first turn after switching projects. Header menu: "Set budget" item with green dot when budget is set; same pre-fills + opens the editor. `/budget` slash command added with autocomplete entry. Budget editor modal: focused number input with `$` prefix, max=100000, step=0.01, IME-guarded Esc/Enter handlers, Save/Cancel buttons with `budgetSaving` spinner state, inline error banner on validation or backend failure. Empty input == 0 == clear cap. Usage stats modal gets a budget progress block at the top of the body: filled green/amber/red bar with percentage label and "Edit" button when set; falls through to a "No budget set / Set budget" CTA when zero so first-time users have an entry point even without using the menu. CSS: full new `.chat-header-budget` (with `warn`/`over` color states) + `.budget-modal`/`.budget-input-row`/`.budget-input` (custom number input without spinners) + `.usage-stats-budget` block (rounded inset card with bar + label + edit button). Tests (3 in `budget_test.go`): validation (negative rejected with "must be >= 0", over-cap rejected with "exceeds maximum", unknown project errors); round-trip (default 0 → set 25.50 → read via Info() → set 0 again to clear); upper boundary (exactly $100,000 accepted); ToConfig persistence + NewProject restore round-trip. Studio test suite: **411 tests**, coverage: **92.6%**.

## What's Done (iter 410+)

- **Per-tab unread indicator** -- new substantial UX feature that fixes a real multi-session pain point: when an agent finishes a turn (or errors out) in a session you weren't viewing, you previously had no way to know unless you happened to have the OS notification surface visible. Now every inactive session tab shows an amber badge with the unread count (1, 2, 3, …, 9+) and a subtle weight bump on the tab label so the cue isn't color-only. Frontend-only (no backend change). Store: `chatStore.ts` gains `unread: Record<string, number>` (chatKey → count) plus `bumpUnread(chatKey)` and `clearUnread(chatKey)` actions. Cleanup wired into existing `clearChat`/`dropSession`/`dropProject` so a deleted session doesn't leak a stale unread entry. Event handlers: `useWailsEvents.ts` `chat:complete` and `chat:error` both bump `unread[chatKey]` when the user isn't currently viewing this exact session. The "viewing" check is three-way: `activeProjectId === data.projectID` AND `activeSession[projectID] === sessionID` AND `document.hasFocus()` — any miss bumps. The `document.hasFocus()` term means even when the user is on the matching tab, an unfocused window still bumps so they see the badge when they tab back in. Tab rendering (`App.tsx`): new `unreadCount` derived from `unread[sessionKey]` (suppressed when `view === s.id` to avoid a flash before the clear effect runs); renders `.tab-badge.unread` with the number (`9+` cap) when count > 0 and the session isn't `live`. The `live` badge takes precedence — a still-running session shows "live", not the unread count, so the two don't visually compete. Tooltip extended to include "N new turn(s) since last viewed". `view-tab.has-unread:not(.active) .tab-label` adds `font-weight: 600` so the cue isn't purely color-based (helps with reduced color sensitivity). Clear-on-switch effect: new `useEffect([activeProjectId, view, clearUnread])` calls `clearUnread(activeProjectId + '_' + view)` whenever the user lands on a chat tab — no manual click-to-dismiss needed. Excludes 'files' / 'settings' (not real sessions). CSS: new `.tab-badge.unread` (accent-colored, distinct from `.live` green and `.err` red) + `badgePop` keyframe (0.6 → 1.18 → 1 scale bounce) so the badge briefly pops when first appearing, drawing the user's attention without being annoying. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules, 234 ms), TypeScript clean, Rules-of-Hooks audited (all new hooks declared before the existing `if (!ready) return` early return at App.tsx:338). Studio test suite unchanged: **411 tests**, coverage **92.6%**.

## What's Done (iter 420+)

- **Sidebar project search + Ctrl+Shift+P shortcut** -- new substantial UX feature that addresses a real pain for users with many projects. Previously, the only way to find a project was to scroll the sidebar (visual search) or open the Ctrl+K command palette (keyboard search, but loses sidebar context). Now there's an in-place search input that filters the project list visually so the user keeps the sidebar layout, project metadata (git branch, model, status dot), and right-click context menu all available. Frontend-only. Sidebar.tsx: new `searchQuery` state + `searchInputRef`. Search input rendered between the header and the project list, but only when `projects.length >= 4` AND `!collapsed` — solo-project users don't see redundant chrome, and a collapsed sidebar has no room. Filter matches case-insensitive substrings against name, directory, AND git branch (the three things shown on a project card), so users can search by `~/work/cli` to find a path-matched project, or `main` to find all projects on the main branch. Sort + filter happens in a single IIFE inside `.project-list` so the existing `sorted` (lastUsedAt desc) order is preserved among matches. Empty-state when query has matches=0 ("No projects match \"foo\""). Sidebar footer count switches to "X of Y" while filtering so users always know the filtered count vs total. Esc inside the search input clears the query and blurs (so the user can immediately tab to the project list). New "×" clear button appears when query is non-empty. Window-level keyboard shortcut: `Ctrl+Shift+P` / `Cmd+Shift+P` focuses + selects the search input. Distinct from `Ctrl+P` (file picker) and `Ctrl+K` (command palette). No-op when search isn't rendered (fewer than 4 projects); the focus call returns early on null ref. IME-guarded keydown handler. CommandPalette.tsx: new "Search projects in sidebar" action under the Navigate group with Ctrl+Shift+P hint that uses `requestAnimationFrame` to defer focus call until after the palette unmounts (without this the focus call lands during teardown and never visibly receives focus). SettingsPage shortcuts grid: 17th shortcut entry added. CSS: new `.sidebar-search` block (subtle grey background that brightens on focus, accent-colored search icon when focused, transparent input, hover-ready clear button) + `.sidebar-search-empty` (italic muted "no matches" text) — designed to disappear into the sidebar chrome when not in use. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean. Studio test suite unchanged: **411 tests**, coverage **92.6%**.

## What's Done (iter 430+)

- **Project pinning to top of sidebar** -- new substantial UX feature that fixes the "my main project keeps drifting down when I touch other projects briefly" pain. Pinned projects always sort above unpinned regardless of `lastUsedAt`, so a user with a daily-use project can anchor it to the top. Backend: `ProjectConfig` gains `Pinned bool` (yaml `pinned`, json `pinned`, both omitempty so old config files load with pinned=false). `Project.Pinned` field, `ProjectInfo.Pinned`, `NewProject`/`Info()`/`ToConfig()` all wire it through so it round-trips on save+restart. New `(*Studio).SetProjectPinned(id string, pinned bool) error` Wails method holds `s.mu.Lock()` for the duration to atomically check exists+update+save (no client cache invalidation needed — pin state doesn't affect any model behavior). Frontend: Wails JS/TS bindings hand-patched. `ProjectInfo` interface in `projectStore.ts` and `models.ts` both gain `pinned?: boolean`. Sidebar sort comparator updated: `(ap !== bp) → bp - ap` runs first so pinned projects always anchor to the top, then existing lastUsedAt-desc + alpha tiebreaker rules apply within each pin group. Inline `Pin` icon (slight 15° rotation, accent color) appears next to pinned project names. Right-click context menu: new "Pin to top" / "Unpin from top" item between Rename and Delete (separator above and below) with `Pin`/`PinOff` icons that flip based on current state. Optimistic local update via `updateProject(id, { pinned: next })` so the sort runs immediately on click; `SetProjectPinned` failure rolls back the optimistic update + logs to console (silent UX fallback — failure here is non-blocking). Tests (2 in `pin_project_test.go`): `TestSetProjectPinned` (default unpinned, unknown-project rejected, pin → Info reports true, unpin → Info reports false); `TestSetProjectPinned_PersistsToConfig` (ToConfig.Pinned correct, NewProject restores pinned state from config — full restart-style round-trip). `SetProjectPinned` at 100%. Studio test suite: **413 tests**, coverage: **92.7%** (+0.1pp).

## What's Done (iter 440+)

- **In-app help modal (`/help`, `Ctrl+/`, `?`)** -- new substantial UX/discoverability feature. Previously the only way to find a slash command or keyboard shortcut was to scroll the Settings page (out of context) or remember it. Now there's a comprehensive, searchable in-context help modal accessible from anywhere. Frontend-only. ChatPanel.tsx: new `showHelp` + `helpQuery` state. Three open paths: (1) `/help` slash command + autocomplete entry; (2) `Ctrl+/` window-level keyboard shortcut; (3) `?` window-level shortcut that fires only when the user isn't typing in an input/textarea/contentEditable (so it doesn't steal "?" mid-prose); (4) "Help & shortcuts" item in the chat header `…` menu with `Ctrl+/` hint. Modal rendered via IIFE inside JSX so all the data structures (slashItems, shortcuts grouped by category, gestures) live next to where they're rendered — much easier to keep in sync than data + render in separate functions. Slash commands section is generated from the existing `SLASH_COMMANDS` array, so it stays in sync automatically when commands are added/removed. Shortcuts section grouped into Navigate / Search / Chat / Help categories so users can skim by intent. Mouse / gestures section enumerates non-keyboard interactions: right-click message context menu, double-click to rename, drag-file-to-chat, "@" file references, click pinned-context badge ×, click budget chip — these were entirely undocumented. Search filter at the top of the modal: case-insensitive substring match against both the left (key/command) and right (description) cells; empty-state ("No matches for ...") when filter eliminates everything. Esc inside the search clears the query first, then closes the modal on second press. Also reset on session switch and added to the global Escape handler with explicit `help-backdrop` class. Settings shortcuts table also gets the new `Ctrl+/  or  ?` row pointing at the in-app help. CSS: full new `.help-modal`/`.help-search-row`/`.help-rows` block — matches the existing modal pattern (centered fixed position, blur backdrop, fade-in animation) but with a 720px width to fit two-column rows comfortably. `.help-rows` uses `display: grid` with `grid-template-columns: minmax(180px, 220px) 1fr` so the keyboard-key cells line up vertically across all sections; rows use `display: contents` so each row's two cells participate in the parent grid directly. `.help-row-left` styled like a small key cap (subtle background + border + monospace) so visual scanning is fast. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean, Rules-of-Hooks audited (new state at lines 92-93, well before the first early return at line 1134). Studio test suite unchanged: **413 tests**, coverage **92.7%**.

## What's Done (iter 450+)

- **Default project budget in Settings + bug fix: ask_user unread bump** -- two related polish-tier improvements that close gaps from iter 400+ (per-project budget) and iter 410+ (per-tab unread badge).
  - **Default budget:** new `Settings.DefaultBudgetUSD float64` field (yaml `default_budget_usd`, json `defaultBudgetUSD`, both omitempty for backward-compat with old configs). `AddProject` now copies `s.config.Settings.DefaultBudgetUSD` into the new project's `BudgetUSD` field on creation — symmetric with how `DefaultThinkingMode`/`DefaultThinkingBudget` are already cascaded. `UpdateSettings` clamps the field: negative → 0, > $100,000 → $100,000 (silent clamps mirror the per-project validation; better than rejecting save for a typo). Frontend: `Settings` interface in `settingsStore.ts` gains `defaultBudgetUSD: number`; Wails `models.ts` Settings class extended with the field + constructor assignment. App.tsx settings hydration includes the new field with `|| 0` fallback for old configs. SettingsPage.tsx: new "Default Budget" section between "Default Thinking" and "API Keys" with a number input (`min=0 max=100000 step=0.01`), inline clamp on change for safety, hint text "Per-project overrides take precedence; existing projects are not affected." `handleSave` includes the new field in both the `UpdateSettings` payload and the `keys` array passed to `updateField` so the local store stays in sync.
  - **ask_user unread bump:** `useWailsEvents.ts` `chat:ask_user` handler now bumps `unread` for the chatKey when the user isn't currently viewing this exact session — matching the same three-way active check (project + session + window focus) used for `chat:complete`/`chat:error` in iter 410+. Without this, an agent asking for input in a background tab would silently block forever and the user had no indication. Now the unread badge appears on the tab so the user sees something needs attention.
  - Tests (3 in `default_budget_test.go`): `TestAddProject_AppliesDefaultBudget` (sets DefaultBudgetUSD=12.34 then confirms `info.BudgetUSD == 12.34`); `TestAddProject_DefaultBudgetZero` (no default → new project has BudgetUSD=0); `TestUpdateSettings_ClampsDefaultBudget` (table-style: -50 → 0, 999999 → 100000, valid 25.50 passes through). Studio test suite: **416 tests** (+3), coverage holds at **92.7%**. Frontend build clean.

## What's Done (iter 460+)

- **Chat input history recall (Up/Down arrows) + "Mark all sessions as read"** -- two daily-use UX improvements that complement each other. Frontend-only.
  - **Up/Down recall:** terminal-style chat input recall. Pressing Up when the input is empty (or already in recall mode) walks back through previous user messages — most recent first. Down walks forward. Esc / past-most-recent restores whatever the user was typing before recall started. Three guards keep it from interfering with multi-line edits or other shortcuts: (1) the "no newlines" check (`!input.includes('\n')`) so Up/Down still work for cursor movement inside a multi-line draft; (2) skip when slash-command popup is showing (popup's own ArrowUp/Down navigation takes precedence); (3) cancel recall on any non-arrow keystroke (so the user can edit the recalled message freely without surprise behavior on the next Up). New state: `historyIdx` (-1 = not recalling). `savedDraftRef` (useRef, captures pre-recall draft on first Up). Reset on send + on session switch (added to existing reset useEffect). Cursor placed at end after each recall via `requestAnimationFrame` so React commits the new value first. Filters to text-only user messages (function-response synthetic user turns excluded). Help modal Chat section gets a new row: "Up arrow (empty input) — Recall previous user message (Down to walk forward, Esc to restore)".
  - **Mark all sessions as read:** new `chatStore.clearAllUnreadForProject(projectID)` action drops every unread entry whose key starts with `${projectID}_`. Wired into the chat header `…` menu (with `CheckCircle` icon, between "Help & shortcuts" and the danger separator) and the Ctrl+K command palette under the Actions group. One click clears every tab's badge in the active project — useful after stepping away and coming back to a wall of unread indicators that aren't actionable.
  - Hooks audit: new state declared at lines 92-97 alongside `showHelp`/`helpQuery`, well before the first early return at line 1134. Studio test suite unchanged: **416 tests**, coverage **92.7%**. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean.

## What's Done (iter 470+)

- **Quick model switcher (`Ctrl+M`)** -- substantial UX feature. Switching the active project's model previously required clicking the ProviderSelect popup, picking a provider, then a model, then Apply. Power users who switch models multiple times per session (e.g. "use GLM for the planning, then Kimi for the long-context refactor") found this slow. Now `Ctrl+M` opens a Spotlight-style picker showing every provider×model combo across all configured providers, filtered live. Frontend-only (no backend change). ChatPanel.tsx: imports `SetProjectProvider` + `GetProject`. New state — `showModelSwitcher`, `modelSwitcherQuery`, `modelSwitcherIdx`, `modelSwitcherSaving`, `modelSwitcherError` — plus a `providers` selector from `useSettingsStore`. Window keydown handler: `Ctrl+M` / `Cmd+M` toggles the modal. Modal flattens `providers` into one list (`{provider, model, label, isCurrent}`); search filters the label (provider name + model) so users can type "kimi", "5.1", "ollama qwen" etc. ↑↓ moves selection (clamped to filtered length); Enter applies; Esc closes; mouse hover also moves selection (matches Spotlight behavior so the keyboard and mouse don't fight). Apply path: calls `SetProjectProvider`, then `updateProject` for immediate local feedback, then `GetProject` to refresh `contextWindow` (since different models have different windows — gauge would otherwise show stale value). Error state: failure surfaces as a red inline banner in the modal, modal stays open so user can retry. Disabled state during save with `Loader2` spinner inline. Empty state: "No models registered. Configure providers in Settings." when no providers loaded. The current model is marked with a green ✓ prefix and a "current" tag chip. Discoverability: (1) `Ctrl+M` window shortcut; (2) "Switch model" action in `Ctrl+K` command palette under Actions group with `Bot` icon — palette dispatches `gokin:open-model-switcher` custom event since synthesizing keydown is brittle, ChatPanel listens via new useEffect; (3) help modal Help section gets a new row "Ctrl+M — Quick model switcher"; (4) Settings shortcuts table updated. Reset on session switch + added to global Esc handler with explicit `model-switcher-backdrop` class. CSS: full new `.model-switcher-modal` block (520px wide, top:22% positioning so it sits Spotlight-style not centered, accent-colored selected item with current-tag inversion, monospace label). Hooks audit: new state at lines 99-103 alongside other early-state declarations, well before the first early return at line 1134; new useEffect for the open-model-switcher event listener after the openMemoryViewer effect — both before any early return. Studio test suite unchanged: **416 tests**, coverage **92.7%**. Frontend build clean.

## What's Done (iter 480+)

- **Session pinning to top of tab list** -- new substantial UX feature, parallel to project pinning (iter 430+). Users with many sessions per project can now anchor "main" sessions (e.g. "the long-running refactor", "the debugging notebook") to the top of the tab list so they don't drift down as siblings get used. Backend (new file `internal/studio/session_pins.go`): per-project JSON file at `~/.config/gokin-studio/session-pins/<projectID>.json` containing a sorted array of pinned session IDs. Three helpers — `loadPinnedSessions`, `savePinnedSessions` (empty input removes the file rather than persisting `[]`), `removeProjectSessionPins` (called from `RemoveProject` so deleted projects don't leak orphan files). Why a separate file rather than extending `historyFile`: the structure is small, frequently-read at startup, and adding to historyFile would require extending `loadHistoryRaw`'s 5-value return (touched by every test using it). `ChatSession` gains a `Pinned bool` field, `ChatSessionInfo` gains `Pinned bool`, and `Info()` surfaces it. `NewProject` pre-loads the pin map once before the per-session disk loop and sets `sess.Pinned = pinned[sid]` on each session — no per-session file hit, hydrated at startup. New `(*Studio).SetSessionPinned(projectID, sessionID string, pinned bool) error` Wails method: validates project + session existence, mutates `sess.Pinned` under the session lock, then rebuilds the pinned set from the LIVE session map (not the on-disk file) and persists. The rebuild-from-live approach means two concurrent SetSessionPinned calls for different sessions can race on the file — last writer wins — but since the in-memory truth (`sess.Pinned`) is what drives the sort, the user can fix any drift with a single click. `ListChatSessions` sort comparator: `if result[i].Pinned != result[j].Pinned { return result[i].Pinned }` runs FIRST, so pinned sessions always anchor to the top regardless of LastUsedAt; within each pin group the existing recent-first + default-last rules still apply. Tests (6 in `session_pins_test.go`): `TestSetSessionPinned` (default unpinned, empty/unknown project/session rejected, pin → ListChatSessions reports true, unpin → false); `TestSetSessionPinned_EmptySessionIDDefaultsToDefault` (empty sessionID conventionally maps to "default"); `TestSetSessionPinned_SortPinnedFirst` (pinned default leapfrogs more-recently-used unpinned session); `TestPinnedSessionsRoundTrip` (file save/load cycle, false-valued entries excluded, empty save removes the file, empty projectID rejected); `TestPinnedSessionsRestart` (writes pin file, then `NewProject` restores Pinned state — full restart-style round-trip); `TestRemoveProject_CleansUpSessionPins` (file gone after RemoveProject). `SetSessionPinned` at 96.4%, `sessionPinsPath` at 100%, savePinnedSessions/loadPinnedSessions at 83/77%. Frontend: Wails JS/TS bindings hand-patched. `SessionTab` interface gains `pinned?: boolean` (App.tsx); `setSessions` calls now copy `pinned: !!s.pinned` from `ChatSessionInfo`. Tab rendering: new `is-pinned` class + inline `Pin` icon (slight 15° rotation, accent color, matching `.project-pin-icon` from sidebar) shown next to pinned tab names. New `onContextMenu` handler on every tab opens a right-click menu (state: `tabCtxMenu = {id, x, y}` with viewport clamping, Esc to close). Menu items: "Pin to top" / "Unpin from top" with `Pin`/`PinOff` icons that flip based on current state + "Rename" (the existing double-click rename hidden behind the same menu so users discover it). Pin click: optimistic local update via `setSessions` so the UI re-sorts immediately; `SetSessionPinned` failure rolls back; on success, refetches `ListChatSessions` to get the canonical sorted order. New `useEffect([tabCtxMenu])` registers the Esc handler only while the menu is open. CSS: new `.tab-pin-icon` block (accent color, 15° rotation, flex-shrink: 0 so it doesn't compress on narrow tabs) + `.view-tab.is-pinned .tab-label { font-weight: 600 }` so pinned tabs are visually emphasized even before the icon catches the eye. Studio test suite: **422 tests** (+6), coverage: **92.6%** (-0.1pp, expected dip from new code paths). Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean.

## What's Done (iter 490+)

- **Custom user snippets — `/<name>` chat input macros** -- new substantial UX feature. Every user has a handful of frequently-typed prompts ("run the linter and fix issues", "explain this in 3 bullets", "summarize the diff for a commit message") that they re-type in slightly different forms each time. Now they can save them as `/<name>` snippets that expand into the chat input on selection. Different from `/system` templates (iter 340+, full project system prompt) — these are short text aliases for the chat input. Backend (new file `internal/studio/user_snippets.go`): `UserSnippet{ID, Name, Body, UpdatedAt}`. Storage at `~/.config/gokin-studio/user_snippets.json` (single file, not per-project — snippets are globally available across all projects). Three Wails methods: `SaveUserSnippet(name, body) -> id` (validates name 1-30 chars matching `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`, body 1-10000 chars; strips leading "/" so users can paste either form; refuses reserved names that conflict with built-in slash commands like clear/export/help — keeps the built-in list in sync via a hardcoded map; dedup by lowercased name with in-place update preserving ID); `DeleteUserSnippet(id)` (idempotent — removing non-existent ID is not an error); `ListUserSnippets() -> []UserSnippet` (sorted alphabetically by lowercased name for predictable autocomplete ordering). Tests (8 in `user_snippets_test.go`): round-trip; leading-slash stripped; validation table covers empty name/body, whitespace-only, name>30 chars, invalid chars (space/slash/dot), reserved names (case-insensitive: clear/HELP/export), valid chars including dash and underscore; dedup-by-name preserves ID; delete + idempotent re-delete + empty-ID rejection; alphabetical sort with mixed case; empty list returns `[]` (not nil); body truncation at max bytes. Frontend: Wails JS/TS bindings hand-patched. ChatPanel.tsx: imports `ListUserSnippets`. New `userSnippets` state. Effect loads them on mount AND listens for `gokin:snippets-changed` event so Settings can trigger refresh after save/delete. SLASH_COMMANDS array refactored — built-ins extracted to `SLASH_COMMANDS_BUILTIN`, then a `useMemo` merges in user snippets tagged with `snippet: true` and a `body` field. Description shows a body preview (first 80 chars + "↳" prefix) so the autocomplete is informative. `executeSlashCommand` checks for the snippet tag FIRST: snippets call `setInput(body)` + cursor-at-end via `requestAnimationFrame` instead of executing an action — letting the user review and tweak before pressing Enter to send. Settings page: new "Snippets" section between "Default Budget" and "API Keys" with a count badge, a hint explaining the syntax/restrictions, a list of existing snippets (each row: `/<name>` in mono accent color + body preview + delete button with `Loader2` spinner during in-flight delete), and an inline create form (name input with `/` prefix chip + multi-line body textarea + `Plus`-iconed Save button + inline error banner). After each save/delete: refreshSnippets reloads + dispatches `gokin:snippets-changed` so the chat panel picks up the change without a session switch. CSS: `.snippets-list` (max 280px scrollable), `.snippet-row` (compact one-line per snippet), `.snippet-form-row` (input with mono `/` prefix that visually previews the final command shape), `.snippet-form-body` (mono textarea), `.snippet-form-error` (red banner matching budget-error pattern). Hooks audit: ChatPanel new state at line ~107 (before first early return at 1134); SettingsPage new state at lines 40-45 (before the only return at line 149) + new useEffect at line 52 (before settings-init useEffect at 54). Studio test suite: **430 tests** (+8), coverage: **92.6%**. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean.

## What's Done (iter 500+)

- **Bulk session management modal (`/sessions`)** -- new substantial UX feature. Users with many sessions per project (15-20+) had no way to clean up beyond clicking the close × on each tab one at a time. Now there's a multi-select modal with bulk operations. Frontend-only — uses existing `ListChatSessions` + `DeleteChatSession` Wails methods. ChatPanel.tsx: new state — `showSessionsMgr`, `sessionsMgrList`, `sessionsMgrSelected` (Set<string>), `sessionsMgrConfirm`, `sessionsMgrDeleting`, `sessionsMgrError`. New `openSessionsManager()` helper lazy-loads the session list (cheaper than pre-fetching on every chat panel render). Three open paths: (1) `/sessions` slash command; (2) "Manage sessions" item in chat header `…` menu (`ListChecks` icon); (3) Esc handler for closing. Modal layout: header with `ListChecks` icon, optional error banner, toolbar with bulk-select buttons, scrollable list of session rows, footer with selection count + actions. Bulk-select buttons compute their target sets at render time so the counts in the labels are always live: "Select all (N)", "Empty (N)" (sessions with `messages === 0`), "> 30 days (N)" (`lastUsedAt < now - 30d`), "> 90 days (N)" (`lastUsedAt < now - 90d`), "Clear selection". Each row shows: checkbox, name (with optional `Pin` icon for pinned sessions and italic `↳ parent` chip for forked sessions), and meta row with msg count + relative age (`fmtAge`: just-now / Nm / Nh / Nd). Click anywhere on the row toggles the checkbox (it's a `<label>`). Footer flips between two states: idle (selection count + Close + red Delete N button) and confirm (red explainer text + Cancel + red Confirm-delete button) — explicit two-step confirmation prevents accidental mass deletion. The Delete button is disabled when `selected.size === list.length` (would delete every session) — backend would reject anyway, but a UI block is more humane. Delete loop: iterates selected IDs, calls `DeleteChatSession` for each, calls `chatStore.dropSession` to clean local state, collects per-session failures. After completion: dispatches `gokin:sessions-changed` so App.tsx reloads its tab list, refetches the session list to show the updated state, surfaces any partial failures via the error banner. Help modal Help section gains a new row "/sessions — Manage sessions (multi-select + bulk delete)". CSS: full new `.sessions-mgr-modal` block (720px wide, max-height 84vh, blur backdrop) + `.sessions-mgr-toolbar` (flex-wrap pill buttons that hover-highlight the accent color when not disabled) + `.sessions-mgr-row` (label-wrapped row that toggles selection on click, with selected-state border and accent-color background tint, native checkbox accent-color override) + `.sessions-mgr-row-pin` (15° rotated accent pin, matching the tab-pin pattern from iter 480+) + `.sessions-mgr-confirm-text` (red text in the confirm state). Hooks audit: new state at lines ~117-122 alongside other early-state declarations, well before the first early return at line 1134. Studio test suite unchanged: **430 tests**, coverage **92.6%**. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean.

## What's Done (iter 510+)

- **Quiet mode — collapse tool calls into "N hidden" markers** -- new substantial UX feature for long agent runs. When the agent runs many bash/grep/edit/read calls between user turns, the chat fills with tool cards and the actual conversation gets buried. Quiet mode hides all tool/dispatch/thinking messages, leaving just user + assistant text. Each consecutive run of hidden messages is replaced by a one-line clickable marker showing the count ("5 tool calls hidden") + a "show" affordance to expand that specific group inline. Per-project preference persisted in `localStorage` (key `gokin:quietmode:<projectID>`) so different projects can have different defaults — a project where the agent runs lots of bash commands benefits from quiet mode by default, while a planning-heavy project might want everything visible. Frontend-only. ChatPanel.tsx: new `quietMode` boolean state (eagerly initialized from localStorage) + `expandedMarkers` Set<string> for individual marker overrides. New useEffect re-hydrates `quietMode` whenever `activeProjectId` changes (different project = different preference); resets `expandedMarkers` on session switch (otherwise stale ids from session A would leak into session B). New `displayMessages` useMemo applies the collapsing logic on top of the existing `filteredMessages` (search): it walks the message list, accumulates consecutive noise messages (role===tool/dispatch/thinking), and emits either the original messages (if the marker for that run is in `expandedMarkers`) or a synthetic `{role: 'hidden-marker', count, firstIdx, id: 'hidden-<idx>'}` entry. The render `.map` was switched from `filteredMessages` to `displayMessages` and a marker short-circuit was added: when `msg.role === 'hidden-marker'`, render a centered `.hidden-marker` button with `Zap` icon + count + uppercase "show" affordance, click adds the marker id to `expandedMarkers`. Toggle button in chat header (between FilePicker and Terminal): `Eye` icon when quiet mode is OFF, `EyeOff` (with `active-icon` class) when ON. Click flips the state, writes to localStorage, and clears `expandedMarkers` on disable so the next enable starts fresh. Help modal Chat section gets a new row documenting the toggle: "Eye icon in header — Toggle quiet mode...". CSS: new `.hidden-marker-row` (centered, smaller margin than regular messages) + `.hidden-marker` (rounded pill with dashed border that becomes solid + accent-color on hover) + `.hidden-marker-action` (uppercase "SHOW" text with vertical separator and accent color). Hooks audit: new state at lines ~149-156 + new useEffect at line ~167 (both well before the first early return at line ~1134). Studio test suite unchanged: **430 tests**, coverage **92.6%**. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean.

## What's Done (iter 520+)

- **Inline @path autocomplete popup** -- new substantial UX feature. Closes a long-standing gap: users had to know exact file paths for `@`-mention syntax to work, or open the separate Ctrl+P picker. Now typing `@<chars>` in the chat input pops up a list of matching files inline; ↑↓ Enter/Tab inserts; Esc closes. Backend (new file `internal/studio/file_suggest.go`): `(*Studio).ListProjectFiles(projectID string) ([]string, error)`. Recursive walk via `filepath.WalkDir` with `noiseDirNames` map excluding common build/dependency dirs (.git, node_modules, dist, build, target, vendor, .next, .nuxt, .venv, venv, __pycache__, .pytest_cache, .cache, .idea, .vscode, out, coverage, .gokin) AND any directory whose basename starts with `.`. Hidden FILES at any level are kept (`.gitignore`, `.env.example` etc. — users do want to reference those). Per-entry errors (permission denied, broken symlink) are swallowed: skip the offending path, keep walking. Hard cap at 5000 entries via early-stop sentinel so a monorepo doesn't blow up the bridge payload. Paths returned with forward slashes (cross-platform consistency for the @ syntax). Tests (6 in `file_suggest_test.go`): basic walk + sort; noise exclusion table covers all hardcoded dirs + hidden subdirs; root-level hidden files are kept; unknown projectID errors; empty projectID errors; empty dir returns empty list. Frontend: Wails JS/TS bindings hand-patched. ChatPanel.tsx: imports `ListProjectFiles`. New `projectFiles` cache (refreshed on `activeProjectId` change with a cancelled-flag guard against stale responses). New `atMention: { query, start } | null` state + `atMentionIdx`. The chat textarea's `onChange` runs a backwards scan from cursor: if the run from cursor back to a `@` contains only path-friendly chars (not space/newline/comma/etc), AND the `@` is at start-of-input or preceded by whitespace (avoids matching `addr@host` email-style), it sets `atMention`. Otherwise clears. `atMentionMatches` useMemo: empty query shows top 10 alphabetical files; non-empty does case-insensitive substring match capped at 10 results. Match-key reset useEffect resets `atMentionIdx` when the matches change. `applyAtMention(path)`: replaces the `@<query>` in the input with `@<full-path> ` (trailing space, cursor placed after) via `requestAnimationFrame` so React commits the new value first. Keyboard nav in `handleKeyDown` runs BEFORE the slash-command handler with priority: ↑↓ cycles selection, Enter/Tab applies, Esc closes. The popup renders ABOVE the chat-input-wrapper as a floating panel (rounded top, top-right hint "↑↓ Enter Esc", scrollable list, mouse-hover updates selection — matches Spotlight-style behavior). Mouse click also applies the selection. Reset on session switch via the existing reset useEffect. Help modal entry updated from "Reference a project file by path" to "Inline file picker — type to filter, ↑↓ Enter to insert" so the new behavior is documented. CSS: new `.atmention-popup` (panel-bg, rounded top, subtle shadow upward to show it's anchored to the input below) + `.atmention-header` (subtle bar with title + keyboard hint) + `.atmention-list` (scrollable max-height 280px) + `.atmention-item` (mono path text, accent background when selected with white inverted icon). Hooks audit: new state at lines ~129-131 (well before first early return at ~1134); new useEffect for `ListProjectFiles` after the snippets effect; new useMemo + useEffect for `atMentionMatches` / `atMatchKey` after the `slashMatchKey` block — all early. Studio test suite: **436 tests** (+6), coverage holds at **92.6%**. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean.

## What's Done (iter 530+)

- **Toast notifications for completion** -- new substantial UX feature. Complements the per-tab unread badge (iter 410+): the badge says "something happened over there" passively, the toast adds the "what happened" + click-to-jump for fast navigation. Specifically targets the focused-but-elsewhere case — OS notifications via `notifyIfBlurred` already cover the unfocused case from iter 410+. Frontend-only. New file `frontend/src/components/layout/ToastStack.tsx`: subscribes to `chat:complete` and `chat:error` events. Toast gating: skip if user is currently viewing this exact `(project, session)` AND window is focused (it'd be redundant); skip if window is unfocused (OS notification already fires); push otherwise. Each toast: project name + "finished"/"errored" status chip + 100-char preview from `data.text` (whitespace-collapsed), green ✓ for success / red ✗ for error. Stack max 3 visible (oldest dropped when over capacity), auto-dismiss after 5.5s, manual × close, click anywhere on toast → `setActiveProject` + `setActiveSession` + dispatches `gokin:switch-tab` to navigate. Newest-on-top via `flex-direction: column-reverse` so the most recent event is always visible. Pointer-events: none on the stack container so clicks pass through gaps; toasts override with pointer-events: auto. CSS animation: `toastSlideIn` (translateX from 20px to 0, opacity 0→1, 0.22s cubic-bezier ease) so they slide in from the right. Mounted in App.tsx between `<StatusBar />` and the tab context menu. Settings: new "Notifications" section with one toggle "Show completion toasts". Stored in `localStorage` under `gokin:toasts-enabled` (default `'1'`) — frontend-only preference, no backend round-trip needed. Setter dispatches `gokin:toasts-toggled` event so the live ToastStack picks up the change without remount. Component reads localStorage on mount + listens for the event to flip its own `enabled` state. Tests: no new backend tests (frontend-only, no changed code paths). Studio test suite: **436 tests**, coverage **92.6%**. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean.

## What's Done (iter 540+)

- **Drag-to-reorder session tabs** -- new substantial UX feature. Closes the longstanding "I want my main tab to stay where I put it" gap. Default ordering (lastUsedAt-desc + pinned-first) is sensible until the user has 5+ sessions and wants manual control. Now they can grab any tab and drag it to a new position; the order persists across restart. Backend (new file `internal/studio/session_order.go`): per-project JSON file at `~/.config/gokin-studio/session-order/<projectID>.json` — array of session IDs in user-preferred order. Three helpers (`loadSessionOrder`, `saveSessionOrder`, `removeProjectSessionOrder`) follow the iter 480+ session_pins.go pattern: empty input removes the file, corrupt JSON treated as empty, atomic writes. New `(*Studio).ReorderChatSessions(projectID, orderedIDs []string) error` Wails method validates project + filters IDs against the live session map (drops unknown IDs and dedups repeats — defends against frontend race conditions where a deleted session ID still appears in a drag). `ListChatSessions` sort comparator extended: pinned-first still wins (iter 480+ behavior preserved), but within each pin group, the user-defined order array now takes precedence over `LastUsedAt`. Sort priority: pinned vs unpinned → user-order index (both in array sort by index, only-one-in-array wins, neither falls through) → LastUsedAt-desc → "default" stable last → CreatedAt asc. `RemoveProject` cleans up the order file via `removeProjectSessionOrder(id)`. Tests (7 in `session_order_test.go`): basic reorder + ListChatSessions reflects the change; pinned still wins over explicit order; unknown/ghost IDs filtered out; repeated IDs deduped; empty projectID + unknown project rejected (empty array valid — clears the order); file save/load round-trip; RemoveProject cleans up. Frontend: Wails JS/TS bindings hand-patched. App.tsx: new `draggingTabId` and `dropTargetId` state. Tab buttons gain `draggable={!isRenaming}` + `onDragStart` (sets draggingId, marks `effectAllowed='move'`, sets text/plain data for Firefox compat) + `onDragEnd` (clears state) + `onDragOver` (preventDefault to enable drop, sets `dropEffect='move'`, marks dropTargetId) + `onDragLeave` (clears dropTargetId only when leaving the tab proper, not just a child element via `currentTarget.contains(relatedTarget)` check) + `onDrop` (computes new order via splice-from-source-index + splice-to-target-index, updates local sessions state, calls `ReorderChatSessions` with the full ID array). Drop semantics: dragged tab inserts BEFORE the target (matches "drop indicator on left edge" mental model). Visual states via new CSS classes `is-dragging` (45% opacity, deeper background) and `drop-target` (3px accent-color inset shadow on left edge as a drop indicator). Cursor: `grab` when draggable, `grabbing` while pressed. Renaming inputs disable dragging. ReorderChatSessions failures logged but the local order stays — backend will catch up on next save. Studio test suite: **443 tests** (+7), coverage **92.6%**. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean.

## What's Done (iter 550+)

- **Session export/import as JSON** -- new substantial feature for backup, sharing, and migration. The existing Markdown export is good for human reading but loses structure (role tags, usage stats, lineage); JSON preserves everything that can survive a save/load cycle. Backend (new file `internal/studio/session_export.go`): `SessionExportEnvelope{Version int, ExportedAt int64, Name string, ParentID string, Usage *SessionUsage, Entries []HistoryEntry}` — versioned envelope so future format changes are detectable. New `(*Studio).ExportSessionJSON(projectID, sessionID) (string, error)` snapshots the session under read lock, strips thinking parts (matching `saveHistoryFull` semantics — reasoning tokens shouldn't bleed into transferable formats), pretty-prints to JSON. New `(*Studio).ImportSessionJSON(projectID, jsonBlob) (*ChatSessionInfo, error)` validates payload (rejects empty/whitespace/invalid-JSON/version-too-new), generates a fresh 8-char session ID (no collision with the source's), bumps `lastUsedAt` so the imported tab lands at the top of the recent-first sidebar, persists via `SaveHistoryWithMetadata`. Name handling: empty → "Imported chat" fallback; non-empty gets " (imported)" suffix unless it already contains "imported"; capped at 60 chars. Parent ID intentionally NOT preserved on import — the source session likely doesn't exist in the target project, so the lineage link would be broken. Tests (7 in `session_export_test.go`): basic export + version field + name + entry count + exportedAt populated; thinking parts stripped from the export; validation (empty/unknown projectID/unknown session, empty sessionID defaults to "default"); import round-trip (export then import in same project — fresh ID, "imported" suffix, history preserved); import validation (empty/unknown project, empty/whitespace/invalid JSON, future version); minimal payload (no name, no entries) produces usable session with fallback name; persists to disk for restart survival. Frontend: Wails JS/TS bindings hand-patched. ChatPanel.tsx: imports `ExportSessionJSON` + `ImportSessionJSON`. New `showImport`, `importDraft`, `importBusy`, `importError` state. New `handleExportJSON()` reuses the existing Blob+download flow (filename includes session name when human-readable, else session ID prefix). Two new menu items between "Export all sessions" and "Summarize session": "Export as JSON" with `Download` icon, "Import session…" with rotated `Download` icon (looks like upload). Import modal: 600px wide with paste textarea, Ctrl+Enter to submit / Esc to cancel, busy spinner on the Import button, inline red error banner on failure. On success: `setActiveSession` + `gokin:switch-tab` event navigates to the new session; `gokin:sessions-changed` event tells App.tsx to reload its tab list. Reset on session switch via the existing reset useEffect. Added to global Esc handler with explicit `import-backdrop` class. CSS: full new `.import-modal`/`.import-textarea`/`.import-error`/`.import-actions` block (panel-bg, blur backdrop, monospace textarea for JSON readability, italic action hint footer). Studio test suite: **450 tests** (+7), coverage **92.6%**. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean.

## What's Done (iter 560+)

- **Project export/import as JSON** -- extends iter 550+ to the project level. Bundles project metadata (name, system prompt, provider, model, temperature, maxTokens, budget) AND every session into one JSON envelope. Useful for full project backup, machine migration, or sharing a project setup with a teammate. Backend (new file `internal/studio/project_export.go`): `ProjectExportEnvelope` wraps version + exportedAt + project fields + `[]SessionExportEnvelope`. The project Directory is intentionally NOT serialized — paths don't transfer between machines, so the importer picks a fresh one. New `(*Studio).ExportProjectJSON(projectID) (string, error)` snapshots project state under p.mu.RLock, then per-session under each sess.mu.RLock (releases project lock first to avoid lock-order issues), strips thinking parts identically to per-session export. New `(*Studio).ImportProjectJSON(jsonBlob, directory) (*ProjectInfo, error)` validates payload (rejects empty/invalid-JSON/version-too-new/oversize via new `ImportPayloadMaxBytes = 5 MB` constant), reuses `AddProject` for the heavy lifting (id gen, default-session creation, duplicate-directory rejection — important so imports can't silently overwrite an existing project), then applies project-level settings (`SetProjectSystemPrompt`/`SetProjectProvider`/`SetProjectModelParams`/`SetProjectBudget` — failures non-fatal so partial config still produces a usable project), then restores each session via `ImportSessionJSON` (reusing the same name-suffix + lineage-drop semantics from iter 550+). Name fallback: empty → "Imported project"; capped at 60 chars; "(imported)" suffix added unless already present. Tests (6 in `project_export_test.go`): basic export structure (version + name + systemPrompt + sessions[]); validation (empty/unknown projectID); round-trip (export → import to fresh dir → at least 2 sessions appear, system prompt preserved, "imported" suffix on at least one session name, directory matches target); import validation (empty/whitespace/invalid JSON, empty target dir, future version, oversize payload); duplicate-directory rejection (target dir already has another registered project); minimal envelope (no name, no sessions) produces usable project with fallback name. **Bug fix**: iter 550+ session-import textarea was missing `maxLength` cap and the backend `ImportSessionJSON` had no payload-size guard — both could OOM the process on a malformed paste. Frontend now caps at 5 MB; backend rejects symmetrically via the new `ImportPayloadMaxBytes` constant. Frontend: Wails JS/TS bindings hand-patched. Sidebar.tsx: imports `Download` + `Upload` icons + `ExportProjectJSON` + `ImportProjectJSON`. Project right-click context menu gets a new "Export project as JSON" item between Pin and Delete (uses `Download` icon, triggers Blob+download with sanitized filename). New "Import project from JSON" button (`Upload` icon) in the sidebar header next to Settings + Add Project. Import modal pattern matches iter 550+ session import: paste textarea + dir input with `BrowseDirectory` button + Cancel/Import actions + inline error. On success: `addProjectToStore(info)` + `setActiveProject(info.id)` for instant navigation. Studio test suite: **456 tests** (+6), coverage **92.6%**. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean.

## What's Done (iter 570+)

- **Sound notification on completion (Web Audio synthesized chime)** -- new opt-in feature. Some users want an audio cue when an agent finishes a long-running turn — especially with `quietMode` on and the window in the background. Frontend-only, no audio asset file. ToastStack.tsx (iter 530+) gains a lazy-init `audioCtx: AudioContext | null` and a `playCompletionSound(kind: 'success'|'error')` helper. The chime is two sine-wave notes via `OscillatorNode` + `GainNode` envelope: success = upward arpeggio (660 Hz → 880 Hz), error = downward dip (440 Hz → 330 Hz). Each note has a 10ms attack + exponential decay so the tone doesn't pop at start/end. Peak gain capped at 0.12 — noticeable on headphones but not jarring. The `AudioContext` is lazily constructed (no resource claim until first sound) and resumed if browser suspends it (modern browsers require a user gesture before audio plays — by the time a toast fires, the user has clicked through the app multiple times so this works). Try/catch around the whole helper means audio failures are silent fallback. Sound playback gated independently of toast visibility: `soundEnabledRef` (useRef so the value is read at push-time without re-render churn) reads `localStorage['gokin:sound-on-complete']` (default OFF — sounds are intrusive) and listens for `gokin:sound-toggled` window event so the user can flip the setting without remount. Triggered only inside the existing toast `push()` function, after the same focused-but-elsewhere gate fires — sound fires together with the toast, never alone. Settings page: new "Play chime on completion" toggle in the Notifications section, persisted to `localStorage['gokin:sound-on-complete']` (default '0'/off), dispatches `gokin:sound-toggled` on flip. Studio test suite: **456 tests**, coverage **92.6%**. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean.

## What's Done (iter 580+)

- **Per-project notification mute** -- new UX feature for users with many projects. Adds a third "silence axis" on top of iter 410+ unread badges + iter 530+ toasts + iter 570+ chime: now any specific project can be muted entirely so its events don't generate any of those signals. Useful when an "experimental" or "archived" project shouldn't ping while the user is focused on something else. Frontend-only via `localStorage['gokin:muted-projects']` (JSON array of project IDs). New utility file `frontend/src/lib/mutedProjects.ts` with module-level `Set<string>` cache, lazy load + idempotent `ensureListener` that subscribes to BOTH the native `storage` event (for cross-window sync) AND the custom `gokin:muted-changed` event (for same-window sync — `storage` doesn't fire on the originating tab). Three exports: `isProjectMuted(id)` (reads cache, no IO), `toggleProjectMute(id)` (flips state, persists, returns new state, dispatches event), `unmuteProject(id)` (idempotent removal, called from the project-delete handler so a re-added project at the same ID doesn't inherit prior mute state). Empty-set persistence removes the localStorage key rather than storing `[]` to keep storage clean. Wired into ToastStack.tsx push() — early-return before toast push and sound playback if `isProjectMuted(data.projectID)`. Wired into useWailsEvents.ts `chat:complete` handler — added to the same condition that bumps `unread` so the badge stays at zero too. Sidebar.tsx: imports + uses `Volume2`/`VolumeX` icons. New `muteTick` state with `gokin:muted-changed` listener forces a re-render when mute set changes (otherwise the icons don't update without a project store touch). Project right-click context menu gains a new "Mute notifications" / "Unmute notifications" item between Pin and Export, with the icon and label flipping based on current state. `RemoveProject` handler calls `unmuteProject(id)` so deleted projects don't leak mute state. Inline `VolumeX` icon (muted gray, opacity 0.7 — reads as "off" rather than as a status badge competing for attention) shown next to muted project names. Tooltip on the project-name div lists active states ("Pinned to top · Notifications muted · Double-click to rename") joined with bullets. Help modal "Right-click project" row updated to mention mute. CSS: new `.project-mute-icon` block (mirrors `.project-pin-icon` shape but uses muted color). Studio test suite unchanged: **456 tests**, coverage **92.6%**. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean.

## What's Done (iter 590+)

- **Bug fix: ImportProjectJSON empty default session leak + new slash commands** -- audit pass on iter 560+ found a real defect: `ImportProjectJSON` reused `AddProject` which always creates an empty "Chat 1" default session, so importing a project with N sessions resulted in N+1 sessions (the AddProject default + N imports), with the user staring at an empty placeholder tab they didn't ask for. Fix: track `importedCount` in the import loop; if ≥1 sessions were imported AND the default session is still empty (`!hasUserMessage(history)`), call `DeleteChatSession(info.ID, "default")` after the loop. Safe because we only delete when at least one imported session exists — `DeleteChatSession`'s last-session-guard wouldn't fire. Updated `TestImportProjectJSON_RoundTrip` to assert exactly 1 session (was ≥2) and that no session has ID "default" post-import. Extended `TestImportProjectJSON_FreshDefaults` to confirm the OPPOSITE branch: when the envelope is empty (no sessions to import), the default IS preserved (otherwise the project would have zero sessions and the user couldn't open a chat). Added `TestImportProjectJSON_DeletesDefaultAfterImport` as a focused regression. Bonus: two new slash commands surfaced for iter 550+ session export/import — `/exportjson` calls the existing `handleExportJSON` to download the current session as JSON, `/importsession` opens the existing import modal. Discoverability win — users who don't know about the `…` menu items can find them via the slash autocomplete. Studio test suite: **457 tests** (+1 net — one new test, and adapted the existing round-trip test rather than removing it), coverage **92.6%**. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean.

## What's Done (iter 600+)

- **Project usage CSV export** -- small but useful polish on iter 360+ usage stats. Power users tracking spend in spreadsheets need a structured download — JSON is too verbose for spreadsheet tools, the in-app modal isn't shareable. Backend (new file `internal/studio/usage_csv.go`): `(*Studio).ExportProjectUsageCSV(projectID) (string, error)` reuses `ProjectUsageStats` and emits CSV with columns `Session, Cost (USD), Input tokens, Output tokens, Cache tokens, Turns, Last turn`. Per-session rows match the modal's per-row breakdown; a final "TOTAL" row at the bottom mirrors the totals strip. Cost formatted with `fmtCurrency` helper at 4-decimal precision (kept trailing zeros for visual alignment, no `$` prefix so spreadsheets can sort numerically — header makes the unit clear). Last turn rendered as RFC3339 timestamp when populated, empty cell otherwise (never-used sessions). Encoded via `encoding/csv` so commas/quotes in session names are properly escaped. Tests (5 in `usage_csv_test.go`): basic shape (header + at least one session row + TOTAL row); per-session usage data flows through (cost, tokens, ISO timestamp); validation (empty/unknown projectID); never-used session shows zero values + empty Last-turn cell; `fmtCurrency` table covers integer + decimal + rounding cases. Frontend: Wails JS/TS bindings hand-patched. ChatPanel.tsx imports `ExportProjectUsageCSV`. ## What's Done (iter 610+)

- **Cost alert toast at budget thresholds** -- closes a real UX gap with iter 400+ per-project budget. Currently the chat header chip just colors amber at 80% / red at 100% — but if the user isn't looking at the header (or has the project muted), they sail past these thresholds without noticing. New toast notification fires once per threshold per budget cycle, with persistent state in `localStorage` so it doesn't re-fire on every restart and the spam stays bounded. ChatPanel.tsx: new useEffect deps on `[projectTotalCostUSD, activeProject?.budgetUSD, activeProjectId, activeProject, currentSessionId]`. Computes pct from current `projectTotalCostUSD` / `budgetUSD`, reads/writes `localStorage[gokin:budget-alerts-<projectID>]` storing `{budget: number, alerted: number[]}`. Smart reset: when stored.budget !== current budget, reset alerted=[] (a re-set budget gets a fresh alerting cycle — user just changed it intentionally). For each threshold (80, 100): if pct ≥ threshold AND not in alerted set, dispatches `gokin:budget-alert` window event with detail `{projectID, sessionID, projectName, threshold, total, budget}` and adds the threshold to alerted. ToastStack.tsx: new "warning" ToastKind variant. The budget-alert handler is intentionally separate from the regular `chat:complete`/`chat:error` paths because budget alerts BYPASS the focused-but-elsewhere gate — the user explicitly opted into budget tracking, so they want to see the alert immediately even on the source project. Inline-construct the toast (no `push()` call) and add a longer dwell (9000 ms vs the regular 5500) since the alert is more actionable — the user might want to read the numbers carefully before it fades. Toast renders with `AlertTriangle` icon, "budget alert" status chip, preview "Spent $X.XX of $Y.YY budget — N% reached.". CSS: new `.toast-warning` variant with amber 3px left border + amber icon color (matches the chip's amber-at-80% state for visual continuity). Studio test suite unchanged: **462 tests**, coverage **92.6%**. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean.

## What's Done (iter 620+)

- **Bug fix: per-project localStorage cleanup on project delete** -- audit pass found a real leak. Two recent features stored per-project preferences in `localStorage` keys: `gokin:quietmode:<pid>` (iter 510+) and `gokin:budget-alerts-<pid>` (iter 610+). Neither was cleaned up when the project was deleted. A long-running install with 100+ added/removed projects would slowly accumulate dead keys, eventually risking the browser's per-origin localStorage cap (5-10 MB on most platforms). Fix: new `clearProjectLocalStorage(projectID)` helper in `lib/mutedProjects.ts` (siblings of the existing `unmuteProject` cleanup helper) that drops both per-project key patterns. Sidebar.tsx `handleRemove` now calls `clearProjectLocalStorage(id)` alongside the existing `unmuteProject(id)` (which handles the third per-project key, the muted-set entry). Per-project keys cleaned up explicitly enumerated in the helper's docstring so future per-project preferences can register themselves there. Audit also confirmed all backend per-project files have cleanup hooks: `session-pins/<pid>.json` via `removeProjectSessionPins` (iter 480+), `session-order/<pid>.json` via `removeProjectSessionOrder` (iter 540+), `pins/<pid>_<sid>.json` via `removeProjectPins` (iter 300+), `drafts/<pid>_<sid>.txt` via `removeProjectDrafts` (iter 270+), `history/<pid>_<sid>.json` via the per-session `DeleteHistory` calls during `RemoveProject`. Same audit confirmed the `chatStore.dropProject(pid)` prefix-match cleanup covers all per-chat-key state including `unread`. Studio test suite unchanged: **462 tests**, coverage **92.6%**. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean.

## What's Done (iter 630+)

- **Settings → Reset preferences button** -- new escape hatch for users who want to clear all frontend-only state and start fresh. Useful after upgrades that change defaults, when recovering from inconsistent state, or simply when handing the app to a new user. Frontend-only — backend state (projects, sessions, history, snippets, all the persisted Wails files) is intentionally left untouched. New `resetAllPreferences()` helper in `lib/mutedProjects.ts` enumerates ALL `gokin:*` keys via `localStorage.key(i)` prefix-scan (so future preferences auto-clear without per-key list maintenance), removes them, resets the in-memory mute cache to an empty Set, dispatches the three live-update events (`gokin:toasts-toggled`, `gokin:sound-toggled`, `gokin:muted-changed`) so subscribed components re-read defaults without a window reload. Returns the count of removed keys for the success toast. Settings page: new "Reset preferences" section just above the About block. Two-stage UI prevents accidents — first click shows a red-tinted inline confirmation block ("This will clear toasts, sound, mute, quiet-mode, and budget-alert state..."), second click on the red Reset button performs the operation. Local component state (toastsEnabled / soundEnabled) is also reset alongside the localStorage clear so the toggles in the same Notifications section visibly flip back to defaults. Success feedback: green inline toast for 6s showing how many keys were cleared. CSS: new `.reset-prefs-confirm` block (red-tinted background + border, flex column layout with action buttons right-aligned). Studio test suite unchanged: **462 tests**, coverage **92.6%**. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean.
