## What's Done (iter 650+)

- **Bug fix: drag-to-reorder couldn't drop at end of tab list** -- iter 540+ drag-to-reorder uses "drop on tab N puts dragged BEFORE N" semantics. That meant there was no way to move a tab to the very end of the list — dropping on the last tab puts the dragged before it, never after. Fix: new "drop-at-end" zone (`tab-drop-end`) rendered just after the new-chat (+) button only while a drag is in progress (so it doesn't take static visual space). 6px-wide vertical strip that highlights with the accent color on hover. New `dropAtEnd` state + onDragOver/onDragLeave/onDrop handlers. On drop: splice the dragged tab out and `push` it to the end of the array, then call `ReorderChatSessions` with the new order. Skips the splice if the tab is already at the end (idempotent). The existing per-tab `onDragEnd` handler now also clears `dropAtEnd`. CSS: `.tab-drop-end` invisible by default + `.active` variant turns the strip accent-colored. The drop zone uses `align-self: center` and 28px height so it visually matches the tab height. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean. Studio test suite unchanged: **471 tests**, coverage **92.0%**.

## What's Done (iter 660+)

- **Alt+1..9 direct session-jump keyboard shortcuts** -- power-user navigation for users with many tabs. Currently navigation between sessions was Ctrl+PgUp/PgDn (cycles by one) or click. Now Alt+N jumps directly to session N in the current tab order. Frontend-only. App.tsx `handleKeyDown` callback gains a new branch outside the existing `if (e.ctrlKey || e.metaKey)` block — `if (e.altKey && !e.ctrlKey && !e.metaKey && !e.shiftKey && !isInput)` reads `parseInt(e.key, 10)` and if `1 <= n <= 9`, sets the view to `sessions[n-1].id` via `setSessions((list) => { ... return list })` (using the functional updater so we read the live list without depending on the latest state in the closure). Skip when input is focused — Alt+number can be a system shortcut on some platforms (eg. Alt+1 = bookmark on Linux Firefox); we don't want to steal during text entry. No-op when `n > sessions.length` (the target is null, view stays). Help modal Navigate section gains a new row "Alt+1 … Alt+9 — Jump directly to session N in the tab order". Settings shortcuts grid gains the same row right after Ctrl+1. Studio test suite unchanged: **471 tests**, coverage **92.0%**. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean.

## What's Done (iter 670+)

- **Bug fix: budget alerts ignored project mute** -- audit found that iter 610+ budget threshold alerts didn't respect iter 580+ per-project mute. A user could mute a project ("I don't want anything from this project pinging me right now") and still get budget alert toasts. Fix has two parts: (1) **ToastStack defense-in-depth**: the `gokin:budget-alert` handler now does `if (isProjectMuted(detail.projectID)) return` before constructing the toast — even if a stale dispatcher fires the event, the user sees nothing. (2) **ChatPanel dispatcher early-exit**: the budget threshold useEffect skips the entire alert flow when `isProjectMuted(activeProjectId)` returns true. Critically, this skips the *persistence* too — the `alerted` array in `localStorage[gokin:budget-alerts-<pid>]` is NOT updated. That way, when the user un-mutes the project later, the next chat:complete that fires the effect will see the threshold isn't in the alerted set and properly deliver the (now-unmuted) alert. Without this, muting → threshold crosses → unmute → user is permanently blind to the threshold for the current budget cycle. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean. Studio test suite unchanged: **471 tests**, coverage **92.0%**.

## What's Done (iter 680+)

- **Bug fix: OS notifications ignored project mute** -- continuing the iter 670+ mute audit. Two more notification paths weren't gated: (1) `chat:complete` triggers `notifyIfBlurred` (the OS-level toast that fires when the window is unfocused) which would still fire for muted projects — exactly what the user said NO to; (2) `dispatch:complete` fires the same path with a different title ("Dispatch to <name> finished"), also unguarded. Fix: wrap both `notifyIfBlurred` calls in `if (!isProjectMuted(...))`. For dispatch, the gate uses the SOURCE project (`data.from`) — that's the project the user originated the dispatch from, so muting the SOURCE silences its dispatch notifications. Now the mute coverage is comprehensive: unread badges, in-app toasts, completion chime, budget alert toasts, AND OS-level notifications all respect mute. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean. Studio test suite unchanged: **471 tests**, coverage **92.0%**.

Now also includes `provider` and `model` so users can type "kimi" to find all Kimi-backed projects, "glm-5.1" to find specific model versions, "ollama" to find all local-LLM projects. Both filter sites updated (the visible-list filter + the "X of Y" footer counter). (2) **Token estimate in chat input footer**: the existing char count was useful but didn't translate to the LLM's actual unit of cost/context. Format: "12,345 chars · ~3,087 tokens". Helps users gauge "is this paste going to blow my context window?" before pressing Enter. Frontend build clean (2,111 modules), TypeScript clean. ## What's Done (iter 700+)

- **Release-ready diagnostics + version constant** -- new substantial feature for release readiness. Before this, version was hardcoded as "v1.0" string in the About panel with no way for users (or support) to inspect the running build, no health checks to detect common misconfiguration, and no copyable diagnostic report for bug submissions. Now there's a proper `Version` constant in Go (`1.0.0`), a `BuildInfo` struct (version + Go runtime + OS/arch/CPU count), and a full `DiagnosticsInfo` report with health checks across 4 categories: config (writability), providers (per-provider API key + Ollama URL presence), projects (working directory still exists), data (stale replay logs older than 7 days). Backend (new file `internal/studio/diagnostics.go`): `Version` const + `BuildInfo` struct + `HealthCheck` struct (Category/Name/Status/Message/Detail) + `DiagnosticsInfo` (build + config dir + totals + checks). Three Wails methods: `GetBuildInfo()` (cheap, runtime info only), `GetDiagnostics()` (read-only walk over config dir + all projects + history dir, ~few hundred ms on a large config), `DiagnosticsReport()` (renders the info as plain-text suitable for copy/paste into a support ticket). Health check categories: writability via sentinel-file probe; per-provider check only fires for providers that actually have a project using them (don't nag about missing GLM key if user only uses Kimi); Ollama URL falls back to `localhost:11434` if unset, warning-but-not-error; project directories use `os.Stat`; stale replays older than 7 days warn (safe to leave, auto-clean when next session completes). The report dispatcher uses categories and statuses (ok/warn/error) so the frontend can render with color-coded left borders. Frontend: imports `GetBuildInfo`/`GetDiagnostics`/`DiagnosticsReport`. `SettingsPage` About section now reads real version from `BuildInfo` (replaces hardcoded "v1.0" → "v1.0.0") + shows OS/arch/Go-version/CPU subtitle. New "Run diagnostics" button opens a modal showing: 8-cell stats grid (Version/Go/Platform/CPUs/Projects/Sessions/Config size/History size); config path; full health-check list with color-coded left borders (green=ok, amber=warn, red=error); three action buttons (Refresh, Copy report, Save as file). Copy uses `navigator.clipboard.writeText` with `DiagnosticsReport`'s plaintext output. Save-as-file builds the same plaintext client-side from the loaded `DiagnosticsInfo` (no extra disk walks) and triggers a Blob+download with filename `gokin-diagnostics-<YYYY-MM-DD>.txt`. Escape handler closes modal with IME guard. Tests (18 in `diagnostics_test.go`): `GetBuildInfo` verifies all fields match runtime; `GetDiagnostics_BasicShape` (non-nil + populated); `GetDiagnostics_TotalsReflectProjects` (2 projects + extra sessions counted correctly); `checkConfigDirWritable` paths (writable/missing/is-file); `checkAPIKeys` (GLM error when missing key for project using it, OK when set, Ollama URL warn when unset, OK when set); `checkProjectDirs_Missing` (deleted directory flagged); `countStaleReplays` (8-day-old replay detected, fresh ones excluded); `DiagnosticsReport_ContainsKeyFields` (version/Go-runtime/Storage/Health-checks/[OK] all present); `humanBytes` table; `inspectConfigDir` writable/missing paths; `inspectHistoryDir` returns correct totals + stale count. Diagnostics coverage: `GetBuildInfo` and `GetDiagnostics` 100%, `countStaleReplays`/`dirSize`/`humanBytes` 100%, all health-check functions 83-94%. Studio test suite: **489 tests** (+18), coverage holds at **92.0%**. Frontend build clean (2,113 modules), TypeScript clean.

## What's Done (iter 710+)

- **Application event log + viewer** -- closes the "what just happened?" gap for backend errors. Before this, when the agent recovered from a panic, a config save failed, or a chat:retry fired, the only trace was stderr — invisible to anyone running the app from a launcher. Now there's a bounded ring buffer of recent events surfaced through a dedicated Logs modal. Backend (new file `internal/studio/event_log.go`): `EventLogCapacity = 500` cap on entries (older overwritten on wrap so memory stays bounded), `EventLogEntry{TimestampMs, Level, Source, Message}`. `EventLog` struct with `Log/Snapshot/Clear/Len` methods, all thread-safe via internal sync.RWMutex. Message truncation at 2 KB with ellipsis suffix so a runaway error chain can't bloat memory. Level normalization: "info", "warn", "error" pass through; anything else (including uppercase "INFO") becomes "info". Snapshot returns entries in chronological order with proper wrap-around handling — the slice is a copy so callers can mutate or hand it to Wails without re-locking. Nil-receiver safe (all methods early-return on nil) so tests using bare `&EventLog{}` zero-values don't panic. Three Wails methods on Studio: `LogEvent(level, source, message)`, `GetRecentLogs() []EventLogEntry`, `ClearLogs()`. Lazy-init via `sync.Once` (not s.mu — that would deadlock since `saveConfig` already holds s.mu when it calls `s.logf` on failure), so tests doing `&Studio{}` work too. Auto-instrumented at three error sites: `saveConfig` failure → `error`/`config`; `saveConfigAsync` failure → `error`/`config`; and a generic tee in `Project.emitEvent` that captures every `chat:error` (level=`error`/`agent`) and `chat:retry` (level=`warn`/`agent`) event — so the full picture of agent failures lands in the log without each emit site needing explicit log calls. `summarizeEventForLog` extracts the human-readable text from `ChatTextEvent` or generic map payloads. Recovery panic-barrier guard around the tee call so a log failure can't crash the caller. Frontend (`SettingsPage.tsx`): imports `GetRecentLogs/ClearLogs`. New "View logs" button in the Diagnostics modal action bar opens the dedicated Logs modal. State: `showLogs`, `logs`, `logFilter` ('all'/'info'/'warn'/'error'), `logsLoading`. Filter chips at the top show live counts per level ("All (N)", "Info (N)", "Warn (N)", "Error (N)") with the active filter outlined in accent/amber/red. Newest-first ordering (reverse of the chronological snapshot) so the most recent issue is at the top. Each row: colored left border (gray=info, amber=warn, red=error), small uppercase level chip, monospace source tag, right-aligned local time, monospace message body with word-break. Empty state distinguishes "no events yet" from "no events at this level". Refresh + Clear actions; Esc closes; explicit `logs-backdrop` class. Tests (16 in `event_log_test.go`): append/snapshot order; level normalization (uppercase/unknown/empty → "info"); message truncation at 2 KB with UTF-8-aware ellipsis byte calculation; ring buffer wrap-around (550 events → buffer holds exactly 500, oldest is event-50, newest is event-549); Clear resets state and allows new writes after; Len at capacity reports cap (not raw write count); nil-receiver safety for all four methods; concurrent writes from 10 goroutines × 100 events; TimestampMs populated within `[before, after]` bounds; Studio `LogEvent`/`GetRecentLogs`/`ClearLogs` end-to-end; lazy-init from bare `&Studio{}` works; `logf` formats with `fmt.Sprintf`; `summarizeEventForLog` for `ChatTextEvent`, generic `map[string]any` with text/message/error keys, and unknown-type fallback. All event_log.go functions at **100%** coverage. Studio test suite: **505 tests** (+16), coverage rises to **92.1%** (+0.1pp). Frontend build clean (2,113 modules), TypeScript clean.

## What's Done (iter 1222+ — streamRequest: capped Retry-After + rune-safe reason truncation, ported from gokin)

- **Uncapped Retry-After** (`internal/engine/client/anthropic.go`): `streamRequest` honoured `httpErr.RetryAfter` unconditionally when it exceeded the computed backoff. A misbehaving provider sending an absurd value (e.g. a unix timestamp like `1735689600s ≈ 55 years`) would bypass `maxDelay` entirely and park the client indefinitely. Studio already had `cappedRetryDelay` in `retry.go` (added in v0.85.15) but wasn't calling it here. Fixed by: (1) adding `maxRetryAfter := 2 * time.Minute` constant to the function (matching gokin); (2) replacing the inline `if httpErr.RetryAfter > delay { delay = ... }` with `delay = cappedRetryDelay(delay, httpErr.RetryAfter, maxRetryAfter)`.
- **Byte-slice reason truncation** (same function): the retry-reason string emitted to `StatusCallback.OnRetry` was truncated with `reason[:47]` (byte index). A Cyrillic/CJK error message at the 47-byte boundary would split a multi-byte rune and produce invalid UTF-8 in the UI. Fixed to `[]rune(reason)` rune-safe slicing, matching gokin.
- **Tests** (`retry_test.go`, 2 additions): `TestStreamRequestRetryReasonTruncation` — verifies correct truncation for ASCII (no truncation under 50 runes), long ASCII, and long Cyrillic (validates `utf8.ValidString` on the result); `TestCappedRetryDelay` was already present and covers the fix. Full `-race ./internal/engine/client/` green.
- **Honest value**: (1) The Retry-After cap prevents a provider bug or intentional misbehavior from locking up the client for hours or days — `cappedRetryDelay` was already there for exactly this purpose but unused in the hottest retry path. (2) Rune-safe truncation ensures the retry banner in the UI always shows valid text for non-ASCII error messages from GLM/Kimi.

## What's Done (iter 1221+ — GLM error classification + auto-retry, ported from gokin)

- **`classifyGLMErrorCode`** (`anthropic.go`): ported from gokin. Z.AI/GLM returns structured errors inside HTTP-200 SSE events (`{error:{code,message}}`). Previously, studio emitted a generic `API error (code): message` string with no retry signal, so transient GLM errors (rate limit 1210, concurrency 1301, throughput 1302/1303, overloaded 1305) were silently dropped and the agent turn failed. Now: `classifyGLMErrorCode` maps known codes to (retryable, keyword, description); the SSE handler embeds keyword (`"rate limit"`, `"overloaded"`) into the error text so `isRetryableError`'s substring check fires, enabling auto-retry; also calls `statusCb.OnError` for rate-limit UI feedback. Non-retryable codes (1211/1213 balance, 1212 quota, 1214/1215 auth) get user-friendly messages ("GLM account balance insufficient — top up or switch provider") instead of raw numeric codes.
- **Tests** (`glm_error_test.go`, new file, 3 tests): `TestClassifyGLMErrorCode` (12 cases: all known codes + unknown fallbacks), `TestClassifyGLMErrorCode_ErrorTextContainsKeyword` (verifies retryable errText includes the keyword for `isRetryableError`), `TestClassifyGLMErrorCode_UnknownCodeFallback` (raw message and "GLM error N" fallbacks).
- Full `-race ./internal/engine/client/ ./internal/studio/` green.
- **Honest value**: GLM (the default provider) uses HTTP-200 SSE errors for rate limiting and overload — the most common transient error class. Studio was not retrying these; every GLM 1305 "overloaded" during a long tool chain caused the turn to fail silently. With this fix, all 5 retryable GLM error codes trigger auto-retry with the same 3× exponential backoff that HTTP 429s already get.

## What's Done (iter 1219+ — thinking support helpers + model registry tests, ported from gokin)

- **`SupportsKimiThinking` + `SupportsDeepSeekThinking`** (`internal/engine/client/factory.go`): extracted as exported helpers (ported from gokin upstream). `SupportsKimiThinking` returns true for `kimi-for-coding` and `kimi-k2*` prefix; `SupportsDeepSeekThinking` returns true for `deepseek-v4*`, `deepseek-reasoner`, and bare `deepseek`, false for `deepseek-chat`. Case-insensitive prefix matching covers future model variants. Previously this logic was hardcoded inline in `project.go::initClient`.

This extends thinking auto-enable to **DeepSeek V4 Flash** (previously only V4 Pro got it; Flash also confirmed to accept thinking on the Anthropic-compat endpoint per gokin April 2026 testing).
- **Tests** (`factory_provider_test.go`, new file, 5 tests):
  - `TestSupportsKimiThinking` — 9 model cases, case-insensitive prefix matching.
  - `TestSupportsDeepSeekThinking` — 10 model cases including deepseek-chat (false) and V4 Flash (true).
  - `TestAnthropicClient_SupportsPromptCachingForDeepSeek` — pins the prompt-caching gate for DeepSeek, Kimi, MiniMax, Anthropic, GLM/Z.AI (off), and unknown providers (off).

(2) `SupportsPromptCaching` regression guard catches the specific case where DeepSeek caching is accidentally disabled (costs 95% more per turn on repeat prefixes). (3) Provider defaults + preset consistency tests catch `AvailableModels` drift before it hits users as "model not found" errors.

## What's Done (iter 1216+ — unsigned thinking drop + tool_result ordering, ported from gokin)

- **`buildAssistantMessage` unsigned-thinking drop** (`anthropic.go`): studio previously emitted thinking blocks without a `signature` field when `ThoughtSignature` was empty. Unsigned thinking can't be round-tripped to strict providers (Kimi, Anthropic native) and is noise for permissive ones (GLM). Now the function checks `len(part.ThoughtSignature) == 0` and skips with a `logging.Warn` — exactly matching gokin's behaviour. Signed thinking (`ThoughtSignature != nil`) continues to be emitted with its `signature` field. Existing tests (stub injection for legacy history, signed thinking with tool_use, double-placeholder guard) all unaffected.
- **`orderToolResultsFirst` + `mergeConsecutiveMessages` update** (`anthropic.go`): ported `orderToolResultsFirst` from gokin — reorders user message content so `tool_result` blocks precede text blocks. Applied inside `mergeConsecutiveMessages` after each same-role merge AND in a final pass over all user messages. Previously, when a user turn in history contained notification text (e.g. `[System: reuse tool results]`) and pending tool results were appended after it, `mergeConsecutiveMessages` produced `[text, tool_result]` — which causes 400 validation errors on strict providers. Now the order is always `[tool_result, …text]`.
- **Tests** (`anthropic_history_test.go`, new file, 4 tests): ported from gokin `anthropic_history_test.go`:
  - `SkipsUnsignedThinkingOnlyAssistantTurns` — history turn with only unsigned thinking is excluded from messages (already handled by `canSerialiseAssistantForProvider`; guards the path).
  - `DropsUnsignedThinkingButKeepsToolUse` — unsigned thinking dropped; tool_use preserved in the same message.
  - `EmitsSignedThinkingBeforeToolUse` — signed thinking appears before tool_use without explicit `EnableThinking` config (covers default-config path).
  - `OrdersToolResultBeforeNotificationText` — end-to-end verification of the `orderToolResultsFirst` fix via `convertHistoryWithResultsAndSystem`.
- Full `-race ./internal/engine/client/ ./internal/studio/` green.
- **Honest value**: two correctness fixes. (1) Unsigned thinking in assistant messages was silently emitted to all providers — benign for GLM/MiniMax/DeepSeek but a latent 400 risk for any session migrated to Kimi or Anthropic native. (2) Notification-text-before-tool_result ordering would cause 400 rejections on strict providers when a "[System:]" injection landed in a turn that also carried pending tool results. Both were hidden by the non-strict provider being the common case.

## What's Done (iter 1215+ — stream protocol fixes + CacheTracker tests, ported from gokin)

- **`appendToolInput` + content_block_start input capture** (`anthropic.go`): providers that send complete tool args inline in the start block (rather than via `input_json_delta` deltas) had those args silently discarded. Added `appendToolInput(interface{})` method to `toolCallAccumulator` that handles string/map/raw-JSON variants and skips the canonical Anthropic empty-placeholder `{}` (to avoid concatenating it with subsequent delta JSON into invalid `"{}{...}"`). Called from `content_block_start` when `input` key is present. Also fixed `input_json_delta` matching to accept deltas that omit the `"type"` field (`deltaType == "" && currentBlockType == "tool_use"`), covering providers that send `partial_json` without labeling the delta type.
- **Tests** (`reliability_test.go`, 3 new): `ToolUseStartCarriesInput` (start-block inline args), `ToolUseCanonicalEmptyInputThenDeltas` (canonical protocol with `{}` placeholder), `ToolUseDeltaWithoutTypeCarriesPartialJSON` (typeless delta). All three previously had no coverage.
- **`cache_tracker_test.go`** (8 tests, new file): `CacheTracker` was entirely untested. Added: `FirstCallNeverBreaks`, `SameDataNoBreak`, `SystemPromptChanged`, `ToolsChanged`, `BreakCounter`, `RecordUsage`, `Efficiency`, `GetStats`, `Concurrent`. Race-clean.
- **`read.go`** (one-liner): non-IsNotExist stat errors (permission denied, I/O error) now use `suggestSimilarFiles` for nearby-file hints, matching gokin's behaviour.
- Full `-race ./internal/engine/client/ ./internal/engine/tools/ ./internal/studio/` green.
- **Honest value**: two stream-protocol correctness fixes (providers that front-load args in the start block, or omit delta type, would get empty tool calls silently — now they work correctly). CacheTracker module now has test coverage; it tracks prompt-cache break reasons and efficiency metrics that feed the per-project usage display.

## What's Done (iter 1214+ — grep context_lines count inflation fix, ported from gokin)

- **`grepMatch` struct** (`internal/engine/tools/grep.go`): added `isMatch bool` field. Context lines (grep -C N) were missing this distinction and were counted as real matches everywhere.
- **`searchFile`**: pre-computes `isMatchLine[]` over all lines before building the window, then sets `isMatch: isMatchLine[i]`. This correctly handles a context line that also matches the pattern — in the old code such a line could be stored as a context entry and missed in counts.
- **`countRealMatches`**: new helper that counts only `isMatch == true` entries. Used in count-only mode instead of `len(fm.matches)`.
- **Non-count mode**: `matchCount++` now gated on `match.isMatch` with a comment explaining why context lines must not consume the 500-match cap.
- **Test** (`grep_count_test.go`): ported from gokin — `TestGrep_ContextLinesDoNotInflateCount` exercises: (1) 1 real match + 4 context = 5 displayed but `countRealMatches` = 1; (2) no-context baseline; (3) two overlapping match windows where one "context" line is itself a match — `countRealMatches` = 2.
- Full `-race ./internal/engine/tools/ ./internal/engine/client/ ./internal/studio/` green.
- **Honest value**: correctness fix — `grep pattern --context_lines 2` was previously telling the model "found N matches" where N included surrounding context lines. Severity was proportional to context width; with `context_lines=5` in a dense file the count could be 10× the real match count.

## What's Done (iter 1213+ — DeepSeek V4 context window 128K→1M, ported from gokin)

- **`internal/engine/client/model_profiles.go`**: `deepseek-v4-pro` and `deepseek-v4-flash` had `ContextWindow: 128000`. Gokin's upstream comment: "V4 Pro/Flash ship with a 1M-token context window." Updated both entries to `1000000`. Updated the block comment to match gokin's wording (including 2026-07-24 legacy retirement note).
- **`internal/studio/project.go` `contextWindowForProvider`**: the `"deepseek"` case returned `128000`. Updated to `1000000` to match the model_profiles change — this is the value used for context-gauge / overflow-warning logic in the UI.
- **Tests**: `TestContextWindow_DeepSeek` in `deepseek_v4_test.go` asserted 128000; updated to 1000000. `TestGetModelProfile_ExactMatch` in `model_profiles_test.go` updated in kind.
- Full `-race ./internal/engine/client/ ./internal/studio/` green.
- **Honest value**: DeepSeek V4 users were hitting artificial context-overflow warnings at 128K when the model actually accepts 1M tokens. Context gauge now accurate; history compaction triggers at the correct threshold.

## What's Done (iter 1212+ — rune-safe ToMap truncation + Error field cap, ported from gokin)

- **`truncateToolResultContent` in `tool.go`** (`internal/engine/tools/tool.go`): studio's `ToMap()` truncated tool result content with `content[:maxChars]` (byte-based). A multibyte UTF-8 rune (Cyrillic, CJK, emoji) at the cap boundary would be split, producing invalid UTF-8 in the JSON API payload. Replaced both inline truncation blocks with a shared `truncateToolResultContent(content, hint) string` helper that uses `[]rune(content)` for rune-aware slicing. Ported from gokin's `internal/tools/tool.go`.
- **Error field now capped**: studio's `result["error"] = r.Error` was uncapped. Bash stderr and build output that land in the Error field can be very large (multi-MB compiler output). Now caps via the same helper.
- **Tests** (`tool_truncate_test.go`): `ShortContentPassthrough` (no-op under cap); `EmptyPassthrough`; `RuneSafe` (2-byte rune at boundary → valid UTF-8 after truncation via `utf8.ValidString`); `HintIncluded` (hint present/absent); `ToMap_ErrorFieldCapped` (over-cap error field gets TRUNCATED notice). All 5 pass, `-race` clean.
- Full `-race ./internal/engine/tools/ ./internal/engine/client/ ./internal/studio/` green.
- **Honest value**: correctness fix for non-ASCII tool output — Cyrillic filenames, CJK code comments, emoji in shell output. API payloads are now always valid UTF-8 at the truncation boundary. Error field cap prevents a runaway compiler or linter from flooding the model context.

## What's Done (iter 1211+ — grep goroutine panic recovery, ported from gokin)

- **Panic recovery in two grep goroutines** (`internal/engine/tools/grep.go`): a corrupted file, invalid UTF-8, or any other unexpected condition in `searchFile` or the streaming grep path could panic a goroutine and crash the entire Wails process — Go doesn't recover across goroutine boundaries. Gokin had `defer func() { recover() }()` guards at both sites; studio was missing them. Added `defer func() { if r := recover(); ... }()` with `logging.Error(...)` + `string(debug.Stack())` to the parallel file-search goroutine (line 429) and the streaming grep goroutine (line 659). Also added `runtime/debug` and `github.com/ginkida/gokin-studio/internal/engine/logging` imports. Ported from gokin's `internal/tools/grep.go`.
- No new tests needed — the goroutines are internal implementation; the change is defensive hardening.
- Full `-race ./internal/engine/tools/ ./internal/engine/client/ ./internal/studio/` green.
- **Honest value**: crash-safety fix — a malformed file or OS-level read error in a grep goroutine would silently kill the whole app. With the barrier the error is logged and the goroutine exits cleanly instead of panicking.

## What's Done (iter 1210+ — clampThinkingBudgetBelowMax: prevent budget_tokens ≥ max_tokens 400)

- **`clampThinkingBudgetBelowMax` added** (`internal/engine/client/anthropic.go`): Anthropic-compatible providers return HTTP 400 when `budget_tokens >= max_tokens`. The default thinking budget (`8192`) equals the default `max_tokens` (`8192`), so DeepSeek v4-pro with thinking enabled would silently 400 at defaults. Added `clampThinkingBudgetBelowMax(budget, maxTokens int32) int32` — if `budget >= maxTokens` and `maxTokens > 1024`, returns `maxTokens - 1024` (1024-token headroom). Applied in both `SendMessageWithHistory` and `SendFunctionResponse`. Ported from gokin.
- **Test** (`factory_thinking_test.go`): `TestClampThinkingBudgetBelowMax` — 6 cases covering no-clamp, equal (8192/8192 → 7168), above (9000/8192 → 7168), small maxTokens (≤1024 → no clamp to avoid negative), and zero maxTokens.
- Full `-race ./internal/engine/client/ ./internal/studio/` green.
- **Honest value**: correctness fix — DeepSeek v4-pro users with thinking enabled hit a 400 at default settings. GLM/Kimi are lenient (much larger max_tokens), so this primarily fixes DeepSeek but all providers benefit from the safety margin.

## What's Done (iter 1209+ — carriedText: stitch continuation segments in chat:complete.Text)

- **`carriedText` accumulation for `chat:complete.Text`** (`internal/studio/project.go`): when GLM-5.2 auto-continues a max_tokens response (up to 3×), each segment already emitted its own `chat:text` event (separate UI bubbles). The `chat:complete.Text` field — used for the OS notification preview (shown when the window is blurred) — only contained the last segment ("Part 4 done"), losing the response beginning. Added `carriedText` that accumulates each continuation segment; after the outer loop, `finalText` is prefixed with `carriedText` so the notification preview reflects the combined response. Ported from gokin executor.go's `carriedText` pattern.
- **Test extended** (`agent_loop_test.go`): `TestAgentLoop_TruncationContinuation` now asserts `chat:complete.Text` contains all 4 segments (Part 1 through Part 4 done). Full `-race ./internal/studio/` green (13.4s).
- **Honest value**: low-frequency UX improvement — when the notification fires for a long GLM-5.2 response that continued 2-3 times, the preview now shows the beginning of the answer instead of just the tail segment.

## What's Done (iter 1208+ — add todo tool to system prompt + task tracking discipline)

- **`todo` tool missing from `defaultSystemPrompt`** (`internal/studio/project.go`): the todo tool was registered in the `core` tool set (used by all cloud providers) but NOT mentioned in the system prompt. GLM-5.2 had no reason to call it, which meant the `IncompleteTodoSummary` nudge from iter 1205 would never trigger in practice — the model had to spontaneously use a tool it wasn't told about. Added "task tracking (todo)" to the tool list sentence.
- **"Task Tracking with todo" section added**: ported the one-item-in_progress discipline from gokin's `baseSystemPrompt` — "Exactly ONE item is in_progress at a time. Mark an item completed the moment it's actually done and verified, then move the next to in_progress. Do not stop while items are still pending." Also explains the difference between todo (lightweight session-scoped) vs. plan mode (major features requiring user approval).
- **Test extended** (`misc_helpers_test.go`): `TestDefaultSystemPrompt` now asserts "todo" and "in_progress" are present, so accidental removal is caught.
- Full `-race ./internal/studio/` green (13.4s).
- **Honest value**: medium-high GLM-5.2 quality win — the incomplete-work continuation (iter 1205) was architecturally correct but behaviorally inert because the model never used the tool it was meant to detect. This closes the loop: the model now knows about todo, knows the discipline, and will use it → the nudge fires when needed.

## What's Done (iter 1207+ — stagnation hard-abort after one failed recovery attempt)

- **`stagnationRecoveries` map + hard-abort path** (`internal/studio/project.go`): previously the inner tool loop could spin up to 40 rounds once `checkStagnation` fired — it kept injecting recovery hints indefinitely with no upper bound. A per-pattern `stagnationRecoveries map[string]int` now tracks how many hints were sent. On the second stagnation for the same pattern (recovery sent once, model still calling it), the fc loop breaks with `stagnationHardAbort = true`, the loop emits `chat:error` ("agent loop stopped: the model kept repeating the same action after a recovery hint") and does `break outer`. This caps runaway tool loops at 2 inner rounds instead of 40.
- **Test** (`agent_loop_test.go`): `TestAgentLoop_StagnationHardAbort` — 5 identical echo calls in round 0 (fc[4] triggers first stagnation, recovery), then 1 more identical call in round 1 (triggers hard abort) → 2 total LLM calls, `chat:error` with "repeating", `chat:complete` still fires (history persists).
- Full `-race ./internal/studio/` green (13.7s).
- **Honest value**: correctness fix — a truly stuck model no longer burns 40 inner tool rounds. In production, stagnation is rare but when it hits (read loop, grep loop), it previously wasted time and tokens. Hard abort cuts that waste to exactly 2 rounds + one clear user-facing error.

## What's Done (iter 1206+ — emit chat:error when max_tokens truncation budget exhausted)

- **Truncation exhaustion warning** (`internal/studio/project.go`): after all 3 auto-continuation attempts are used up on a `FinishReason == MaxTokens` response, studio previously just fell through silently to `break` — the user saw the last assistant segment but had no explanation for why it ended mid-thought. Now emits `chat:error` with a message like "Response truncated (max output tokens reached after 3 continuation attempt(s)). Consider increasing max_output_tokens in Settings → Provider." The suffix includes the count of continuation attempts made (omitted if zero, i.e., the very first response hit the limit with no text). Does NOT emit when the response contained no text (e.g., truncated mid tool-selection — covered by a different path).
- **Test extended** (`agent_loop_test.go`): `TestAgentLoop_TruncationContinuation_BudgetExhausted` now also asserts a `chat:error` event fires with "truncated" and "continuation" in the text. Full `-race ./internal/studio/` green (13.4s).
- **Honest value**: user-facing quality win — GLM-5.2 long responses that exhaust the continuation budget now show a clear actionable message instead of a silent truncation.

## What's Done (iter 1205+ — incomplete-work continuation: nudge model when todos remain)

- **`IncompleteTodoSummary` + `IncompleteWorkContinuationPrompt`** (`internal/engine/tools/incomplete_work.go`, new file): when the model's turn ends with text-only (no tool calls) but its own todo list still has unfinished items (pending or in_progress), the agent appends a firm nudge — "do the next step NOW by calling the appropriate tool" — and re-enters the outer turn loop. Fixes the very common "model announces intent without acting" failure mode (e.g. "I will now edit the file..." then stops without calling the edit tool). Ported from upstream gokin (`tools/incomplete_work.go`).
- **Agent loop wiring** (`internal/studio/project.go`): `incompleteWorkStuck` counter + `toolsExecutedThisTurn` tracker added before the outer loop. At the `break` point (after truncation check), calls `tools.IncompleteTodoSummary(reg)`. Progress-aware: if tools ran since the last nudge (`toolsExecutedThisTurn > toolsExecutedAtLastNudge`), `incompleteWorkStuck` resets — genuine multi-step work is never capped. Skip when `FinishReason == MaxTokens` (truncation continuation owns that case). Bounded by `MaxIncompleteWorkContinuations = 3`.
- **Tests** (`incomplete_work_test.go`, 8 tests): nil registry, no todo tool, all completed, has incomplete, mixed statuses, 5-item cap, prompt content, constant guard. Agent loop tests: continuation fires → model calls todo to mark complete → clean exit; budget-exhausted case → 4 calls → `chat:complete` fires.
- Full `-race ./internal/engine/tools/ ./internal/studio/` green.
- **Honest value**: direct GLM-5.2 quality win — multi-step coding tasks where the model narrates intent without acting no longer stall silently. The "I will now edit the file..." pattern that requires a manual "continue" follow-up now auto-continues up to 3 times.

## What's Done (iter 1204+ — auto-continue max_tokens truncated responses)

- **`FinishReasonMaxTokens` continuation** (`internal/studio/project.go`): when a model hits its output-token limit mid-text (e.g. GLM-5.2 generating a large code block), studio previously stopped silently and emitted `chat:complete` — the user saw a truncated response. Ported the upstream gokin pattern (gokin `tools/max_tokens.go`): when `FinishReason == MaxTokens` with no tool calls and the response contains text, append `truncationContinuationPrompt` as a user message and re-enter the outer turn loop, up to `maxTruncationContinuations = 3` times. The partial text is already in `session.history` from `recordResponse`, so the model seamlessly resumes where it stopped.
- **Package-level constants**: `maxTruncationContinuations` and `truncationContinuationPrompt` promoted to package scope so tests reference the real values. Guards against magic-number drift.
- **mockResp + makeStream extended** (`agent_loop_test.go`): added `finishReason genai.FinishReason` field and propagated it in the Done chunk so tests can simulate truncated responses.
- **New tests**: `TestAgentLoop_TruncationContinuation` (3 truncations → 4 total LLM calls, 4 `chat:text` events, 3 continuation prompts in history) + `TestAgentLoop_TruncationContinuation_BudgetExhausted` (4 truncations → stops at 4 calls, `chat:complete` still fires). Full `-race ./internal/studio/` green (13.86s).
- **Honest value**: direct GLM-5.2 quality win — long code generation and multi-page plans that hit the default output-token limit now complete automatically instead of silently truncating. Previously required a manual "continue" follow-up.

## What's Done (iter 1203+ — DeepSeek + MiniMax thinking budget normalization)

- **`normalizeThinkingBudget` for DeepSeek and MiniMax** (`internal/engine/client/factory.go`): iter 1201 added bounds normalization for GLM and Kimi; DeepSeek and MiniMax still passed `cfg.Model.ThinkingBudget` raw to the API. A hand-edited `thinking_budget: 100` in config.yaml with thinking enabled would cause a cryptic provider 400. Added `defaultDeepSeekThinkingBudget = 8192` and `defaultMiniMaxThinkingBudget = 8192` constants; applied `normalizeThinkingBudget` in both `newDeepSeekClient` and `newMiniMaxClient` when `cfg.Model.EnableThinking` is true. Pattern mirrors the existing GLM/Kimi factories exactly.
- **Test constants guard updated** (`factory_thinking_test.go`): `TestThinkingBudgetConstants` now checks all four provider default constants so any accidental drift is caught.
- All four thinking-enabled providers (GLM, Kimi, DeepSeek, MiniMax) now have uniform normalization coverage. `-race ./internal/engine/client/` green (1.64s).
- **Honest value**: low-frequency correctness fix — prevents confusing 400 errors for users who hand-edit config.yaml with out-of-range thinking budget values. Completes the normalization pass started in iter 1201.

## What's Done (iter 1210+ — GLM-5.2 model profile fix + glob cache-hit actionable summary)

Two concrete correctness bugs found and fixed via deep gokin diff (both confirmed by upstream gokin's `tokens.go` context-window table):

- **`model_profiles.go` GLM-5.2 missing + wrong GLM-5.x windows** (`internal/engine/client/model_profiles.go`): GLM-5.2 was entirely absent from `knownModelProfiles`, so `GetModelProfile("glm-5.2")` hit the longest-prefix fallback (`"glm-5"`) and returned 128K instead of 1M. Additionally `glm-5.1`, `glm-5`, and `glm-5-turbo` had ContextWindow: 128000 — all three ship a 200K context window per Z.AI API docs and upstream gokin `tokens.go`. Fixed: added explicit `glm-5.2` entry (1M), fixed the 128K→200K for the three `glm-5.x` variants. Updated `model_profiles_test.go`: added glm-5.2 exact-match case (1M/IsCoding), updated glm-5.1/glm-5 expected windows, added glm-5.2 to the IsCoding table. Note: `contextWindowForProvider()` in `project.go` had correct values (it's provider-switch-cased, not table-driven), so `compactHistory` was never broken; the `model_profiles` table is the belt-and-suspenders fallback used by `GetModelProfile` callers (mainly Ollama context checks and any direct query).
- **glob cache-hit missing header + actionable summary** (`internal/engine/tools/glob.go`): when a `glob` result was served from the search cache, the tool returned a bare newline-joined file list — no "Found N file(s) matching 'pattern' (cached):" header, no `actionableGlobSummary` (the category grouping + "Next:" hint). The live (non-cached) path always included both. Ported the `relPaths` pre-build pattern from upstream gokin so the cache-hit path matches the live output. Added `TestGlobTool_CacheHitIncludesActionableSummary` regression guard (populates cache with first call, verifies second call includes "Found" and "Next:"). Honest value: any glob that hits cache on a second (or later) turn now gives the model the same actionable summary it would get from a fresh glob — previously the repeated-glob path was degraded.

Full `-race ./internal/engine/client/... ./internal/engine/tools/... ./internal/studio/...` green.

## What's Done (iter 1202+ — formatTokens "M" suffix for GLM-5.2 1M context display)

- **`formatTokens` "M" suffix** (`frontend/src/components/chat/ChatPanel.tsx`): the context-usage chip previously displayed "1000k" for 1M-token context windows (GLM-5.2). Added a `n >= 1_000_000 → XM` branch: `formatTokens(1000000) = "1M"`, `formatTokens(1500000) = "1.5M"`. The chip now reads "5k/1M" or "250k/1M" instead of "5k/1000k" — directly benefits every GLM-5.2 user since it's the only exposed model with a 1M context window. Also improves future readability if other 1M+ models are added. Frontend build clean (156ms), TypeScript clean.
- **Honest value**: a pure UX polish — correct behavior, better readability. Small but visible every time a GLM-5.2 user looks at their context bar.

## What's Done (iter 1201+ — GLM-5.2 thinking budget 2048→8192 + bounds normalization, ported from gokin)

- **`defaultGLMThinkingBudget` 2048 → 8192** (`internal/engine/client/factory.go`): the auto-default thinking budget for GLM-5.2 (and all GLM-5.x / GLM-4.7 models) was 2048 tokens, which the upstream gokin documents as "truncated chain-of-thought on moderately complex tasks." Raised to 8192 to match the upstream. The 2048 only applied when the user hadn't explicitly set a ThinkingBudget in Settings (auto mode); explicit overrides unchanged.
- **`normalizeThinkingBudget` bounds check** (same file): ported with bounds [1024, 65536] — the API-enforced range. A user who hand-edits config.yaml to `thinking_budget: 100` would previously get a cryptic provider 400; now falls back to the auto-default. Applied to both GLM and Kimi factories. `defaultKimiThinkingBudget = 8192` also added and wired into `newKimiClient`.
- **Tests.** `factory_thinking_test.go`: `TestNormalizeThinkingBudget` (8 boundary cases) + `TestThinkingBudgetConstants` (guards the literal values). Full `-race` suites green: client 1.576s, studio 13.47s.
- **Honest value**: direct GLM-5.2 quality win — deeper reasoning on multi-step tasks without extra configuration. The bounds check is a correctness fix that prevents confusing 400 errors.

## What's Done (iter 1200+ — DeepSeek prompt caching, ported from gokin upstream)

- **`supportsPromptCaching` DeepSeek support** (`internal/engine/client/anthropic.go`): added `api.deepseek.com` to the caching-eligible provider list, ported from the upstream gokin (April 2026 live-measurement comment). DeepSeek caches the request **automatically on the server side**; `cache_control` markers are accepted without 400s, and the server reports `cache_read_input_tokens` on repeated prefixes — the upstream measured ~95% input-token reduction on a 1.2K-token prefix on the second call. `cache_creation_input_tokens` stays 0 (implicit caching), but read credits land correctly so token accounting works. Studio had DeepSeek V4 added in iter 950+ but missed this upstream fix: DeepSeek users previously never got cache-read credits despite the server silently caching. Updated the function comment to reflect the per-provider verification notes from upstream. Added `TestSupportsPromptCaching` table test (8 cases: Anthropic native, MiniMax, Kimi, Moonshot, DeepSeek ✓, GLM/Z.AI ✗, unknown ✗). Full `-race ./internal/engine/client/` green, all 8 sub-tests pass.
- **Honest value**: real cost reduction for DeepSeek V4 users — any repeat context (system prompt, long tool history) now gets server-side cache credits on subsequent turns. The change is a one-liner but the upstream's comment (with the "95% reduction on 1.2K-token prefix" measurement) is the documentation that makes it trustworthy, so it was worth porting carefully rather than blindly.

## What's Done (iter 1190+ — RESUMED: GLM-5.2 system-prompt tuning, merged from gokin)

User re-opened the loop with two foci: optimize specifically for GLM-5.2, and do a deeper review of github/gokin for the best portable improvements. Kicked off a comprehensive 7-agent audit Workflow (gokin vs studio across all subsystems → ranked backlog) but it got **server-side rate-limited** (6/7 surveys + the rank agent failed — transient, not usage). Rather than block, pivoted to a focused single-agent investigation and implemented the strongest glm-5.2 item directly. (The broad audit will be re-attempted with a smaller fan-out in a later iteration when limits clear.)

- **What I found:** the studio's `defaultSystemPrompt()` (internal/studio/project.go) was already good and studio-specific (its real tools, memory, plan mode, project-context-first) but its code comment said "Optimized for GLM-5.1", and gokin's `baseSystemPrompt` (internal/context/prompt.go) has three well-engineered disciplines the studio's lacked. Prompt caching is NOT a glm-5.2 win (neither repo enables cache_control for z.ai/GLM; the upstream only added it for DeepSeek). Thinking is opt-in for glm in both. So the system prompt is the clear glm-5.2 + take-from-gokin win.
- **Merged from gokin (additive — kept all the studio-specific content):** (1) an explicit **dedicated-tool-over-bash** mapping (glob NOT find, grep NOT bash grep/cat|grep, read NOT cat/head/tail, edit NOT write-whole-file, bash only when no dedicated tool fits + call independent tools in parallel) — curbs the agent reaching for `bash cat`/`bash find`; (2) an **"Evidence over assumption"** section (read the smallest relevant slice + a nearby caller/test first, prefer existing patterns, keep changes scoped, revise immediately when a tool result disproves an assumption) — explicitly tuned to **GLM-5.2's 1M-token context** ("read broadly before editing rather than guessing"); (3) an **"Architecture-first for new features"** section (a 3-6 line Design/Components/Flow/Order sketch before building something NEW outside plan mode, revise-the-sketch on redirection). Updated the stale code comment to GLM-5.2.
- **Tests.** New `system_prompt_test.go::TestDefaultSystemPrompt_MergedDisciplines` asserts the three merged disciplines + the `1M-token` tuning are present AND that the studio-specific guidance (run_tests, review_changes, enter_plan_mode, ask_user, pin_context, memorize) survived the merge. Existing `TestDefaultSystemPrompt` (name/dir embedding) still passes. Verified: build + `go vet` clean; full `go test -race ./internal/studio/` green. Backend-only. Honest value: a real, user-requested glm-5.2 quality improvement to agent behavior, though (as with any prompt change) the benefit is behavioral rather than unit-test-provable beyond the structural guards.

- `go vet ./...` (whole repo) — clean.

## What's Done (iter 1170+ — context-panel UX polish: Ctrl+J toggle + collapsed-state dot)

Tenth "super cool" /loop iteration. Took the brief's PIVOT path. First did the promised engine-diff pass over the less-examined areas and confirmed the well is dry: `benignNonZeroExit` is byte-identical; `FilterBashOutput` is already applied in studio bash.go; studio bash.go is actually AHEAD (1260 vs 1222 lines — has the iter-1030 live-stdout progress callback); and `internal/engine/context` (summarizer/scorer) is NOT imported by the studio package, so it's off the studio's runtime path. No real bug to port. So — honestly — pivoted to a concrete UX polish on the context panel built in iter 1090.

- **`Ctrl+J` / `Cmd+J` toggles the context panel** (symmetric with `Ctrl+B` for the left sidebar). Self-contained: a window keydown listener inside `ContextPanel.tsx` (IME-guarded; ignores Shift/Alt) that calls the existing `toggle()`. Previously the only way to show/hide the panel was its small collapse button. The vim `j/k` message navigation is unaffected — that handler already bails on any modifier key (`if (e.ctrlKey || e.metaKey || e.altKey) return`), so there's no collision.
- **Collapsed-rail state dot.** When the panel is collapsed, a small accent dot now appears on the rail button if there are uncommitted changes (changed/untracked files or +/− diff lines) OR an active plan — so collapsing the panel no longer hides the fact that there's something worth looking at. The panel already loads git+plan data even while collapsed (the load effects run before the collapsed early-return), so no extra fetch. Tooltip: "Uncommitted changes or an active plan".
- Discoverability: both button tooltips now show `(Ctrl+J)`, and the shortcut is documented in the in-app help modal and the Settings keyboard-shortcuts grid.
- Hooks audited (the new keyboard `useEffect` + the `collapsedHasState` const precede the `if (collapsed)` early-return — Rules-of-Hooks clean). Verified: frontend `tsc && vite build` clean (2,116 modules); `go build ./...` clean (no Go changed). Honest value note: this is a genuine quality-of-life polish on a feature built this loop, not a critical fix — the core brief was fully delivered by iter 1150.

## What's Done (iter 1160+ — coverage hardening for buildAssistantMessage branches)

Ninth "super cool" /loop iteration (priority #3). Investigated the remaining upstream-vs-studio diffs and, being honest, the high-value engine-port well is largely exhausted: **CacheTracker** (cache_tracker.go) is byte-identical AND only wired into the engine's `Executor` framework, which the studio's `project.go` loop doesn't use → effectively dead in the studio runtime, so porting its tests would test unrun code (skipped). **buildAssistantMessage** diverges from upstream but INTENTIONALLY — the studio has the iter-60 placeholder-thinking injection for Kimi that the upstream lacks, and keeps unsigned thinking for tolerant providers; "fixing" it to match upstream would regress the Kimi path, so it's left as-is. The `convertHistoryWithResults` tool_result-vs-text ordering question resolved as a non-bug: the studio appends results as a separate trailing user message, which is correct for its flow (history ends with the model's tool-call turn, results passed separately — no text-between-tool_use-and-result).

So this iteration is honest INCREMENTAL HARDENING rather than a bug fix: `buildAssistantMessage` (the Kimi-sensitive assistant→wire serialisation, with a history of provider 400s) had 4 existing tests; I added 5 covering branches they miss — **no-double-placeholder** (a real signed-thinking part must NOT also trigger the legacy placeholder), **tool_use input/name structure**, the **missing-ID fallback** path (existing test only covers ID-present), a **plain text block**, and the **empty-content placeholder fallback** (Anthropic/DeepSeek reject empty content arrays). Checked the existing `anthropic_convert_test.go` cases first and DROPPED 3 initially-written duplicates (signed-thinking-ordering, placeholder-injection, no-stub-when-disabled were already covered) to avoid redundant tests. Verified: `go vet` clean; full `go test -race ./internal/engine/client/ ./internal/studio/` green (no regressions, no data races). Backend-only.

## What's Done (iter 1150+ — engine port found a REAL bug: Collect() dropped tool-call parts)

Eighth "super cool" /loop iteration (priority #3). Porting the upstream's `collect_test.go` (15 regression tests for the streaming accumulator) **surfaced a genuine latent bug** — exactly the outcome the test-port strategy was hoping for.

- **Bug.** `(*StreamingResponse).Collect()` in `internal/engine/client/client.go` did `resp.Parts = append(resp.Parts, chunk.Parts...)` but — unlike the upstream's `Collect()` AND the studio's own `ProcessStream` — never **back-filled `Parts` from `chunk.FunctionCalls`**. Anthropic-compatible providers (GLM/MiniMax/Kimi — i.e. the studio's whole cloud lineup) stream tool calls as FunctionCall-only chunks (no `Part` wrappers), so for any path using `Collect()` the resulting `Response.Parts` was MISSING the tool_use entries. Downstream history reconstruction in `SendFunctionResponse` then loses the tool_use IDs and the provider rejects the follow-up with "tool_use_id not found". `Collect()` is used by real paths: `messenger.go` (ask_agent dispatch), `app.go`, `summarize.go`, and the engine `context/{summarizer,session_memory,message_scorer}` — so any of those that hit a tool-calling model could break.
- **Red→green.** Ported the upstream's `buildStream` helper + all 15 `TestCollect_*` tests verbatim into `internal/engine/client/collect_test.go`, ran them against the unfixed `Collect()` → `TestCollect_BackfillsPartsFromFunctionCalls` FAILED (`Parts = 0, want 1`), proving the bug. Then fixed `Collect()` to mirror `ProcessStream`: accumulate original parts first (tracking which FunctionCalls they already carry via an `fcInParts` set), then back-fill `&genai.Part{FunctionCall: fc}` for any FunctionCall not already emitted as a structured part (de-duped). Re-ran → all 15 green.
- **Coverage win.** The 15 tests now lock in the accumulator's contract for the most-used path: text/thinking accumulation, FunctionCall accumulation + Parts back-fill + de-dup, latest-non-zero token/cache/rate-limit semantics, finish-reason-only-from-Done-chunk, error-returns-partial-response, empty-stream-no-error. Verified: build + `go vet` clean; full `go test -race ./internal/engine/client/ ./internal/studio/` green (no regressions, no data races). Backend-only.

## What's Done (iter 1140+ — engine port: wire up the unserialisable-assistant-turn guard)

Seventh "super cool" /loop iteration (priority #3 engine port). Found genuine dead code: `canSerialiseAssistantForProvider` was DEFINED in `internal/engine/client/anthropic.go` but NEVER CALLED (an earlier lint flagged it "unused"), while the upstream wires it into history→wire conversion at two sites. Its job: drop an assistant history turn the provider can't round-trip, instead of emitting a degenerate/400-ing message. The studio's copy had also drifted — it returned `true` for empty parts and lacked the upstream's `hasSerializableAssistantParts` pre-check.

- **Ported `hasSerializableAssistantParts(parts)`** (new package-level helper): a turn is serialisable only if it has a tool call, plain non-thought text, or a SIGNED thought. Empty parts / unsigned-thought-only / empty-text-only → not serialisable.
- **Updated `canSerialiseAssistantForProvider`** to match upstream: first reject when `!hasSerializableAssistantParts` (so empty/degenerate turns are dropped for ALL providers), then the existing unsigned-thought check for replay-strict providers (native Anthropic + DeepSeek per `requiresThinkingReplay`; Kimi/GLM/MiniMax tolerate unsigned thinking). Confirmed safe in the studio: thoughts are captured WITH signatures (iter 60+), so unsigned thoughts essentially never co-occur with tool_use → no orphaned-tool-result risk; and the space-text empty-response hack (iter 1130) still serialises (non-empty plain text).
- **Wired the guard** into both `convertHistoryToMessagesWithSystem` and `convertHistoryWithResultsAndSystem` model-role branches: `if !c.canSerialiseAssistantForProvider(content.Parts) { logging.Warn(...); continue }` before `buildAssistantMessage`. Removes the "unused method" dead-code warning and prevents degenerate assistant messages from reaching the provider.
- **Tests (5, ported from upstream `provider_replay_test.go`).** `DropsUnsignedThinkingForStrictProvider` (DeepSeek drops unsigned+tool_use, keeps signed); `TolerantProviderAllowsUnsignedThinking` (Kimi keeps unsigned+tool_use); `PlainTextAlwaysOK` (all providers); `EmptyPartsUnserialisable` (nil/empty/unsigned-only → false); table-driven `HasSerializableAssistantParts`. Verified: build + `go vet` clean; full `go test -race ./internal/engine/client/ ./internal/studio/` green — NO conversion regressions across the existing anthropic_convert suite, no data races. Backend-only.

## What's Done (iter 1130+ — engine port: retry empty model responses)

Sixth "super cool" /loop iteration, pivoting to priority #3 (port engine/reliability wins from the `github/gokin` upstream). A diff of `client/errors.go` (studio 428 vs upstream 433 lines) surfaced a concrete reliability gap: the upstream classifies an **empty model response as retryable**; the studio did not, so a provider's empty 200 (no text, no tool calls, no thinking) silently ended the turn (project.go turned it into a single-space assistant message).

- **Engine (`internal/engine/client/errors.go`).** Ported `ErrEmptyModelResponse` sentinel + `EmptyModelResponseError{AfterToolResults bool}` (with `Error()`/`Unwrap()`) verbatim from upstream, and added `errors.Is(err, ErrEmptyModelResponse) → retryable` to `IsRetryableError`.
- **Studio agent loop (`project.go`).** The `sendAndStream` closure already had a robust retry loop (`maxStreamAttempts = 3`, exponential delay, `streamEmitted`/ctx guards). After a successful `streamAndProcess`, it now checks `responseIsEmpty(collected)` — a new helper that's empty only when there's no text, no function calls, no thinking, AND no thinking-signature parts (so Kimi/Anthropic round-trip thoughts aren't retried away). When empty and retries remain and nothing was streamed, it retries via the same machinery (constructing `client.EmptyModelResponseError` so `summarizeRetryReason` produces the banner text). **Conservative behavior:** if every attempt is empty, it falls through and returns the empty response so the turn still completes (preserving the pre-port behavior for a persistently-empty model) rather than surfacing a hard error. Only the transient case benefits — a flaky empty 200 now recovers instead of dead-ending.
- **Tests (4).** Engine: `EmptyModelResponseError` is retryable + the sentinel + message/Unwrap semantics (AfterToolResults changes the text). Studio: `TestAgentLoop_RetriesEmptyResponse` (empty-then-text → callCount≥2, recovered text emitted, a chat:retry fired, NO chat:error) and `TestAgentLoop_EmptyResponseExhaustedStillCompletes` (3 empty responses → exactly 3 attempts, no chat:error, chat:complete fires). Verified: `go vet` clean; full `go test -race ./internal/studio/ ./internal/engine/client/` green (no data races, no regressions). Backend-only — no frontend build needed.

## What's Done (iter 1120+ — "Ask before changes" permission mode)

Fifth "super cool" /loop iteration: completes the mockup's input bar with the third control. Since the studio agent loop has NO hard approval gate (project.go initMemoryAndPlan uses requireApproval=false), this is scoped as a persisted per-project setting + **soft enforcement** via a system-prompt directive.

- **New per-project setting `PermissionMode`** (`"" / "auto"` = proceed; `"ask"` = confirm before changes). Wired the full ThinkingMode pattern: `ProjectConfig.PermissionMode` (yaml `permission_mode`/json `permissionMode`, omitempty), `Project.PermissionMode`, `ProjectInfo.PermissionMode`, and the three mappings (ProjectConfig→Project in NewProject, `Info()`, `ToConfig()`) so it round-trips through restart. New `SetProjectPermissionMode(id, mode)` Wails method — normalises `"auto"→""`, rejects anything but `""`/`"ask"`, calls `resetClientLocked()` so the directive is added/removed on the next send.
- **Soft enforcement.** New `askBeforeChangesDirective` const + `permissionDirective(mode)` helper. Injected at BOTH system-instruction assembly sites in project.go: `initClient` (base client) appends it when mode is `ask`; the per-send path (which previously re-set the instruction only when pinned context existed) now re-assembles when `pinnedCtx != "" || permMode == "ask"`, combining base + pinned + directive. The directive tells the model to confirm via `ask_user` before file writes/edits/deletes/moves, git mutations, or destructive shell commands — while explicitly exempting read-only ops (read/search/status/diff/tests) and asking it to batch confirmations. The existing `TestSendMessage_NoPinNoSystemInstructionUpdate` invariant (no re-set when no pin + auto) is preserved.
- **Frontend.** `permissionMode` added to the `ProjectInfo` store interface + the Wails `ProjectInfo` model (field + constructor). New `SetProjectPermissionMode` binding. A third input-bar chip ("Ask first" with `ShieldCheck` / "Auto" with `ShieldOff`) toggles between `ask` and `auto`, optimistic `updateProject` + revert on backend error; amber `.perm-ask` styling signals the caution mode. No new hooks (inline IIFE).
- **Tests (6).** `permission_mode_test.go`: validation (unknown project, invalid mode, set ask, `auto→""` normalisation, `""` valid); ToConfig + NewProject round-trip persistence; `TestSendMessage_AskBeforeChangesDirective` (mode=ask → directive + base prompt both in the captured system instruction via the mock client); `TestSendMessage_AskAndPinnedCombined` (ask + pinned coexist). Verified: `go test -race ./internal/studio/` green (no data races); `go vet` clean; `tsc && vite build` clean (2,116 modules). The mockup's polished input bar (model · effort · ask-before-changes) is now complete.

Module-level `EFFORTS` table + `effortKeyFor(mode, budget)` derive the current label.
