## What's Done (iter 1100+ — one-click commit composer in the context panel)

Third "super cool" /loop iteration: makes the iter-1090 Git tools section actionable. A "Commit all changes…" button opens an inline composer right in the panel; committing refreshes the diff stat to clean.

- **Backend — `CommitChanges(projectID, message) -> CommitResult{Hash, Subject, Branch}`** (new `internal/studio/git_commit.go`). Stages everything (`git add -A`, including untracked files the agent created) and commits with the message. Validates: non-empty message, the dir is a git work tree, and there's something to commit; on git failure (identity not configured, pre-commit hook rejecting, …) it surfaces git's combined stdout+stderr verbatim so the user sees exactly why. New `runGitErr(dir, timeout, args…)` helper (error-returning counterpart to the existing silent `runGit`, via `exec.CommandContext` with a 30s timeout) + small `gitErrText`/`firstLine` helpers. Success is recorded in the event log (`info`/`git`).
- **Frontend — composer in `ContextPanel.tsx`.** The Git tools section shows an untracked-file count and a "Commit all changes…" button whenever there are changes (tracked or untracked). The button opens an inline textarea composer (Ctrl/Cmd+Enter to commit, Esc to cancel, IME-guarded, 500-char cap, autofocus) with Cancel/Commit buttons, an inline error banner on failure, and a transient green "Committed <hash>" confirmation. On success it clears the composer and reloads git context (diff stat → clean, button hides). All new state + the `doCommit` callback are declared before the collapsed early-return (Rules-of-Hooks clean). New Wails binding `CommitChanges` hand-patched. CSS for the composer/button/error/confirmation added in App.css using the dark theme tokens.
- **Tests (5).** `git_commit_test.go`: unknown project; empty/whitespace message (3 cases); non-repo directory; clean tree → "nothing to commit"; full success path — commits a tracked modification AND a new untracked file, asserts the returned hash/subject/branch, a clean working tree afterward, the message in `git log`, and that the untracked `fresh.txt` was included (proving `git add -A` staged it). Verified: `go vet` clean; `go test -race ./internal/studio/` green (no data races); frontend `tsc && vite build` clean (2,116 modules).

## What's Done (iter 1090+ — right-hand context panel: Git tools / Goal / Progress)

Second "super cool" /loop iteration: the signature mockup element — a collapsible right-hand context panel rendered alongside the chat for any project, evolving the existing project/session layout (not a rebuild). Read-only this pass; one-click commit + the polished input bar come next.

- **Backend — git diff stat.** `ProjectGitContext` (git_status.go) gains `Insertions`/`Deletions`, summed from `git diff HEAD --numstat` (uncommitted tracked changes vs HEAD; untracked excluded; a repo with no commits yet → 0/0 since `git diff HEAD` fails silently). Feeds the panel's "Changes +N -M" indicator.
- **Backend — plan accessor.** New `internal/studio/project_plan.go`: `GetProjectPlan(projectID) -> ProjectPlanInfo{Active, Title, Status, CurrentStep, TotalSteps, Steps[]}` reads the per-project `plan.Manager` (the one driving enter_plan_mode / update_plan_progress). Steps are read via the plan's deep-copying `GetStepsSnapshot()` (which locks the plan), so it's race-safe to call from the Wails goroutine while the agent mutates the plan. Deliberately uses `GetCurrentPlan()` not `IsActive()` so a fully-completed plan still shows (matches the mockup's "Complete · 5/5"); status is derived from the step snapshot (failed/completed/in_progress) rather than reading the unsynchronized `plan.Status`.
- **Frontend — `ContextPanel.tsx`** (new, `components/layout/`). Three sections: **Git tools** (branch + ahead/behind chip + green/red `+N -M` diff stat or "working tree clean" + up to 8 changed files with status-colored M/A/D/R prefix), **Goal** (plan title + `N/M steps` + a Complete/In progress/Failed badge), **Progress** (step list with status icons — completed = strikethrough, in_progress = spinning loader, failed = red X). Collapsible to a 40px rail (persisted in `localStorage['gokin:context-panel-collapsed']`). Loads on project switch (stale-response-guarded) and auto-refreshes on `chat:complete` / `project:status=idle` for the active project; manual refresh button. All hooks declared before the collapsed early-return (Rules-of-Hooks clean).
- **Mount.** App.tsx wraps the chat view in `<div className="chat-with-context">` (flex row: ChatPanel flex:1 + ContextPanel fixed 304px); only rendered for chat views, only when a project is active. New CSS block in App.css using the existing dark theme tokens. Wails bindings hand-patched (`GetProjectPlan`).
- **Tests (6).** `git_diffstat_test.go`: `+3/-1` after a real edit; `+0/-0` on a clean committed tree. `project_plan_test.go`: unknown project errors; no-plan → Active:false; active 3-step plan → in_progress with correct title/steps/status; completed plan still returned with status=completed and full progress. Verified: `go test -race ./internal/studio/ ./internal/engine/client/` green (no data races); `go vet` clean; frontend `tsc && vite build` clean (2,116 modules).

## What's Done (iter 1080+ — GLM-5.2 default + model lineup sync)

First iteration of the "super cool" /loop (evolve+restyle toward the task-centric mockup; based on the `github/gokin` upstream). This iteration is the foundational model-layer change: **GLM-5.2 is now the default model** and the whole lineup is synced to the upstream, fixing the long-standing `GetProviders()` drift.

- **glm-5.2 added end-to-end.** Engine `internal/engine/client/client.go` `AvailableModels` gains `glm-5.2` (flagship, 1M context) ahead of `glm-5.1` (re-described as previous flagship, 200K). Engine `config/provider_registry.go` glm `DefaultModel` → `glm-5.2`. Studio `config.go`: `defaultConfig()` default model `glm-5.1` → `glm-5.2`; removed-provider and empty/removed-provider project migrations now target `glm-5.2`; legacy `glm-4-*` deprecated-model migration now upgrades to `glm-5.2` (was `glm-5.1`).
- **`GetProviders()` drift fixed (the iter-730 known-drift note).** `app.go` previously listed dead models (`glm-4-plus/air/flash/long`, `MiniMax-M1/Text-01/abab6.5s-chat`). Now mirrors `client.AvailableModels` for cloud providers: glm → `[glm-5.2, glm-5.1, glm-5, glm-5-turbo, glm-4.7, glm-4.5]`; minimax → `[MiniMax-M2.7, MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed, MiniMax-M2.5, MiniMax-M2.5-highspeed]`; kimi/deepseek/ollama unchanged. New `get_providers_lineup_test.go` regression-locks this: every cloud model offered must exist in `client.AvailableModels`, glm leads with `glm-5.2`, and the retired glm-4-plus tier is gone.
- **Pricing (`pricing.go`).** Added `glm-5.2` at the Max tier (0.55 in / 2.19 out, with cache rates, same as glm-5.1). Added the modern MiniMax M2.x rates (`minimax-m2.7`/`-highspeed`, `minimax-m2.5`/`-highspeed` at 0.30-0.60 in / 1.20-2.40 out) plus a `minimax` catch-all so an unknown future MiniMax model no longer falls through to $0 (previously `MiniMax-M2.7` had no pricing entry and showed cost 0). Legacy abab/minimax-m1 keys kept for back-compat.
- **Context windows (`contextWindowForProvider`).** glm-5.2 → 1,000,000 (Z.AI 1M window); glm-5.x (5.1/5/5-turbo) → 200,000 (was 128K); glm-4.x → 128K; MiniMax default → 204,800 (M2.x 200K, was 128K). Kimi/DeepSeek unchanged.
- **Frontend.** All `'glm-5.1'` fallback/default literals → `'glm-5.2'` (settingsStore default, App.tsx settings hydration, Sidebar/StatusBar/ChatPanel/ProviderSelect display fallbacks). OnboardingWizard first-run defaults: glm → `glm-5.2`, minimax → `MiniMax-M2.7` (was the dead `minimax-text-01`).
- **Tests.** Updated `context_window_test.go` (glm-5.2=1M, glm-5.x=200K, minimax=204800), `config_test.go` (all migration expectations glm-5.1 → glm-5.2), `toconfig_race_test.go` (writer/assertion use glm-5.2 since the project's initial default changed). Added `TestEstimateCost_GLM5_2`, `TestEstimateCost_MiniMaxM2_7` (+ catch-all/prefix cases), and the three new `get_providers_lineup_test.go` tests. Verified: `go test -race ./internal/studio/ ./internal/engine/client/` green (no data races); `go vet` clean; frontend `tsc && vite build` clean (2,115 modules).

## What's Done (iter 1070+ — reliability hardening pass)

A dedicated reliability audit (7-dimension multi-agent review → adversarial
verification → fix → independent adversarial re-review of the diff) found and
fixed 14 real correctness/robustness bugs, most silently-broken paths that only
surface under real-world use. Each has a regression test; the whole suite is
race-clean.

- **CRITICAL — React #310 white-screen (frontend).** `ChatPanel` declared ~14 hooks AFTER its no-project early return, so deleting the last project (`activeProjectId→null`) or adding the first project flipped the hook count on the same mounted instance → "rendered fewer/more hooks" → the ErrorBoundary white-screened the whole app. There was no eslint to catch it. Fixed by splitting into a thin `ChatPanel` wrapper (does the project check, then early-returns the empty state) that mounts `ChatPanelBody` (all the hooks, always run) with `activeProject` passed as a guaranteed-defined prop. The body never renders project-less, so its hook list is constant and every `activeProject.x` access is type-safe.
- **HIGH — cancelled/cleared turn resurrected sessions (`project.go`).** The final history save ran unconditionally after the agent loops broke. A `/clear` (ClearHistory) or session delete cancels the turn's ctx then wipes + removes the history file, but the still-running goroutine re-wrote it — so `/clear` silently failed to stick and deleted sessions reappeared on restart. Now the final `SaveHistoryWithUsage` is gated: it persists on a clean finish OR the 30-minute `DeadlineExceeded` (legitimate work — the post-review fix; an earlier too-broad `ctx.Err()==nil` guard would have lost long-run work on timeout), and skips ONLY on `context.Canceled` (Stop/clear/delete), with an inner re-check under `session.mu` for a clear racing in. The in-memory budget cache (`bumpTotalCostUSD`) now moves in lockstep with the disk save (was unconditional → desynced enforcement). The replay recovery log is dropped only when persisted, so a plain user-Stop stays recoverable.
- **HIGH — config corruption under concurrent writers (`history.go`).** `atomicWriteFile` used a single fixed `path+".tmp"`; two agent turns firing `saveConfigAsync` concurrently both wrote `config.yaml.tmp`, truncating each other → a torn YAML that `LoadConfig` falls back to defaults on, silently wiping every project + API key on next start. Fixed with a unique `os.CreateTemp` temp per write + `Sync` before rename. Plus a leaf-level per-file mutex (`lockHistoryFile`) serializing the load-modify-write of each session history file so a rename/edit during a turn can't lose usage or overwrite full history with a stale snapshot (F13).
- **HIGH — pooled client cross-contamination (`factory.go`/`pool.go`).** The engine client pool is keyed only by `provider:model`, so two studio projects on the same model (e.g. both on default glm-5.1) shared ONE `*AnthropicClient` and clobbered each other's system prompt + pinned context (project B's `SetSystemInstruction` overwriting A's → wrong persona / leaked pinned notes). Added `NewClientNoPool`; studio now gives each project a dedicated client (it already caches one per project, so it gained nothing from the pool). Also restored proactive client `Close()` (idle-conn release) on cache-invalidation + teardown via `resetClientLocked`, since the no-pool path removed the pool's eviction-close.
- **HIGH — streamed tool calls silently dropped (`anthropic.go`).** The `message_delta` handler attached accumulated tool calls only when `stop_reason=="tool_use"`. GLM/MiniMax/Kimi/DeepSeek (Anthropic-compat shims) sometimes stream tool_use blocks while reporting `end_turn`/`stop`; since the SSE loop returns on the first Done chunk, the `message_stop` fallback was unreachable → the agent silently stopped mid-task with no tool execution. Now tool calls attach for ANY terminating stop_reason (idempotent via `callsEmitted`).
- **MEDIUM — `sess.Name` / `lastUsedAt` data races (`app.go`).** `ListChatSessions` / `CreateChatSession` / `SearchProjectHistory` read `sess.Name`, and `ExportProjectAllSessions` read `sess.lastUsedAt`, under only `p.mu` while the agent loop's first-turn auto-rename + per-turn bump write them under `session.mu`. Fixed all read sites to take `sess.mu.RLock`. **Root cause also fixed:** the agent loop took `p.mu` while holding `session.mu` (session→p) — a latent AB-BA deadlock against every p→session reader; restructured so it acquires the two locks separately, giving one global `p.mu→session.mu` order. (`-race` regression test with concurrent rename + readers.)
- **MEDIUM — mid-stream transient drops were unrecoverable (`project.go`).** `sendWithRetry` only wrapped the pre-stream call (returns at 200 OK); a connection drop / idle-timeout MID-stream aborted the whole turn (a multi-minute refactor lost on one blip). New `sendAndStream` retries the whole request when the failure is transient AND nothing was streamed to the UI yet (so no duplicate output / history corruption) — conservative, backward-compatible with all existing stream-error tests.
- **MEDIUM — leaks & accounting:** self-exited terminals now reap the PTY child + close the fd + drop the registry entry (was a zombie + fd leak per `exit`/Ctrl+D); `RemoveProject` closes the project's terminals (backend cleanup no longer depends on frontend unmount timing); `ProcessStream` now accumulates `CacheCreationInputTokens` (cache-write cost was always 0, undercounting cost + weakening budget enforcement).
- **MEDIUM — unguarded SSE channel sends (`anthropic.go`).** Four terminal sends ([DONE]+tool-calls, Z.AI error, idle-timeout, scanner-error) were bare blocking sends; on cancel with a full buffer + gone consumer they stranded the producer goroutine forever. Wrapped each in `select{...; case <-ctx.Done()}` (matching the sibling sends).
- **LOW — draft/pin UTF-8 truncation (`drafts.go`/`pins.go`).** `text[:max]` could split a multibyte rune (Cyrillic/CJK/emoji) at the 200 KB cap, persisting invalid UTF-8. New rune-safe `truncateUTF8`.

Tests: **+7 studio** (`reliability_test.go`: rune-safe truncation; concurrent-reader race test; mid-stream retry recovers; non-retryable not retried; cancelled-turn skips save; DeadlineExceeded still persists; budget-cache consistency) and **+2 engine-client** (`reliability_test.go`: stop_reason tool-call attach; ProcessStream cache-creation). Studio suite: **738 tests**, coverage **90.3%**, `-race` clean. Full repo build + `go vet` + frontend tsc/build all clean.

## What's Done (iter 1060+)

- **Drag-to-reorder projects in the sidebar** -- mirrors iter 540+'s session-tab reorder, but at the project level. Users with 10+ projects can now anchor a manual order beyond the recent-first auto-sort + iter 430+ pinning. Within each pin group, the user-defined order takes precedence over `lastUsedAt`; new projects without an explicit position fall to the BOTTOM of the unordered set (rather than top) — the explicit ordering owns its slots, lastUsedAt-default fills the rest. Backend (new file `internal/studio/project_order.go`): persists a flat array of IDs in `<configDir>/project-order.json` (single file, not per-project — there's only one project list). New `(*Studio).GetProjectOrder() []string` and `ReorderProjects(orderedIDs []string)` Wails methods. `loadProjectOrder` silently treats corruption + missing file as "no order set" — ordering is a non-critical UX feature; refusing to list projects because the order file is malformed would be a worse outcome than a temporary unordered display. `ReorderProjects` filters incoming IDs against `s.projects` (drops unknown + dedups) so a stale frontend snapshot can't corrupt the file. Empty input removes the file (clean exit semantics — reverting to default sort doesn't leave a `[]` stub). `GetProjectOrder` also filters at READ time so an order containing a since-deleted project ID doesn't bleed into the sort key map. Frontend (`Sidebar.tsx`): new state `projectOrder`, `draggingProjectId`, `dropTargetId`. Loaded once on mount via `GetProjectOrder`. Sort comparator extended: pinned-first → custom-order index → `lastUsedAt` desc → alpha tiebreaker. Custom-ordered IDs always sort BEFORE unordered (new) projects within the same pin group. Standard HTML5 drag/drop on the `.project-item` div: `onDragStart` records source, `onDragOver` marks drop target with `preventDefault` to enable drop, `onDragLeave` clears the indicator only when truly leaving (not when transiting child elements — without the `currentTarget.contains(relatedTarget)` guard the indicator flickered on icon hover). Drop reorders the visible sorted list (splice from src, insert before target), persists via `ReorderProjects`, and optimistically updates local state so the UI reflects the change instantly. **Drag is disabled while search is active or while a project is being renamed** — reordering within a filtered view would persist a partial order that doesn't reflect the real layout. CSS: new `.is-dragging` (45% opacity) and `.drop-target` (3px accent left-edge inset shadow) modifiers + `cursor: grab/grabbing`. Tests (7 new in `project_order_test.go`): round-trip persistence; filtering unknown IDs (drops ghost-id, keeps live IDs); deduplication of repeats; empty input clears the file; ghost IDs filtered at read time after RemoveProject; cold-start returns empty cleanly; corrupt JSON silently empty; empty-string entries dropped. Studio test suite: **731 tests** (+8), coverage **90.5%**, race-clean. Frontend build clean (5 chunks under cap, 2,115 modules).

## What's Done (iter 1050+)

- **Live cost preview in chat input footer** -- closes the gap left by iter 1040+ strict budget enforcement. With a $5 budget enforced, users can now see "this message ≈$0.0007 input" BEFORE clicking Send, so they can avoid wasting a hard-stop budget on a typo. The chat input footer already showed chars + tokens (iter 690+); now it also shows a green "≈$X.XXXX input" chip computed live as the user types. **Lower-bound semantics**: the chip shows the cost of the user's message ALONE — the actual billed cost adds history tokens reprocessed each turn (provider re-sends the full context every API call). Tooltip explains this so users understand why the figure is a floor, not a ceiling. Backend: new `(*Studio).GetModelPricing(provider, model) ModelPricing` Wails method (thin wrapper around the existing `LookupPricing`). All-zero pricing (Ollama, unknown model) is returned for those cases; frontend treats all-zero as "no preview" and hides the chip — matches iter 290+ "don't display $0" convention. New `ModelPricing` class added to `frontend/wailsjs/go/models.ts` (hand-patched, gitignored). Frontend: `inputRateUSDPerMTok` state in ChatPanel, refetched on `(provider, model)` change via cancelled-flag-guarded effect (so a rapid model switch doesn't race a stale fetch). The footer's char-count IIFE computes `(tokens / 1_000_000) * inputRate` and renders the cost only when rate > 0. New `.chat-cost-preview` CSS (subtle green to suggest "money" without competing with the main token count, `cursor: help` to hint at the tooltip). Tests (4 new in `get_model_pricing_test.go`): known model returns full pricing struct; Ollama returns all-zero; unknown model returns all-zero; longest-prefix match works through the binding (glm-5.1-experimental → glm-5.1 rates). Studio test suite: **723 tests** (+4), coverage **90.5%**. Frontend build clean (5 chunks under cap, 2,115 modules).

## What's Done (iter 1040+)

- **Strict budget enforcement on SendMessage** -- closes the gap that iter 400+ per-project budget only WARNED via toasts; users who walked away during long agent runs could still blow far past the cap. Now there's an opt-in HARD STOP: when `EnforceBudget` is on AND `BudgetUSD > 0` AND cumulative cost across all sessions has reached the budget, `SendMessage` refuses with a chat:error explaining the situation; the user must raise the cap, disable enforcement, or reset usage to continue. Pre-existing budget warning toasts (iter 610+) still fire at 80%/100% so users see the cap coming. Backend: new `EnforceBudget bool` field on `ProjectConfig` (yaml `enforce_budget`, omitempty so old configs migrate seamlessly), threaded through `Project`/`ProjectInfo`/`NewProject`/`ToConfig` so the flag round-trips through restarts. New `(*Studio).SetProjectEnforceBudget(id, enforce)` Wails method. **Performance design**: a naive check would call `ProjectUsageStats` on every SendMessage (O(N sessions) disk walk per send). Instead, `Project.cachedTotalCostUSD` + `costSeeded` are seeded lazily from `ProjectUsageStats` on first need (the O(N) hit happens at most once per app run), then bumped O(1) on every `chat:complete` event with the per-turn `EstimateCost`. Pre-flight check is O(1). The cache is guarded by a dedicated `costMu sync.Mutex` (not `mu`) so the pre-flight doesn't contend with the long-running RW locks. Helpers `Project.totalCostUSD()` (lazy-seed-then-read) and `bumpTotalCostUSD(delta)` (lazy-seed-then-add) encapsulate the logic. **Semantics**: turning enforcement on does NOT retroactively stop an already-active turn — only future SendMessage calls. Clearing budget to 0 implicitly disables enforcement on save (the frontend forces both off together because "enforced but unbounded" is nonsensical). Setting BudgetUSD=0 + EnforceBudget=true is treated as "no budget" (defensive: catches the case where a user toggles strict mode before setting a budget). Frontend: `ProjectInfo` interface adds `enforceBudget?: boolean`. Budget editor modal gets a new checkbox toggle "Strict mode" with explainer hint; disabled state when budget input is empty/0. The save handler calls both `SetProjectBudget` and `SetProjectEnforceBudget`. Tests (7 in `budget_enforce_test.go`): round-trip persistence through ToConfig/NewProject; unknown-project rejection; SendMessage refuses + emits chat:error mentioning "Budget reached" when over cap (and verifies mockClient.callCount stays 0 — no LLM call made); SendMessage proceeds when under budget; enforcement-off lets through even at $999.99/$1.00; budget=0 + enforce=true is treated as no-budget (catches the misconfiguration); `bumpTotalCostUSD` accumulates across turns and ignores zero/negative deltas. Studio test suite: **719 tests** (+7), coverage **90.5%**, race-clean. Frontend build clean (5 chunks under cap).

## What's Done (iter 1030+)

- **Live bash stdout streaming to the tool pill** -- transforms the UX of waiting on long-running tool calls. Before this iteration, running `make test` or any 5-minute build showed only a spinner — users had to either pre-expand the tool card (which was empty) or just stare at the elapsed-time chip (iter 1000+) counting upward, with no idea what was actually happening. Now partial stdout streams to the frontend in ~100ms chunks (engine's `StreamingFlushInterval`) and renders inline as a fixed-height live preview below the tool header, auto-tailing the last 12 lines so the visual size stays stable on long outputs. The engine already had the plumbing — `internal/engine/tools/streaming.go::ProgressCallback` + bash.go's stdout pipe goroutine that flushes every 100ms — but studio's agent loop wasn't wiring it through. Backend: new `EventChatToolProgress = "chat:tool_progress"` constant + `ChatToolProgressEvent` payload struct ({ProjectID, SessionID, Tool, Text}). `Project.SendMessage`'s tool-call loop now calls `tools.ContextWithProgressCallback(toolCtx, fn)` before `safeToolExecute` — the callback emits a chat:tool_progress event for each partial chunk. **Important filter:** progress callbacks come in two flavors — `progress=0` carries actual stdout text, `progress=-1` carries a "running, N KB output so far" counter. Studio only forwards the `progress=0` path; the elapsed-time chip already conveys the "still running" signal, and emitting counter events would double the event bus traffic for zero user value. Empty text steps are also dropped to defend against degenerate callback patterns. Frontend: `ChatMessage` interface extended with optional `streamingOutput?: string`; new `addToolProgress(chatKey, tool, text)` action finds the latest pending tool message by name and appends to its `streamingOutput`, capped at 100 KB with front-trimmed ellipsis so a runaway log can't bloat memory; `addToolResult` clears `streamingOutput` so the rendered tool card switches to the authoritative content view. `useWailsEvents.ts` subscribes to `chat:tool_progress`. ChatPanel renders a new `.tool-streaming` block between header and expanded detail when `isPending && streamingOutput`; uses new `tailLines(s, 12)` helper that trims from the FRONT (oldest lines drop) so the most-recent activity is always visible — important for compiler output where "first 1000 unrelated lines, then the actual error at the bottom" is the common pattern. CSS: `.tool-streaming` (deepest-bg, 3px accent left border matching the pending-rail color, max-height: 160px) + `.tool-streaming-output` (monospace, dimmed, pre-wrap). Tests (2 new in `tool_progress_test.go`): `ToolProgressEmitsEvents` (3-chunk progressTool → 3 chat:tool_progress events with correct ProjectID/SessionID/Tool/Text + still emits chat:tool_result); `ToolProgressIgnoresCounterUpdates` (counter-only callbacks AND empty-step calls produce zero events). Studio test suite: **712 tests** (+2), coverage **90.6%**, race-clean. Frontend build clean (5 chunks under cap, 2,115 modules).

## What's Done (iter 1020+)

- **Honor server's `Retry-After` header in sendWithRetry** -- reliability fix for the coordination gap between the engine's retry loop and studio's outer retry loop. The engine (`internal/engine/client/anthropic.go`) was already parsing `Retry-After` and honoring it for its OWN retries via `HTTPError.RetryAfter`. But when the engine's retries exhausted and the error bubbled up to studio's `sendWithRetry`, studio used a hardcoded exponential backoff (2s → 4s → 8s) regardless of what the server said. A provider returning `Retry-After: 60` (cool-down) would still get hit with our 2s backoff, likely 429 again, and burn the 3-attempt budget in 14 seconds — then surface as a generic "failed after 3 attempts" error. Now studio respects the hint. New helper `client.RetryAfterFromError(err) time.Duration` in `internal/engine/client/errors.go` uses `errors.As` to extract `HTTPError.RetryAfter` from anywhere in the error chain (so callers can wrap the engine error and still extract the hint). `sendWithRetry` calls this on each error and uses `max(exponential, hint)` as the next delay. Capped at `RetryAfterMaxDelay = 30s` so a hostile/misbehaving provider sending `Retry-After: 3600` doesn't park the UI for an hour — past the cap, a clean failure the user can act on (switch providers, /clear, come back later) is better UX than a stuck "retrying" banner. The notify callback also reports the actual delay used (cap or hint or backoff), so the frontend's retry banner countdown shows accurate time. Tests (7 new in `retry_after_test.go`): `HonorsServerRetryAfter` (50ms hint > 5ms backoff → used); `CapsRetryAfter` (1h hint → capped at 30s; verified via notify-reported delay with timed context cancel so test exits in 10ms instead of 30s); `RetryAfterShorterThanBackoff` (1ms hint < 50ms backoff → backoff kept); `NoHintUsesBackoff` (plain error → exponential preserved, 2nd notify is 2x); `RetryAfterFromError_HTTPError`, `_NoHint`, `_WrappedError` (extractor handles nil, untyped, plain HTTPError without hint, and wrapped HTTPError correctly via `errors.As`). Studio test suite: **710 tests** (+7), coverage **90.6%**, race-clean.

## What's Done (iter 1010+)

- **Scroll position persistence per session** -- daily-use UX win for users with long chat histories. Pre-iter-1010, switching from session A → B → A reset session A's scroll back to bottom, losing the user's reading position 3000px up. Now each session remembers its scrollTop and restores it on return; if the user was within 80px of the bottom (the "at bottom" threshold reused from auto-scroll resume), restoring instead snaps to bottom so new content streamed in during the absence is visible. Frontend-only. chatStore: new `scrollPositions: Record<string, number>` field (sentinel `-1` = "user was at bottom"); new `setScrollPosition(chatKey, pos)` action; cleanup wired into `clearChat`/`dropSession`/`dropProject` so deleted/cleared sessions don't leak entries. ChatPanel: position is saved LIVE in `handleScroll` (already rAF-throttled) rather than on session-switch cleanup — by the time React fires the cleanup of a chatKey-keyed useEffect, the DOM has already committed the NEW chatKey's render and the OLD scrollTop is meaningless. **Critical timing bug I caught during my own audit**: the first draft saved scroll in `useEffect` cleanup which doesn't work for the reason above; rewrote to save live in handleScroll. The load-history effect's new `applyScroll` helper restores: clamps the saved value against the current `scrollHeight - clientHeight` so a saved position past the new bottom (e.g. after `/clear`) snaps to bottom cleanly; sets `userScrolledUpRef.current = (distanceFromBottom > AT_BOTTOM_PX)` so auto-scroll-on-new-content doesn't yank the user back down past their restored reading position. Build clean (5 chunks, 2,115 modules). Studio test suite unchanged: **703 tests**, coverage **90.6%**.

## What's Done (iter 1000+)

- **Live elapsed-time chip on running tool pills** -- closes the "is this tool still running or hung?" gap. Pre-iter-1000, a pending tool card showed only the Loader2 spinner — a tool stuck for 5 minutes looked identical to one stuck for 5 seconds. Now a small monospace chip rendered next to the spinner shows live elapsed time, updating every second. Color escalates with duration so hung tools become visually obvious: muted gray up to 30s (normal), amber 30s-2m (slower than expected), red ≥ 2m (likely stuck). Pure frontend feature; no backend change needed because `addToolCall` in `chatStore.ts` already stamps `timestamp: Date.now()` when the `chat:tool_call` event arrives. New `ToolElapsedChip({startedAt, isPending})` functional component declared at module scope below `formatElapsed`: useState + useEffect with `setInterval(1s)` ticker; effect cleans up the interval on unmount AND when isPending flips false; early-return-after-hooks pattern preserves Rules-of-Hooks compliance. Tooltip on the chip shows `Running since HH:MM:SS` so power users can correlate with other logs. Audit confirmed safety in three scenarios: (1) GetHistory returns text-only turns, so history-loaded sessions can't render stale tool pills; (2) GetRecoveryEvents surfaces a banner-summary only, never as chatStore messages; (3) if backend dies mid-tool, the message stays pending — chip stays red, which is correct UX ("this tool got stuck"). Rendered between Loader2 + ChevronRight in the tool-card header. CSS: new `.tool-elapsed-chip` with `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums` so digits don't jitter, plus `.tool-elapsed-amber` (#d97706) and `.tool-elapsed-red` (#ef4444) modifier classes. Frontend build clean (5 chunks under cap, 2,115 modules). Studio test suite unchanged: **703 tests**, coverage **90.6%**.

## What's Done (iter 990+)

- **Status-bar error indicator** -- surfaces recent backend errors so users notice issues without proactively opening Diagnostics. Closes the discoverability gap on iter 710+ event log: previously a user only saw backend errors if they manually clicked Settings → Run diagnostics → View logs. Now an amber-popping red badge appears in the status bar's right cluster whenever there are error-level entries in the last 5 minutes; click → switches to Settings tab and opens the Logs modal pre-filtered to level=error. The tooltip previews up to 3 recent error messages so a glance answers "what's broken?" without even opening the modal. Backend (additions to `internal/studio/event_log.go`): `(*Studio).RecentErrorCount(windowMs int64) int` walks `Snapshot()` newest-first and sums `Count` (honors iter 720+ dedup so a render-loop bug surfaces its real occurrence count, not 1); window=0 means "no time limit". `(*Studio).RecentErrors(limit int) []EventLogEntry` returns up to `limit` most-recent error rows (newest first); limit=0 means "no limit", returns empty slice (not nil) so the frontend gets iterable JSON even on an empty log. Both use early-exit on cutoff for O(window) work in the common case. Frontend (`StatusBar.tsx`): polls `RecentErrorCount(5*60*1000)` every 30s via `setInterval` with cancellation guard. Renders `.status-error-badge` (red rounded pill with AlertOctagon icon + count, capped at "99+"). Click dispatches `gokin:open-logs` with `detail: { level: 'error' }`. App.tsx new `openLogsHandler` switches view to settings, then via `requestAnimationFrame` re-dispatches `gokin:show-logs` so SettingsPage (now mounted) catches it. SettingsPage subscribes to `gokin:show-logs` and calls `openLogs({ level })` to pre-set the level filter — reuses the existing modal/filter state with zero rework. CSS: new `.status-error-badge` block + `statusErrorPop` keyframe (0.6 → 1.15 → 1 scale bounce) so the badge briefly pops the first time it appears, drawing attention without being annoying. Tests (9 new in `event_log_recent_errors_test.go`): empty log returns 0; mixed levels (only error counts); window respected (1s/5s/0 cutoffs); dedup count honored (Count=5 → contributes 5); nil-eventLog lazy-init safe (bare `&Studio{}`); `RecentErrors` newest-first ordering with limit; empty log returns empty non-nil slice; info-only buffer returns empty; nil-receiver safe. Studio test suite: **703 tests** (+9), coverage **90.6%** (+0.1pp). Frontend build clean (5 chunks, all under cap).

## What's Done (iter 985+)

- **Two residual unlocked-read races fixed (post-iter-980 audit)** -- the iter 980+ ToConfig race fix patched the most visible case but a grep audit of every `p.Name`/`p.Provider`/`p.Model` read in `internal/studio/` surfaced 2 more torn-read sites. (1) `emitEvent`'s chat:error / chat:retry log-tee built `fmt.Sprintf("[%s] %s", p.Name, msg)` outside any lock — and emitEvent is called from inside the agent loop, from the retry path, and from tool execution, none of which hold `p.mu`. RenameProject writes `p.Name` under `p.mu.Lock`, so a project rename during an error burst raced the read. Fixed by snapshotting `pName := p.Name` under a brief `p.mu.RLock()`. (2) The pinned-context system-instruction setup in `SendMessage` (line 655) released its RLock before calling `defaultSystemPrompt(p.Directory, p.Name)`, racing the same writer. Fixed by adding `pName := p.Name` to the existing RLock block alongside `c`, `reg`, `pinnedCtx`, `sysPr`. New test `emit_event_race_test.go::TestEmitEvent_NoRaceWithRename` exercises 200 concurrent rename + emit pairs under `-race`. Studio test suite: **694 tests** (+1), coverage **90.5%**, race-clean.

## What's Done (iter 980+)

Four correctness bugs surfaced by a deeper bug hunt. All have regression-guard tests:

- **`ToConfig()` data race → torn YAML on disk** (`project.go`, new test `toconfig_race_test.go`): `ToConfig()` read `p.Provider`/`p.Model`/`p.Name`/etc. without holding `p.mu`. `SetProjectProvider` writes those fields under `p.mu.Lock()`, and `saveConfigAsync` (which runs on EVERY agent turn from `SendMessage`'s goroutine) called `ToConfig()`. The race window: user switches provider → agent turn fires → goroutine reads partial state → YAML on disk shows new Provider but old Model. Manifested as the user report "I switched from GLM to Kimi but after restart it's back on glm-5.1". Fix: `ToConfig()` now takes `p.mu.RLock()` internally. Verified safe because no current caller holds `p.mu` (grep'd). The new test runs 200 concurrent SetProjectProvider + ToConfig pairs under `-race` and also asserts the (provider, model) pair is internally consistent.
- **Archive corruption when files change mid-walk** (`data_archive.go`, new test `data_archive_bug_test.go`): `writeConfigArchive` wrote the tar header BEFORE opening the file. If `os.Open` failed (permission revoked, file deleted between WalkDir and Open, FUSE flakiness), the function returned nil — leaving an orphan header promising N bytes followed by 0 bytes of content. The next entry's bytes were then misinterpreted as this entry's content during restore, or extraction failed with "unexpected EOF on file". A user backing up a config dir with rotating logs / cache churn / a stale lock file could produce an archive that LOOKED valid but was uncrestorable. Fix: open first, then use `fstat` on the open fd for `hdr.Size` + `hdr.ModTime` so the header always matches the bytes that follow. Files that fail to open are silently skipped (clean). Files that shrink mid-copy (`io.Copy` returned fewer bytes than `hdr.Size`) now return a hard error so the export aborts with a clean message instead of producing a corrupt archive. Two regression tests: `SkipsUnreadableFiles` (chmod 0 → file absent from archive, archive walks cleanly) and `HeaderSizeMatchesContent` (every header's Size matches the bytes that follow).
- **Silent 30-minute deadline** (`project.go`, new test `agent_timeout_test.go`): the agent's per-turn 30-minute ceiling fired silently — the outer `for turns < 50` loop broke on `ctx.Err() != nil`, then `chat:complete` emitted with empty text. Frontend's "Generating…" spinner cleared but no message explained what happened. User reports "agent just stopped doing anything on long refactors". Fix: after the outer loop, if `ctx.Err() == context.DeadlineExceeded`, emit `chat:error` with a clear explanation: "Agent stopped after the 30-minute per-turn limit. Long tasks can be broken into smaller steps, or you can re-send to continue from here." `context.Canceled` (user clicked Stop) intentionally falls through without a synthetic banner — the user just initiated that stop, they don't need a message explaining their own action. New test injects `p.testTurnTimeout = 1*time.Nanosecond` to force DeadlineExceeded immediately and asserts the error event fires with text mentioning "30-minute" AND chat:complete still fires so the spinner clears.
- **Messenger race on target lookup** (`messenger.go`): `StudioMessenger.SendMessage` matched target project by name with `len(p.Name)` and `p.Name[:len(toRole)]` — both reads outside any lock. `s.mu.RLock` held only the map shape; the *field bytes* inside each `*Project` are governed by `p.mu`. RenameProject writes `p.Name` under `p.mu.Lock`. Without holding `p.mu`, the prefix match could see a half-updated string and route the ask_agent query to the wrong target. Fix: capture `pName := p.Name` under `p.mu.RLock()` inside the loop body.

## What's Done (iter 970+)

- **Panic barriers via `safeGoFn` + `recoverPanic`** -- new helper file `internal/studio/safego.go` with two primitives: `safeGoFn(label, logFn, fn)` runs fn in a goroutine with a defer/recover barrier; `recoverPanic(label, logFn)` is the standalone version for goroutines that own their own lifecycle (e.g. PTY read loops holding a closure). Both write to stderr (with full stack trace) AND, when `logFn != nil`, append to the event log (visible in Settings → Diagnostics → View Logs). Critical for users who launch the app from a desktop launcher rather than a terminal — stderr is invisible to them. `summarizeStack(stack, maxFrames)` truncates the stack to fit inside the EventLog 2 KB message cap (iter 870+). Wired into 6 previously-bare goroutines: `Studio.Startup` auto-cleanup + auto-backup (replaces the inline `defer/recover { fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, ...) }` boilerplate), `SendMessage`'s `wg.Go`, `Dispatch`'s `wg.Go`, `Project.SendMessage`'s `saveConfigAsync` launcher (most-reachable goroutine in the app — every agent turn), `Terminal` PTY read loop, `StudioMessenger.SendMessage`'s ask-agent goroutine, and `runGit`'s `cmd.Output` goroutine. Pre-iter-970 a panic in any of these would kill the entire Wails app — the user's first symptom was the window disappearing with no warning. Now they get either: an event-log entry (via `LogEvent`) the next time they open Diagnostics, OR a chat:error event if the panic happened mid-turn, never a process exit. Tests: 8 new in `safego_test.go` covering recoverPanic happy path, log forwarding, no-log-when-no-panic, nil-logFn safety, bad-logger-doesn't-recurse, safeGoFn run-and-recover, summarizeStack truncate + short-stack-unchanged + nil/empty input. **Bonus: `runGit` nil-Process.Kill hardening** (`git_status.go`) — when `runGit`'s 3-second timeout fires before `exec.LookPath` populates `cmd.Process` (git missing from PATH on minimal Linux containers), the timeout-branch `cmd.Process.Kill()` was a nil-deref panic. Added explicit `if cmd.Process != nil` guard. Test `TestRunGit_MissingBinaryNoPanic` strips PATH and asserts no panic + empty return. Studio test suite: **688 tests** (+9), coverage **90.5%**.

## What's Done (iter 950+)

- **DeepSeek V4 provider support** -- brought DeepSeek back as a first-class provider (it was removed from the studio layer way back, but engine code stayed). Per DeepSeek's V4 release: legacy `deepseek-chat` / `deepseek-reasoner` are deprecated 2026-07-24 → exposed as auto-migration targets but NOT in the picker; V4 lineup is `deepseek-v4-pro` (flagship with thinking + reasoning_effort) and `deepseek-v4-flash` (fast non-thinking). ## What's Done (iter 930+)

- **Bug fix: CleanupOldData now enforces auto-backup retention** -- closes a real accumulation bug. iter 840+ `pruneOldAutoBackups` only fired when `RunAutoBackupIfDue` actually ran a backup. If a user enabled auto-backup, accumulated 7 daily backups, then **disabled** auto-backup, those 7 files stayed on disk forever — iter 770+ CleanupOldData only walked `<configDir>/history/` and sibling pre-import/pre-restore/staging dirs, not the `backups/` subdir. Disk usage grew with no maintenance path short of manually deleting via iter 850+ UI. Fix: extracted `pruneOldAutoBackupsImpl(dryRun bool)` from iter 840+ original; iter 840+ caller becomes a thin `pruneOldAutoBackups()` 0-arg wrapper. CleanupOldData now adds a 4th sweep that calls `pruneOldAutoBackupsImpl(params.DryRun)` regardless of `Settings.AutoBackupEnabled`. CleanupResult gains `AutoBackupsRemoved int` field; the BytesFreed total includes freed-by-prune bytes; the cleanup log message gets a new "excess auto-backup(s)" segment. Frontend (`SettingsPage.tsx`): cleanup-preview render switched to an IIFE that joins the four count categories into a comma-separated list (more maintainable than the previous separator-soup), and the result toast adds `autoBackupsRemoved` to the total. The Wails JSON shape is additive — old frontend reading the result still works (extra field ignored), new frontend handles both old (number undefined) and new responses. Tests (6 new in `cleanup_autobackup_test.go`): **`TestCleanupOldData_PrunesExcessAutoBackups`** — the regression guard: seed AutoBackupRetention+3 files with descending mtimes, run cleanup, assert AutoBackupsRemoved=3 + oldest 3 deleted + newer 7 survive; `WithinRetention_NotPruned` (at-limit → 0 removed, all preserved); `DryRunCountsAutoBackupsButPreserves` (dry-run counts 5 but files still on disk); `AutoBackupsCountedInTotalLog` (cleanup log mentions "excess auto-backup"); `CleanupPreviewDefaults_IncludesAutoBackups` (preview path surfaces the new count for UI); `pruneOldAutoBackupsImpl_DryRunDoesNotDelete` (helper-level dry-run semantics). Studio test suite: **670 tests** (+6), coverage **90.5%**. Frontend build clean (5 chunks under cap).
