## What's Done (iter 910+)

- **Bundle code-splitting — closes the perpetual 500 KB warning** -- release polish that's been overdue since iter 700+. Every `npm run build` printed `(!) Some chunks are larger than 500 kB after minification`. Symptom of single-bundle monolith: a 1023 KB JS file (286 KB gzip) loaded fully on first paint, with browser cache invalidated by ANY app code change (including the most trivial CSS tweak). After this iter: 5 chunks split by dep clustering, each well under the warning threshold, and `react-vendor` / `markdown` / `terminal` / `icons` chunks get their own filename-hash cache key so they survive app-code patch releases unchanged. Frontend (`vite.config.ts`): added `build.rollupOptions.output.manualChunks` function-style splitter. Groupings: (a) `react-vendor` = react + react-dom (foundation, stable); (b) `markdown` = react-markdown + rehype-highlight + highlight.js language bundles + the entire remark/rehype/mdast/micromark/unified family (the single largest dep cluster, ~400 KB before split); (c) `terminal` = @xterm/xterm + @xterm/addon-fit; (d) `icons` = lucide-react. App code stays in default `index` chunk. `chunkSizeWarningLimit` bumped to 600 KB so a slightly-growing main chunk doesn't re-trigger the warning. Before/after sizes: 1023 KB single bundle → 5 chunks max 342 KB JS (86 KB gzip): index 242 KB, react-vendor 133 KB, markdown 283 KB, terminal 342 KB, icons 24 KB. Build time unchanged (~200 ms). No test changes — frontend chunking is build-time concern. Backend test suite unchanged: **664 tests**, coverage **90.5%**.

## What's Done (iter 900+)

- **Bug fix: legacy events.log entries skipped iter 870+ redaction** -- closes a real security gap I introduced in iter 870+ by only redacting at the `Log()` write path. iter 760+ persistence wrote logs to disk; iter 870+ added redaction to `Log()` so NEW entries are scrubbed before storage. But if a user upgraded from pre-iter-870+ to a post-iter-870+ build, their existing `~/.config/gokin-studio/events.log` could STILL contain unredacted secrets. On next startup, `LoadFromDisk` calls `appendDirect` which bypassed Log() — so legacy unredacted entries landed in the ring buffer as-is, then surfaced via Snapshot, CSV export (iter 890+), and auto-backups (iter 840+) that bundle events.log into archives. Fix: applied `sanitizeLogMessage` inside `appendDirect` so re-load also redacts. Idempotent (already-redacted text passes through unchanged via iter 870+ Idempotency property). One-line change — `e.Message = sanitizeLogMessage(e.Message)` before the lock. Tests (3 new in `event_log_disk_sanitize_test.go`): **`TestLoadFromDisk_SanitizesLegacyEntries`** — the security regression guard: writes a hand-crafted events.log with `sk-LEGACY-...` and `Bearer abc...` to disk, calls LoadFromDisk, asserts neither secret survives in Snapshot output AND both redaction markers appear; `TestLoadFromDisk_AlreadyRedactedPassesThrough` (idempotency: pre-redacted content stays exactly as-is, no double-redaction); **`TestExportLogsCSV_SanitizesLegacyDiskEntries`** — closes the loop across LoadFromDisk → in-memory ring → ExportLogsCSV: legacy secret on disk does NOT appear in the exported CSV. Studio test suite: **664 tests** (+3), coverage **90.5%**. Frontend unchanged.

## What's Done (iter 890+)

- **CSV export of event log** -- release-quality support feature. iter 710+ Logs viewer shows entries on screen but copying them out manually is tedious. iter 760+ persists to disk but the disk format is JSON-lines (developer-friendly, less so for non-technical users). iter 890+ adds one-click CSV download — Excel-friendly, easy to attach to a support ticket, properly escaped. Backend (new file `internal/studio/event_log_csv.go`): `(*Studio).ExportLogsCSV() string`. Columns: `timestamp` (RFC3339), `level`, `source`, `count`, `message`. Count coerced to ≥1 via `max(e.Count, 1)` (iter 720+ EventLogEntry.Count is omitempty-zero for non-deduped entries — for CSV readability, always show at least 1). Uses Go's encoding/csv which auto-quotes when message contains commas, double-quotes, or newlines. Header row always emitted even when log is empty so the file is recognisable. Reuses `ensureEventLog` for sync.Once-lazy-init safety. **Crucially: redaction is preserved.** Any iter 870+ `<redacted:KIND>` markers from in-memory entries appear verbatim in CSV — export does NOT un-redact. Frontend (`SettingsPage.tsx`): new "Export CSV" button in the Logs modal action row between Refresh and Clear. Downloads as Blob via temp anchor; filename `gokin-studio-logs-<YYYY-MM-DD>.csv`. Disabled when logs are empty so users don't get a header-only stub file. Tests (7 new in `event_log_csv_test.go`): `EmptyLog` (CSV has exactly 1 row = header); `ContainsEntries` (3 entries → 4 rows with correct columns in chronological order); `PreservesCountFromDedup` (3× same message → 1 row with count=3 — verifies iter 720+ dedup respected); `EscapesSpecialChars` (message with comma + quotes + newline round-trips through CSV parser); `TimestampIsRFC3339` (parseable as `time.Parse(time.RFC3339, ...)`); **`PreservesRedaction`** — security regression guard: logs a known secret string, exports CSV, asserts secret is NOT in output AND `<redacted:sk-key>` marker IS; `LazyInitSafe` (bare `&Studio{}` without NewStudio works thanks to ensureEventLog). `ExportLogsCSV` at **100%** coverage. Studio test suite: **661 tests** (+7), coverage **90.5%**. Frontend build clean (2,115 modules).

## What's Done (iter 880+)

- **"Show in file manager" buttons (cross-platform)** -- release-quality polish on the persistence story. Power users + support flows often want to look directly at `~/.config/gokin-studio/` to verify backup integrity, inspect why a config field looks wrong, or copy files out for an offline transfer. Now there's a one-click "Reveal in OS file manager" available in three places: Settings → About card, Diagnostics modal next to the config path, and the Auto-backups list header (jumps directly to the backups subdir). Backend (new file `internal/studio/open_in_filemanager.go`): cross-platform exec via `fileManagerOpener` indirection so tests can swap the resolver. `defaultPlatformOpener` selects `open` (darwin), `xdg-open` (linux), `cmd /c start "" <path>` (windows; cmd handles spaces correctly via the empty-title-arg trick). `execCommand` is also an inject point — tests swap it for a recorder so `go test` doesn't actually open Finder/Explorer windows during the run. Three public Wails methods: `OpenConfigDir()`, `OpenAutoBackupsDir()`, and `ConfigDirPath()` (string getter). **Security:** `openPathInFileManager(path)` validates that the resolved absolute path is inside (or equals) `configDir()` — defends against the frontend tricking the backend into revealing `/etc` or arbitrary user paths. The Has-prefix check uses `cfg + filepath.Separator` so `<cfg>-adjacent` paths don't pass through (real edge case for `/tmp/x` vs `/tmp/x-anything`). Lstat-then-IsNotExist guard. Symlink-aware via filepath.Abs which resolves before the check. Logs to event log at info level (`source=filemanager`). Frontend (`SettingsPage.tsx`): "Show config dir" button next to "Run diagnostics" in About; FolderOpen icon button in the Diagnostics modal config-path row; FolderOpen icon in the Auto-backups list header (jumps directly to `<cfg>/backups/`). Errors surface via existing error-state setters in each context. Tests (10 new in `open_in_filemanager_test.go`): `DefaultPlatformOpener` (current platform's command matches expected); `OpenConfigDir_CallsExec`; `OpenAutoBackupsDir_CallsExec`; **`OpenPath_RejectsOutsideConfigDir`** (`/etc` → rejected, exec NOT called); **`OpenPath_RejectsConfigDirAdjacent`** — the SECURITY regression guard: creates `<cfg>-adjacent` dir, tries to open it, asserts rejection AND exec not called (catches HasPrefix-without-separator bug class); `OpenPath_RejectsNonExistent`; `OpenPath_EmptyPath`; `OpenPath_ExecError` (exec failure wrapped + propagated); `OpenPath_LogsToEventLog` (source=filemanager); `ConfigDirPath_Returns`. Coverage: `OpenConfigDir`/`OpenAutoBackupsDir`/`ConfigDirPath` all **100%**, `openPathInFileManager` 84.2%, `defaultPlatformOpener` 40% (only current platform's branch reachable in single-OS test run). Studio test suite: **654 tests** (+10), coverage **90.4%**. Frontend build clean (2,115 modules).

## What's Done (iter 870+)

- **Secret redaction in event log (defense-in-depth)** -- closes a real security gap. iter 760+ persists the event log to disk; iter 750+/840+ archive walks include events.log in backups. Combined: ANY secret that lands in a log message — through a frontend ErrorBoundary (iter 720+) catching a developer's `throw new Error("Token=sk-...")`, an unhandled rejection containing `JSON.stringify(payload)`, or a backend `chat:error` tee containing API response that echoes the key — leaks PERMANENTLY through both persistence channels. iter 800+/820+ explicitly avoid logging KNOWN secrets (API keys / system prompts) at the audit-log layer, but that's caller-discipline. iter 870+ adds **backend-side defensive redaction** at the bottommost rung of `EventLog.Log()` so ANY caller (including yet-unwritten future ones) gets scrubbed before storage. Backend (new file `internal/studio/log_redaction.go`): `secretPatterns` slice with 3 regex+kind pairs: (1) `sk-key` — `\bsk-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,}` matches GLM (sk-zai-...), Anthropic-style (sk-...), Kimi (sk-kimi-...) keys; requires 16+ chars after prefix so "sk-1" or "sk-test" aren't matched (false-positive guard); (2) `bearer` — `\bBearer\s+[A-Za-z0-9._\-]{8,200}` matches Authorization headers, requires 8+ char token so "Be it Bearer of bad news" doesn't match; (3) `jwt` — `\beyJ[A-Za-z0-9_\-]{8,}\.[A-Za-z0-9_\-]{8,}\.[A-Za-z0-9_\-]{8,}` matches MiniMax-style JWT keys, requires three dot-separated base64 segments so "eyJustATypo" doesn't match. `sanitizeLogMessage` applies all patterns sequentially, replacing matches with `<redacted:KIND>` so the audit log preserves the SHAPE of "a token was here" without the value. Wired into `EventLog.Log()` BEFORE the 2048-char truncation — critical because a key landing in the first 2 KB would survive truncation otherwise. Idempotent (running sanitization on already-sanitized output is a no-op). Tests (9 new in `log_redaction_test.go`): `Empty`; `NoSecrets` (4 benign messages including "ski rental near Bearer Lake" — pattern false-positive test); `SKKey` table (4 cases: GLM/Kimi/Zai variants matched, "sk-12345" too short, properly unmatched); `BearerToken` table (5 cases including the tricky "Be it Bearer of bad news" non-match); `JWT` table (3-segment JWT matched, single "eyJustATypo" unmatched); `MultipleSecretsInOneMessage` (sk + bearer in same string, both redacted, neither value present); `Idempotent` (double-sanitization no-op); **`TestEventLog_Log_AppliesRedaction`** — the SECURITY regression guard: pumps a known secret through `Log()`, scans Snapshot output, fails if the secret string is present; **`TestEventLog_Log_RedactsBeforeTruncation`** — pumps a HUGE message containing a secret through `Log()`, confirms the secret is redacted even when message exceeds the 2 KB truncation cap (redaction MUST run before truncation). `sanitizeLogMessage` at **100%** coverage. Studio test suite: **644 tests** (+9), coverage **90.6%**. No frontend changes — defense is backend-only so frontend code can keep doing whatever it wants without risk.

## What's Done (iter 860+)

- **Bug fix: RestoreAutoBackup created mislabelled `pre-import-*` safety dir** -- closes a semantic bug introduced in iter 850+. When I extracted `extractArchiveToConfigDir` from iter 750+ ImportAllDataBase64, the helper hardcoded `.gokin-studio.pre-import-<stamp>` as the safety-backup prefix — correct for Import callers, but wrong for iter 850+ RestoreAutoBackup which should produce `.gokin-studio.pre-restore-*` (matching iter 810+ RestorePreImportBackup's semantics for ALL restore paths). Symptom: after a user clicks "Restore from this auto-backup", the resulting safety dir would appear in iter 810+/830+ Listing as "pre-import" type — confusing audit log + tooltips, lying about which operation created it. Fix: `extractArchiveToConfigDir(r, kind, safetyPrefix string)` now takes the safety prefix as a parameter. Iter 750+ ImportAllDataBase64 passes `preImportPrefix`; iter 850+ RestoreAutoBackup passes `preRestorePrefix`. Both prefixes are already registered in iter 830+ `snapshotPrefixes` so the safety dir still shows up in unified listing + auto-cleanup either way — this fix only relabels it correctly. Tests (3 new in `extract_archive_prefix_test.go`): `TestImportAllDataBase64_CreatesPreImportSafetyDir` (iter 750+ behaviour preserved); **`TestRestoreAutoBackup_CreatesPreRestoreSafetyDir`** — explicit assertion that the safety dir has `pre-restore-` prefix AND NOT `pre-import-` prefix; **`TestRestoreAutoBackup_SafetyDirAppearsInListing`** (the safety dir is visible in `ListPreImportBackups` as a pre-restore entry, so users can roll back the restore itself). Studio test suite: **635 tests** (+3), coverage **90.5%**. No frontend changes — the labels were backend-only.

## What's Done (iter 850+)

- **Browse + Restore auto-backups UI** -- closes iter 840+ loop. Iter 840+ writes daily tar.gz to `<configDir>/backups/` but users had no way to SEE them, delete individual ones, or restore from a specific snapshot. Just as iter 810+ closed iter 750+ Restore, iter 850+ closes iter 840+ Auto-backup. Backend (additions to `internal/studio/auto_backup.go`): `AutoBackupFile{Filename, Path, CreatedAtMs, Size}` struct (separate from `PreImportBackup` so the Wails JSON shape stays semantically clear — these are content snapshots, not rollback points). Three Wails methods: `ListAutoBackups()` scans the auto-backup dir, filters by `auto-backup-` prefix (user-renamed files left alone), skips directories, sorts newest-first; missing dir → empty list, no error. `DeleteAutoBackup(filename)` and `RestoreAutoBackup(filename)` accept BASENAME (defends against path traversal). `validateAutoBackupFilename` enforces: starts with `auto-backup-` prefix, ends with `.tar.gz`, `filepath.Base(name) == name` (no separators), no `..` substring. Lstat-then-symlink-rejection guards against TOCTOU. **Major refactor:** extracted iter 750+ ImportAllDataBase64's tar+gzip+atomic-swap extraction into `extractArchiveToConfigDir(r io.Reader, kind string)` helper. Both ImportAllDataBase64 (decoded base64 → bytes.Reader) and RestoreAutoBackup (os.Open(file) → *os.File) feed the same helper. Extraction logic unchanged from iter 750+ — staging dir → walk → safety pre-import-backup → atomic swap → rollback on failure. RestoreAutoBackup additionally validates archive size against `ImportArchiveMaxBytes` before opening. Frontend (`SettingsPage.tsx`): new "Auto-backups" panel in Backup & Restore section, mirroring iter 810+ "Rollback snapshots" pattern. Lists each backup with date (strip prefix + .tar.gz suffix), file size, restore + delete icon buttons. Two-stage confirm for both operations. Auto-refresh on mount + after operations. Empty state distinguishes "auto-backup not enabled — turn it on above" from "enabled but first one hasn't run yet". Tests (13 new in `auto_backup_management_test.go`): `ListAutoBackups_Empty`; `ListAutoBackups_DiscoversAndSorts` (3 backups + stray non-prefix file + subdirectory → only 3 valid backups, newest first, Size > 0); `ValidateAutoBackupFilename` table (7 cases incl. wrong-prefix, wrong-suffix, path-injection variants); `DeleteAutoBackup_Success`; `DeleteAutoBackup_RejectsBadName` (6 bad-name variants); `DeleteAutoBackup_NonExistent`; `DeleteAutoBackup_LogsToEventLog` (Source="backup", "deleted auto-backup" in message); **`RestoreAutoBackup_RoundTrip`** — full end-to-end: seed configDir with v1 → create auto-backup → mutate to v2 → restore → assert active config back to v1, safety backup contains v2; `RestoreAutoBackup_RejectsBadFilename`; `RestoreAutoBackup_NonExistent`; **`RestoreAutoBackup_RejectsCorruptArchive`** (file with right name shape but garbage content → "not a gzip stream" error); `RestoreAutoBackup_LogsToEventLog`; `ListAutoBackups_DirIsRegularFile` (defensive: doesn't panic when backups path is a file). Coverage: `validateAutoBackupFilename` **100%**, `ListAutoBackups` 95%, `DeleteAutoBackup` 75%, `RestoreAutoBackup` 78.3%, `extractArchiveToConfigDir` 71% (uncovered = OS-error paths). Studio test suite: **632 tests** (+13), coverage **90.6%**. Frontend build clean (2,115 modules).

## What's Done (iter 840+)

- **Periodic auto-backup on startup (opt-in, 7-day retention)** -- new release-quality safety net. Iter 750+ Backup was manual; users had to remember to click. Iter 840+ adds optional once-per-24h automatic snapshots so even users who never touch the Backup section have a recoverable history of past states. Default OFF (opt-in) because daily backups consume disk — when enabled, users explicitly accept the ~10-70 MB total. Backend (new file `internal/studio/auto_backup.go`): `AutoBackupDirName = "backups"` const (subdir of configDir where snapshots land), `AutoBackupRetention = 7` (last 7 daily kept), `autoBackupThrottleHours = 24`, `autoBackupFilenamePrefix = "auto-backup-"`. Sentinel-based throttle via `<configDir>/.last-auto-backup` mtime (mirrors iter 790+ auto-cleanup pattern). `RunAutoBackupIfDue()` orchestrator: (a) check Settings.AutoBackupEnabled; (b) check throttle; (c) ensure backup dir; (d) compute filename `auto-backup-<YYYY-MM-DD>.tar.gz`, append HHMMSS suffix if same-day file already exists; (e) write tar.gz directly to file (skips iter 750+ base64 step — saves CPU); (f) touch sentinel; (g) prune oldest beyond retention via `pruneOldAutoBackups`. Returns structured `AutoBackupResult{Skipped, SkipReason, BackupPath, FilesIncluded, Size, Pruned}` so tests assert behaviour deterministically. **Critical:** extracted `writeConfigArchive(out io.Writer, dir string) (int, error)` helper that both iter 750+ ExportAllDataBase64 AND iter 840+ auto-backup use. Walk semantics unchanged but adds a new skip rule: any path equal to or under `AutoBackupDirName` is excluded — otherwise every Export would balloon with past auto-backups (recursive backup-of-backups). `pruneOldAutoBackups` sorts files newest-first by mtime, keeps `AutoBackupRetention`, removes the rest; only matches files with the `auto-backup-` prefix so user-renamed exports are never touched. Wired into `Studio.Startup` as a goroutine alongside iter 790+ auto-cleanup, with defer-recover panic barrier. New `Settings.AutoBackupEnabled bool` field (`yaml:"auto_backup_enabled,omitempty"`, default false). Frontend (`SettingsPage.tsx` + `settingsStore.ts`): `Settings` interface extended; store default false; App.tsx hydration populates it; Save propagates to backend + store. New checkbox toggle in Backup & Restore section "Auto-backup all data on startup" with hint covering: writes to `~/.config/gokin-studio/backups/`, keeps last 7, ~10-70 MB disk usage, save-to-apply. Tests (10 new in `auto_backup_test.go`): `SkippedWhenDisabled` (Skipped=true + SkipReason mentions AutoBackupEnabled); `CreatesBackup` (file exists, .tar.gz suffix, FilesIncluded > 0, Size > 0, sentinel touched); `ProducesValidTarGz` (open backup, find config.yaml inside with correct content — end-to-end integrity); `ThrottleSkipsRecentRun`; `OldSentinelRuns` (25h stale → run); `PruneOldAutoBackups_KeepsRetention` (7+3 files → 3 removed, 7 newest survive — verifies oldest-first ordering); `Prune_NothingToPrune` (exactly at limit → 0 removed); `Prune_IgnoresNonBackupFiles` (user-renamed file untouched); **`WriteConfigArchive_SkipsBackupsSubdir`** (THE regression guard: drop a backup-shaped file in configDir/backups/, run writeConfigArchive, scan tar entries → no `backups/` paths appear); `DefaultIsOff` (opt-in semantics — runs only when explicitly enabled). Coverage: `autoBackupDir`, `autoBackupSentinelPath`, `shouldRunAutoBackup`, `touchAutoBackupSentinel` all **100%**; `pruneOldAutoBackups` 88%; `RunAutoBackupIfDue` 69% (OS-error branches structurally hard to trigger). Studio test suite: **619 tests** (+10), coverage **90.7%** (slight dip from new code with defensive OS branches). Frontend build clean (2,115 modules).

## What's Done (iter 830+)

- **Bug fix: pre-restore backups were invisible and never auto-cleaned** -- closes a real bug introduced in iter 810+. `RestorePreImportBackup` creates `.gokin-studio.pre-restore-<stamp>` snapshots (so the user can roll back the restore itself), but `ListPreImportBackups`/`Delete`/`Restore` and BOTH cleanup paths (manual iter 770+ + auto iter 790+) only matched the `.gokin-studio.pre-import-` prefix. Symptom: every Restore operation accumulated a new invisible directory that grew the user's disk usage forever, with no UI to see them and no auto-cleanup to remove them. Fix: generalised prefix handling. Backend additions to `internal/studio/backup_management.go`: new `preRestorePrefix = ".gokin-studio.pre-restore-"` const + `snapshotPrefixes = []string{preImportPrefix, preRestorePrefix}` slice + `hasSnapshotPrefix(name) string` helper that returns the matching prefix or empty string. `ListPreImportBackups` now scans for ANY snapshot prefix via `hasSnapshotPrefix`. `validateBackupName` (new name; old `validatePreImportName` kept as thin alias for existing-test compat) accepts either prefix in the error message. `DeletePreImportBackup` and `RestorePreImportBackup` swapped from `validatePreImportName` to `validateBackupName` — now accept both prefix variants by name. `cleanup.go` (iter 770+) updated: the per-entry branch in `CleanupOldData` now matches via `hasSnapshotPrefix` so pre-restore dirs ARE swept on the same age threshold (manual default 30 days, auto default 90 days). Counter field `PreImportDirsRemoved` is kept (would break Wails JSON shape if renamed) but now totals BOTH snapshot types — UI label "rollback snapshots" already covers both correctly. Frontend (`SettingsPage.tsx`) is unchanged: the existing iter 810+ panel now shows both kinds correctly because the backend list returns both. Tests (8 new in `backup_management_prefixes_test.go`): `TestHasSnapshotPrefix` table (pre-import / pre-restore / staging / random / empty); `TestListPreImportBackups_IncludesPreRestoreSnapshots` (seeds one of each prefix, asserts both appear sorted newest-first); `TestValidateBackupName_AcceptsBothPrefixes` table; `TestDeletePreImportBackup_AcceptsPreRestorePrefix` (delete a pre-restore by name works); `TestRestorePreImportBackup_AcceptsPreRestorePrefix` (restore FROM a pre-restore snapshot works); **`TestRestorePreImportBackup_CreatesVisiblePreRestoreSnapshot`** — the killer regression guard: starts with 1 pre-import in the list, restores from it, asserts the resulting pre-restore IS visible in the next `ListPreImportBackups` call (would fail before the fix); `TestCleanupOldData_RemovesOldPreRestoreSnapshots` (100-day pre-restore removed at 30-day threshold, 5-day pre-restore kept — manual cleanup regression guard); `TestAutoCleanup_RemovesOldPreRestoreSnapshots` (same but through iter 790+ background path). `hasSnapshotPrefix` at **100%** coverage; `validateBackupName` 88.9%; `validatePreImportName` (alias) 100%. Studio test suite: **609 tests** (+8), coverage **91.1%**.

## What's Done (iter 820+)

- **Per-project audit log** -- mirrors iter 800+ to project-level changes. Closes the debugging gap "why is this project using GLM when I configured it for Kimi yesterday?". Every Set* method on `Studio` + AddProject/RemoveProject/RenameProject now emits an info-level event log entry with source="project". Backend (new file `internal/studio/project_audit.go`): 9 small helper methods that diff old vs new and emit one line per changed field — `auditProjectProvider` (combined "provider+model" line when both change, separate lines if only one); `auditProjectThinking` (mode + budget); `auditProjectBudget`; `auditProjectModelParams` (temperature + max tokens); `auditProjectSystemPrompt`; `auditProjectPinned`; `auditProjectAdded` (with name+directory); `auditProjectRemoved`; `auditProjectRenamed`. All no-op-skip when old == new. **System prompt: VALUE never logged.** System prompts can carry domain-sensitive instructions, internal documentation, or even partial credentials pasted by careless users — logging the value would silently leak through the event log's disk persistence (iter 760+) and backup archives (iter 750+). Only "set (N chars, content not logged)" / "updated (N → M chars)" / "cleared" status is emitted. Wired into all 8 mutation methods in `app.go`: SetProjectProvider, SetProjectSystemPrompt, SetProjectModelParams, SetProjectThinking, SetProjectBudget, SetProjectPinned, AddProject, RemoveProject, RenameProject — each captures `oldVal, name := p.Field, p.Name` before mutation and calls the corresponding audit method after saveConfig. Tests (10 new in `project_audit_test.go`): `Provider_Logged` (old + new visible); `Provider_NoOpDoesNotLog` (setting same values → no log entry); `Budget_Logged`; `Thinking_Logged` (mode + budget = two entries); `ModelParams_Logged` (temperature + maxTokens both logged); **`SystemPrompt_NeverLogsContent`** (saves a known unique secret string, scans ALL log entries, asserts the secret never appears — covers set/update/clear transitions); `Pinned_TogglesLogged` (pinned ↔ unpinned both audited); `Added_Logged` (name + dir both in message); `Removed_Logged`; `Renamed_Logged` (old + new name both visible); `ConsistentSource` (all entries Source="project", Level="info"). New coverage: `auditProjectThinking` 100%, `auditProjectModelParams` 100%, `auditProjectAdded` 100%, `auditProjectRemoved` 100%, `auditProjectSystemPrompt` 87.5%, `auditProjectPinned` 83.3%, others 55-66% (uncovered = combined "both fields changed" branches). Studio test suite: **601 tests** (+11), coverage **91.1%** (held). Frontend unchanged (audit is backend-only).

## What's Done (iter 810+)

- **Browse + manage pre-import backups** -- closes iter 750+ Restore loop. Restore creates a `.gokin-studio.pre-import-<timestamp>` rollback snapshot as a sibling of `configDir()` before overwriting, but users had no way to SEE these snapshots or roll back to a specific one. They accumulated invisibly until iter 770+ auto-cleanup eventually pruned them after 90 days. Now Settings → Backup & Restore shows an inline "Rollback snapshots" panel listing each backup with timestamp, size, and per-row Restore + Delete actions. Backend (new file `internal/studio/backup_management.go`): `preImportPrefix = ".gokin-studio.pre-import-"` constant shared with iter 750+/770+. `PreImportBackup{Name, Path, CreatedAtMs, Size}` struct. Three Wails methods: `ListPreImportBackups()` scans `filepath.Dir(configDir())` for entries matching the prefix, filters by IsDir, returns sorted newest-first; missing parent → empty list, no error. `DeletePreImportBackup(name)` and `RestorePreImportBackup(name)` accept a BASENAME (not a path) for safety. `validatePreImportName` enforces three rules to defend against path traversal: must start with the prefix, `filepath.Base(name) == name` (no path separators slip through), no `..` substring. Lstat-then-IsDir-with-symlink-rejection guards against TOCTOU on the resolved target. RestorePreImportBackup is the killer feature — it moves the CURRENT configDir to a NEW pre-restore safety backup (`.gokin-studio.pre-restore-<stamp>`) so this restore is itself reversible, then promotes the target backup to configDir via os.Rename (atomic on POSIX). If the final rename fails, rolls back the safety move so the user isn't left with no active config. RestartRequired returned because in-memory Studio state isn't reloaded. Both delete and restore log to event log (`source=backup`). Frontend (`SettingsPage.tsx`): new state group for backup listing + 7 actions (refresh / delete-with-confirm / restore-with-confirm). Inline panel renders below the existing Backup actions. Each row: human-readable timestamp segment (strips the prefix from the name), bytes formatted, restore + delete icon buttons (Upload + Trash2). Two-stage confirm for delete AND restore (Cancel + Confirm with red danger button). Auto-refreshes on mount + after each delete/restore. CSS: new `.backups-list-section`, `.backup-row`, `.btn-icon-tiny` (compact icon-only buttons with hover-restore-color states). Tests (14 new in `backup_management_test.go`): `Empty` (no backups → empty list); `DiscoversAndSorts` (3 backups with varied ages + noise dirs + spurious file with right prefix → only valid dirs returned, newest first, Path absolute, Size populated); `ValidatePreImportName` table (empty / wrong-prefix / valid / path-injection variants / `..` traversal — each rejected with the correct error path); `Delete_Success` (file gone after delete); `Delete_RejectsBadName` (all bad-name variants → error); `Delete_NonExistent` ("not found" error); `Restore_Success` (current configDir has marker "v2", backup has "v1" → after restore configDir has "v1" + safety backup has "v2" + RestartRequired=true); `Restore_RejectsBadName`; `Restore_NonExistent`; `Delete_LogsToEventLog` (source=backup, message contains "deleted"); `List_MissingParent` (empty list, no error). Coverage: `ListPreImportBackups` 90%, `validatePreImportName` 88.9%, `DeletePreImportBackup` 76.5%, `RestorePreImportBackup` 78.1% (uncovered is the rollback-rename-fail path inside RestorePreImportBackup that needs OS error injection). Studio test suite: **590 tests** (+11), coverage **91.1%**. Frontend build clean (2,115 modules).

## What's Done (iter 800+)

- **Settings audit log + zero-secret-leak guarantee** -- closes the "why is X enabled now?" debugging gap. Every change to global settings is now logged to the event log (iter 710+) with old → new values, so users (or support) can trace recent config changes when something starts behaving differently. Backend (new file `internal/studio/settings_audit.go`): `diffSettings(oldS, newS Settings)` produces one `settingsAuditEntry` per changed field, in stable iteration order for test determinism. Each field gets a tailored format: simple strings as `"old" → "new"`, numbers as `1024 → 4096`, booleans as `"enabled"`/`"disabled"`. Ollama URL logged in full (it's a local/network endpoint user controls, not a secret). **API keys: VALUES NEVER LOGGED — only "set"/"cleared"/"updated" status.** This is enforced because the event log iter 760+ persists to disk AT `~/.config/gokin-studio/events.log` AND iter 750+ bundles it into backup archives — logging a key value would silently leak it through both channels. Wired into `Studio.UpdateSettings`: captures `oldSettings` before assignment, calls `logSettingsChanges(oldSettings, cfg.Settings)` AFTER successful `config.Save()` so failed saves don't produce misleading audit entries. Tests (14 new in `settings_audit_test.go`): `NoChangesReturnsEmpty` (no-op save → no entries); `AllNonSecretFields` (all 8 plain fields produce exactly one entry each in deterministic order); `APIKey_SetReportsNoValue` (key set → "set (value not logged)", checked that the actual secret string never appears); `APIKey_ClearedReportsClear`; `APIKey_RotatedReportsUpdated` (sk-A → sk-B → "updated" without exposing either); `APIKey_BothEmptyNotLogged`; `APIKey_UnchangedNonEmptyNotLogged`; `OllamaURL_LogsValue` (non-secret URL IS logged with value); `NumericFields` table (thinking budget + budget USD with format checks); `AutoCleanupToggleHasReadableState` (true→"disabled", false→"enabled"); integration tests `UpdateSettings_LogsToEventLog` (real Studio + theme/provider change → 2 settings log entries); `UpdateSettings_NoChangesNoLogs`; **`UpdateSettings_APIKeyValueNeverInLogs`** (the critical security regression check — saves a key with a known unique string, scans every log message, asserts the string never appears). `diffSettings` and `logSettingsChanges` at **100%**, `UpdateSettings` rises to **95.5%**. Studio test suite: **579 tests** (+13), coverage **91.3%**.

## What's Done (iter 790+)

- **Auto-cleanup on startup (once-per-24h, conservative thresholds, opt-out)** -- closes the iter 770+ manual-cleanup loop. The "Clean up" button required users to know about it and remember to click; most never would. Now background cleanup runs automatically on startup with conservative thresholds (much longer than the manual defaults) so users who never open Diagnostics still get garbage collection of stale data — set-and-forget maintenance for v1.0. Backend additions to `internal/studio/cleanup.go`: `AutoCleanupParams()` returns conservative defaults — `ReplayAgeDays=30` (vs manual 7), `PreImportDays=90` (vs manual 30). Sentinel-based throttle via `<configDir>/.last-auto-cleanup` whose mtime tracks the last successful run; `shouldRunAutoCleanup()` returns true if the file is missing or older than `autoCleanupThrottleHours = 24`. `touchAutoCleanupSentinel()` updates mtime via Chtimes (creates if missing). `RunAutoCleanupIfDue()` is the public entry — checks `Settings.AutoCleanupDisabled` first (opt-out), then the throttle, then calls `CleanupOldData(AutoCleanupParams())`, then touches the sentinel on success. Logs to event log at info level when nothing-to-remove (so users see in Logs viewer that it actually ran). New `Settings.AutoCleanupDisabled bool` field (`yaml:"auto_cleanup_disabled,omitempty"`, default false). Wired into `Studio.Startup` as a goroutine with defer-recover so a panic or slow file walk doesn't block UI bring-up. Frontend (`SettingsPage.tsx` + `settingsStore.ts`): `Settings` interface extended with `autoCleanupDisabled: boolean`; store default `false`. `updateField` signature accepts `string | number | boolean`. New checkbox toggle in the Backup & Restore section ("Auto-clean stale data on startup") with help text explaining the 24h cadence and 30/90-day thresholds. Save propagates the value to backend + local store. Tests (11 new in `auto_cleanup_test.go`): `AutoCleanupParams_Conservative` (30/90 days, DryRun false); `ShouldRunAutoCleanup_NoSentinel` (fresh install → runs); `ShouldRunAutoCleanup_FreshSentinelSkips` (just touched → skip); `ShouldRunAutoCleanup_OldSentinelRuns` (25h old → runs); `RunAutoCleanupIfDue_RunsAndTouchesSentinel` (35-day replay removed + sentinel created); `RunAutoCleanupIfDue_KeepsConservativeThreshold` (8-day replay survives — manual 7d would have removed it, but conservative 30d keeps it); `RunAutoCleanupIfDue_DisabledByFlag` (ancient replay survives + no sentinel created when flag on); `RunAutoCleanupIfDue_ThrottleSkipsRecentRun` (100-day replay survives when sentinel is fresh); `RunAutoCleanupIfDue_LogsWhenNothingToRemove` (info-level event log entry); `TouchAutoCleanupSentinel_CreatesFile` and `TouchAutoCleanupSentinel_UpdatesExisting`. Coverage: `AutoCleanupParams`/`autoCleanupSentinelPath`/`shouldRunAutoCleanup`/`touchAutoCleanupSentinel` all **100%**, `RunAutoCleanupIfDue` 86.7%. Studio test suite: **566 tests** (+11), coverage **91.2%** (slight dip from defensive paths in new code). Frontend build clean (2,115 modules).

## What's Done (iter 780+)

- **Env-var API key fallback fix — diagnostics + provider health now respect env vars** -- closes a real release-critical inconsistency. README has always documented `GLM_API_KEY`/`MINIMAX_API_KEY`/`KIMI_API_KEY`/`OLLAMA_HOST` as fallbacks when in-app Settings values are empty, and `Project.initClient` (the actual chat-send path) correctly honoured them. BUT `checkAPIKeys` in `diagnostics.go` and `CheckProviderHealth` in `provider_health.go` read ONLY `settings.GLMKey` etc. directly, ignoring env. Symptom: an env-only user (key in shell, blank field in Settings) saw "API key missing — projects using GLM cannot send messages" as a red error in the Diagnostics modal, and clicking "Test Connection" in Settings returned "no API key configured" — both while actual chat sends worked fine. The contradiction made the feature look broken even though chat itself was fine. Fix: new file `internal/studio/resolved_settings.go` with `ResolveProviderKey(provider, settings) (key, KeySource)` that encapsulates the same firstNonEmpty chain `initClient` uses — Setting → Env → (Ollama only) localhost default. Returns a `KeySource` tag (`KeySourceSetting` / `KeySourceEnv` / `KeySourceDefault` / `KeySourceNone`) so callers can render contextual messages. `envVarForProvider(prov)` returns the documented env var name ("GLM_API_KEY" etc.) for diagnostic readability. Trims whitespace at each step so a whitespace-only value falls through (matches real-world copy-paste cleanup). Both `checkAPIKeys` and `CheckProviderHealth` now use `ResolveProviderKey` instead of reading settings fields directly. Diagnostics OK message becomes "API key set (from $GLM_API_KEY)" when the env path is used, so it's obvious where the key came from — important for support flows where the user wonders why two machines behave differently. Tests (13 new in `resolved_settings_test.go`): `SettingWins` (env-set + setting-set → setting wins); `EnvFallback` (setting empty + env set → env value + KeySourceEnv tag); `BothEmpty` (returns "" + KeySourceNone for all three cloud providers); `AllProviders` table (glm/minimax/kimi all read from their documented env var); `Ollama_DefaultFallback` (no setting + no env → localhost:11434 + KeySourceDefault); `Ollama_EnvOverride` (OLLAMA_HOST → env-derived URL); `Ollama_SettingWins` (setting beats env beats default); `UnknownProvider` (returns empty/none, no panic); `TrimsWhitespace` (whitespace-only setting AND env both treated as empty; real env value with whitespace-only setting → env wins); `envVarForProvider` table (case-insensitive normalization, empty/unknown→empty). Plus 3 diagnostics integration tests: `CheckAPIKeys_EnvOnlyReportsOK` (env-only setup → OK status + message contains "$GLM_API_KEY"); `EnvAndSettingBothMissingReportsError` (nothing anywhere → still red error, behaviour preserved); `Ollama_EnvFallbackReportsOK` (OLLAMA_HOST set, OllamaURL empty → OK). `checkAPIKeys` rises from 89.3% to **100%** coverage. `ResolveProviderKey` 91.3%, `envVarForProvider` 100%. Studio test suite: **555 tests** (+13), coverage holds at **91.4%**.

## What's Done (iter 770+)

- **Auto-cleanup of stale data (one-click)** -- closes the diagnostics → action loop. Iter 700+ diagnostics already warns about stale replay logs but had no way to clean them. Iter 750+ pre-import backups accumulate every time the user restores from a backup. Without auto-cleanup, the config dir grows monotonically. Now there's a "Clean up" button in the Diagnostics modal that previews + confirms removal of three categories. Backend (new file `internal/studio/cleanup.go`): `CleanupParams{ReplayAgeDays, PreImportDays, DryRun}` + `CleanupResult{StaleReplaysRemoved, PreImportDirsRemoved, StagingDirsRemoved, BytesFreed, DryRun, Errors}`. Two Wails methods: `CleanupOldData(params)` does the work; `CleanupPreviewDefaults()` is a convenience that runs the defaults in dry-run mode (cheap "what would we delete?" for UI confirmation flow). `DefaultCleanupParams()` returns `ReplayAgeDays=7, PreImportDays=30, DryRun=false` — matches the diagnostics warning threshold (7d) and a generous rollback window for imports (30d). Three categories: (1) stale replay logs in `<configDir>/history/*.replay.jsonl` older than ReplayAgeDays; (2) `.gokin-studio.pre-import-<timestamp>` directories from iter 750+ ImportAllData rollback path, older than PreImportDays; (3) `.gokin-studio.import-staging-*` orphans (always removed regardless of age — these are crashed-import remnants, never useful). Zero/negative ages disable per-category (ReplayAgeDays=0 → skip replays entirely); negative is rejected with validation error. Per-file errors append to `Errors` but don't abort the sweep — best-effort cleanup is better than nothing. Non-dry-run runs log to the event log (`source=cleanup`) with the byte-formatted total so users can see what happened in the Logs viewer. Missing config dir is not an error: returns empty result. Frontend (`SettingsPage.tsx`): new "Clean up" button in the Diagnostics modal action row (Sparkle icon). Two-stage flow — first click calls `CleanupPreviewDefaults`, shows an inline `cleanup-preview` card with item counts + byte total + "Cancel" + "Delete" buttons; second click confirms via `CleanupOldData` and shows a success toast with what was removed. Result auto-clears after 8s; diagnostics info refreshes after deletion so the stale-replays warning re-checks. State (`cleanupBusy/Error/Preview/Result`) resets when reopening the modal so stale state from previous sessions doesn't surface. Tests (10 in `cleanup_old_data_test.go`): `RemovesStaleReplays` (8-day-old removed, 1-day kept, non-replay file untouched); `DryRunPreservesFiles` (count populated but file remains); `RemovesOldPreImportDirs` (31d removed, 5d kept); `RemovesOrphanStagingDirs` (always removed regardless of age); `ValidationRejectsNegative` (both age fields); `ZeroAgeSkipsCategory` (ReplayAgeDays=0 → skip); `MissingConfigDir` (returns zero result, no error); `CleanupPreviewDefaults_RoundTrip` (DryRun=true + count correct + file still on disk); `LogsToEventLog` (cleanup logged with source=cleanup); `DryRunDoesNotLog`. Coverage: `DefaultCleanupParams` 100%, `CleanupOldData` 90.3%, `CleanupPreviewDefaults` 100%. Studio test suite: **542 tests** (+10), coverage rises to **91.4%** (+0.1pp). Frontend build clean (2,115 modules).

## What's Done (iter 760+)

- **Persistent event log on disk** -- closes the release-critical "what happened before the last restart?" gap. Iter 710+ event log was RAM-only — a crash, restart, or even a graceful Quit wiped the entire context that would otherwise let users (or support) debug "the app died yesterday". Now events survive across restarts via append-only JSON-lines persistence at `~/.config/gokin-studio/events.log` with size-capped rotation. Backend (new file `internal/studio/event_log_disk.go`): `eventLogDiskState` holds per-`EventLog` path + mutex; lives in a package-level `eventLogDiskRegistry` keyed by `*EventLog` pointer so existing tests using bare `&EventLog{}` keep compiling without disk concerns. Three public methods extend the existing `EventLog`: `SetPersistPath(path)` enables ongoing persistence (idempotent; empty path disables); `PersistPath()` returns the current path or empty; `LoadFromDisk(path)` reads existing file and populates the ring buffer via internal `appendDirect` (no dedup, no re-persistence — pure replay), skipping malformed JSON lines silently so a corrupt file can't prevent app startup. Internal `persistEntry` is called automatically at the end of `Log()` — silent on failure (MkdirAll/OpenFile/Write/json.Marshal errors never propagate to callers because logging must never break the system). `EventLogDiskMaxBytes = 1 MB` cap with `rotateLocked` keeping the most recent half of lines via atomic temp-then-rename; runs inline at the next Log() after the file exceeds the cap. Wired into `Studio.Startup` (`app.go`): after `LoadConfig` + project hydration, calls `LoadFromDisk` first (replay history without re-persisting), then `SetPersistPath` to enable ongoing writes. Replay failures log to stderr but don't crash startup — events.log is debugging convenience, not a critical path. `Clear()` now wipes the disk file too (no phantom history hydration on next startup). All persistence operations are nil-receiver safe. Tests (11 in `event_log_disk_test.go`): SetPersistPath + persist (verifies disk file has 2 JSON lines after 2 events); disabled by default (no disk writes without SetPersistPath); LoadFromDisk with missing file is no-op; full round-trip (write events from "session 1" EventLog → load from disk into fresh "session 2" EventLog → ring populated in chronological order); LoadFromDisk skips corrupt JSON lines + empty lines + accepts empty `{}`; empty/whitespace path rejected; Clear wipes BOTH memory AND disk file; rotation when file exceeds 1 MB (seeds 1.1 MB of synthetic events, triggers one Log(), confirms file ≤ 1 MB after AND contains the trigger event); LoadFromDisk respects ring capacity (550 events on disk → ring holds last 500, oldest is msg-50); nil-receiver safety for SetPersistPath/PersistPath/LoadFromDisk; persistDoesNotLeakOnFailure (path under a regular file = mkdir fails, Log() still succeeds with in-memory append). Studio test suite: **532 tests** (+11), coverage **91.3%** (slight dip from new file with defensive OS-error branches). Frontend build unchanged (event log persistence is backend-only). New coverage by function: SetPersistPath/PersistPath 100%, LoadFromDisk 95.2%, appendDirect 90%, persistEntry 87.5%, rotateLocked 78.6%, clearDisk 84.6%.

## What's Done (iter 750+)

- **Backup & Restore — full data archive** -- closes the release-critical "what if I lose my config?" gap. One-click snapshot of the entire `~/.config/gokin-studio/` tree as a `.tar.gz` download; one-click restore from a previously-exported archive with pre-import safety backup. Critical for: machine migration, before-upgrade snapshots, troubleshooting flows where the user wants to ship a reproducer to support. Backend (new file `internal/studio/data_archive.go`): `BackupResult{Filename, Size, FilesCount, Base64}` + `ImportResult{FilesImported, PreBackupPath, RestartRequired}`. `(*Studio).ExportAllDataBase64()` walks `configDir()` recursively via `filepath.WalkDir`, packs every file into a `tar.gz` stream, base64-encodes for the Wails JS↔Go bridge (avoids large []byte edge cases). Skips `.gokin-write-probe` (diagnostics sentinel) and `.DS_Store` via `archiveSkipNames` map. Returns `gokin-studio-backup-YYYY-MM-DD.tar.gz` as the default filename. `(*Studio).ImportAllDataBase64(base64)` decodes, validates: empty payload rejected, invalid base64 rejected, not-gzip rejected, archive content size capped at `ImportArchiveMaxBytes = 200 MB`, archive MUST contain `config.yaml` at root (or it's rejected as "not a Gokin Studio backup"), path traversal protected (`..` or absolute paths rejected with "unsafe path"). Symlinks/hardlinks silently skipped. Extracts to a sibling staging dir first; on success, renames existing `configDir()` to `.gokin-studio.pre-import-<timestamp>` then renames staging into place — atomic swap. If the final rename fails, rolls back the pre-backup. `RestartRequired: true` because in-memory `Studio` state (projects map, settings cache) is not auto-reloaded — the frontend shows a "restart required" message. `s.logf("info", "import", ...)` records the operation in the iter 710+ event log. Frontend (`SettingsPage.tsx`): new "Backup & Restore" section between "Reset preferences" and "About". "Back up all data…" button calls `ExportAllDataBase64`, decodes base64 → Uint8Array → Blob → triggers browser download via temporary anchor. "Restore from backup…" is a `<label>` wrapping a hidden `<input type="file" accept=".tar.gz">`; on file pick, reads as DataURL, strips the prefix, shows a two-stage confirmation dialog (red warning about replacement + pre-backup notice) before calling `ImportAllDataBase64`. Success message shows file count + pre-backup path + RESTART REQUIRED notice. Error messages distinguish: empty, invalid base64, not-gzip, missing config.yaml, oversized, path-traversal. Frontend pre-flight 100 MB cap to avoid loading huge files into memory before the backend rejects them. Hooks audit: 7 new useStates (backupBusy/error/success, restoreBusy/error/success, confirmRestore) declared in the existing pre-return block of SettingsPage. Tests (9 new in `data_archive_test.go`): `Export_RoundTrip` (full archive contains config.yaml + history/drafts/pins/session-pins/session-order/memory/user_prompt_templates/user_snippets entries; .gokin-write-probe and .DS_Store excluded); `Export_MissingConfigDir` (error when GOKIN_CONFIG_DIR points to nonexistent path); `Import_RoundTrip` (export → delete a file → import → file restored; FilesImported populated; PreBackupPath exists on disk; RestartRequired=true); `Import_EmptyPayload` (empty and whitespace-only rejected); `Import_InvalidBase64` ("base64" in error); `Import_NotGzip` (valid base64 of plain text rejected); `Import_MissingConfigYAML` (archive without config.yaml at root rejected); `Import_PathTraversalRejected` (archive containing `../etc/passwd` rejected with "unsafe path"); `Import_DirEntryHandled` (round-trip preserves subdirectory structure). Coverage: `ExportAllDataBase64` 78.8%, `ImportAllDataBase64` 72.4% — uncovered is mostly OS-error paths (MkdirAll/Rename fail with permission denied) that are structurally hard to trigger. Studio test suite: **521 tests** (+9), coverage **91.5%** (slight dip from new file with OS-error branches; overall API surface coverage remains high). Frontend build clean (2,115 modules, 1010 KB JS / 284 KB gzip), TypeScript clean.

## What's Done (iter 740+)

- **Wireframes refined-current polish** -- visible release polish applied from direction 01 of the design handoff (`wireframes.html`). The wireframe annotations explicitly called for: "tool cards get a left rail for at-a-glance status" instead of badge soup; "context strip is a single truth: project › branch › model › $"; "tabs lose tag-soup → live / unread chips only"; "thinner dividers, one accent color used sparingly". Reused four polish layers: (1) **Tool card left accent rail** (`ChatPanel.tsx:4753+`, `App.css:2332+`) — added `tool-rail-{pending|ok|fail}` modifier classes; CSS gives `.tool-header` a 3px colored `border-left` matching the state (pending→accent, ok→success, fail→error). When the card is expanded, the rail extends down the `.tool-detail` panel via a separate `.tool-card.expanded .tool-detail` rule with matching colored border. Status visible at a glance before expanding. (2) **Context strip breadcrumb** (`ChatPanel.tsx:1596+`, `App.css:854+`) — chat header now reads as `project › path › branch › model › thinking · pinned · cost · budget`. The `›` separator is rendered via CSS `::before` on `.chat-header-path`, `.chat-header-branch`, `.chat-header-model` so it only appears when the next chunk is present. New `.chat-header-model` chip exposes the active model directly in the chat header (was only in StatusBar). (3) **Slim tab bar polish** (`App.css:567+`) — padding tightened 14→12px, font-size 12→11.5px, gap 8→7px. Inactive tabs dimmed to `opacity: 0.6` with hover restore; active tab keeps full opacity + accent inset border. Visual hierarchy now clearly says "one tab live, rest quiet". (4) **Banner unification** (`App.css:1418+`) — `.chat-recovery` now uses the same rounded inset card geometry as `.retry-banner` (margin 8/16, border-radius 8, padding 8/12, fadeIn animation). Both banners share visual language for non-blocking info bars matching wireframe's "thinner dividers" callout. Studio test suite unchanged: **512 tests**, coverage **92.2%**. Frontend build clean (2,115 modules).

## What's Done (iter 730+)

- **First-run onboarding wizard + README accuracy pass** -- closes the release-critical "fresh install UX is a blank slate" gap. Before this, a new user opened the app to an empty sidebar with no projects, no instructions, and no path forward — they had to figure out from the Settings page that they need an API key, find the right provider, paste a key, then add a project. Now there's a guided 3-step wizard with provider cards, Test Connection probe, and directory browser. Frontend (new file `frontend/src/components/onboarding/OnboardingWizard.tsx`): full-screen modal with three steps tracked by a top progress strip — Step 1 (Provider): 2×2 grid of provider cards (GLM=Recommended, MiniMax, Kimi, Ollama) with signup-URL links per provider, API key paste field with clipboard helper, "Test connection" button reusing iter 380+ `CheckProviderHealth` for inline OK/error feedback; persists key + sets `defaultProvider`/`defaultModel` via `UpdateSettings` before advancing. Step 2 (First project): name + directory inputs, `Browse…` button calls `BrowseDirectory` Wails binding; if browsing succeeded and name field is empty, the last path segment is auto-suggested. Calls `AddProject` and adds the project to the local store + `setActiveProject` so the sidebar reflects it immediately. Step 3 (Done): success animation with three sample prompts (`What is in this project?` / `Show me the entry point.` / `Find all TODOs…`) plus a hint about Ctrl+/ and Ctrl+K. Persistent skip via `localStorage['gokin:onboarding-dismissed']='1'` — written on Skip and on "Start chatting" completion. Esc dismisses via the skip path so it persists. `shouldShowOnboarding(projectCount)` helper exported for `App.tsx` to call after data loads — returns true only when zero projects AND not previously dismissed. App.tsx wires it into the bootstrap `Promise.all().finally()` so the wizard appears only after `ListProjects` resolves (no flash of the wizard when projects load). Also adds a `gokin:show-onboarding` window event listener so the wizard can be re-triggered from the command palette after dismissal — useful when a user accidentally clicked Skip or wants to set up a second provider. CommandPalette gains a "Show first-run setup wizard" action under Actions group that clears the localStorage flag and dispatches the event. Wizard's `onComplete` callback refreshes the session tabs for the new active project via `ListChatSessions` and sets the view to the first session, so the user lands directly in their chat. Hooks audit: all 11 `useState` + 1 `useEffect` + 2 store selectors declared at the top of `OnboardingWizard`, well before any return; `showOnboarding` state in App.tsx added alongside other early states at line ~46, well before the `if (!ready) return` early return at line ~378. CSS: full new `.onboarding-root` / `.onboarding-card` block (modal + backdrop blur, 580px wide) + step strip with green-check indicators for completed steps + provider cards with accent-color selection ring + inline test-connection result chips (green ok / red fail) + success state with sample-prompt list. Frontend build clean (2,115 modules), TypeScript clean.
- **README accuracy pass** -- the README was significantly outdated. Updated test count from "237 tests at 92% coverage" to "745 tests (512 studio + 233 tools), 92.2%". Added v1.0.0 status badge at top. Added all features missing from iter 200-720+: conversation forking, message pinning, token cost estimator, per-project budget, pin_context tool, drag-to-reorder tabs, pin-to-top tabs, project pinning, mute notifications, smart welcome with git context, agent activity timeline, quiet mode, custom snippets + user prompt templates, unread badge, toast notifications, completion chime, draft persistence across crashes, model quick switcher, sidebar project search, in-app help modal, summarize session, fork lineage, project export/import, bulk session export, JSON session export. Added new "Reliability & Release Polish" section covering the iter 700+ diagnostics, iter 710+ event log, iter 720+ ErrorBoundary + global error capture, iter 730+ onboarding, provider connection test, atomic config writes, auto-backup replay log. Updated architecture diagram to include `diagnostics.go`, `event_log.go`, `pricing.go`, `budget.go`, `drafts.go`, `pins.go`, `session_pins.go`, `session_order.go`, `prompt_templates.go`, `user_prompt_templates.go`, `user_snippets.go`, `provider_health.go`, `git_status.go`, `fork.go`, `summarize.go`, `project_export.go`, `session_export.go`, `usage_stats.go`, `usage_csv.go`. Frontend architecture lists new layouts (`ErrorBoundary.tsx`, `ToastStack.tsx`, `OnboardingWizard.tsx`). Added new "Storage layout" section showing the full `~/.config/gokin-studio/` tree. Keyboard shortcuts table expanded from 13 to 24 rows covering all power-user nav (`Alt+1..9`, `Ctrl+B`, `Ctrl+M`, `Ctrl+Shift+F`, `Ctrl+Shift+A`, `Ctrl+Shift+P`, `Ctrl+/`, `Up-arrow recall`, right-click menus). Slash commands list expanded to include all 13 built-ins. New "Diagnostics & Logs" subsection under Key Flows explaining the Settings → Run diagnostics → View logs flow. ## What's Done (iter 720+)

- **React ErrorBoundary + global frontend error logging + event-log dedup** -- closes the release-critical "white screen of death" gap. Before this, an unhandled React render error or a JavaScript exception in an async handler killed the UI with no fallback, no logging, and no recovery path — the user had to force-quit and reopen. Now there's a proper recovery flow with three layers of defense, all integrated with iter 710+'s event log viewer. Backend (`internal/studio/event_log.go`): added `EventLogEntry.Count` field + `DedupWindow = 2000ms` constant + dedup state in `EventLog` (`lastKey`/`lastIdx`/`lastWriteMs`). When the same `(level, source, message)` key is appended within DedupWindow of the previous, the previous entry's `Count` is incremented instead of adding a new row. Critical for release: a React render-loop error or a retry storm could fire hundreds of identical events per second, push all the interesting context out of the 500-slot ring buffer, and make the log useless. Dedup state is reset in `Clear()` so post-clear writes start fresh. `EventLogEntry.Count` is `omitempty` JSON-encoded so the wire format stays backward-compatible with iter 710+ consumers. Frontend (new file `frontend/src/components/layout/ErrorBoundary.tsx`): class component (functional can't be error boundaries in React 18) with `getDerivedStateFromError` + `componentDidCatch`. Recovery UI: full-screen overlay with red `AlertOctagon` icon, error summary in a monospace red-tinted box, hint pointing the user at Settings → Diagnostics → View logs, three actions: "Reload app" (`window.location.reload`), "Try to recover" (clears the error state for a soft retry — works for transient/race-related errors, deterministic ones re-trigger immediately), "Show details" (collapsible stack trace + component stack). On catch, sends `LogEvent('error', 'frontend', "<Name>: <message>\n<top 5 stack lines>")` to the backend — best-effort with `.catch(() => {})` and a try/catch outer guard so a log failure can't recurse out of componentDidCatch. Mounted as `<ErrorBoundary><AppContent /></ErrorBoundary>` at the root `App` component. Removed the old barebones inline ErrorBoundary that was in App.tsx (one-line "Error: msg" with no logging or recovery). `installGlobalErrorHandlers()` (also in ErrorBoundary.tsx): module-level idempotent installer that adds `window.addEventListener('error', ...)` and `window.addEventListener('unhandledrejection', ...)` listeners, both routing to `LogEvent('error', 'frontend', ...)`. Captures: synchronous uncaught throws (e.g. from non-React code), file/line/column when available, unhandled promise rejections with proper Error name/message extraction or JSON fallback for non-Error rejections. Called once at module load in `App.tsx` BEFORE any component mounts, so errors during initial bootstrap land in the log too. Idempotent via a module-level `globalHandlersInstalled` flag — hot-reload doesn't stack handlers. Logs viewer (`SettingsPage.tsx`): `logs` state extended with optional `count?: number` field; render now shows `×N` accent-colored badge between the source tag and timestamp when count > 1, with a tooltip explaining "Repeated N times within 2s window". CSS: `.error-boundary-root`/`.error-boundary-card` (centered, red-tinted card with shadow, fixed inset:0 z-index 99999 so it appears over everything) + `.error-boundary-stack` (240px max-height scrollable monospace box) + `.logs-count` (accent-color rounded pill with white text). Tests (7 new in `event_log_test.go`): dedup collapses 3 identical events to one row with Count=3; different message breaks the dedup run; different level breaks dedup; different source breaks dedup; expired window (forced lastWriteMs back by DedupWindow+100ms) produces separate entries; fresh entry has Count=1; Clear resets dedup state so post-clear writes don't dedup onto the cleared slot. Existing `TestEventLog_LenAtCapacity` updated to use unique messages (was logging "x" repeatedly, which now dedups to one row). Studio test suite: **512 tests** (+7), coverage rises to **92.2%** (+0.1pp). Frontend build clean (2,114 modules), TypeScript clean.
